Toward the p p -adic Hodge parameters in the potentially crystalline representations of GL n {\rm GL}_{n}
Yiqin He

Abstract
Let p p be a prime number, n n an integer ≥ 2 \geq 2 , and L L a finite extension of 𝐐 p {\mathbf{Q}}_{p} . Let ρ L \rho_{L} be an n n -dimensional (non-critical but not necessary generic) potentially crystalline p p -adic Galois representation of the absolute Galois groups of L L of regular Hodge-Tate weights. By generalizing the previous results and strategy for the crystabelline case of Ding [ 18 ] and the recent work of Breuil-Ding [ 8 ] , we construct an explicit locally analytic representation π 1 ​ ( ρ L ) \pi_{1}(\rho_{L}) , and describe explicitly the information of Hodge filtration of ρ L \rho_{L} it determines. When ρ L \rho_{L} comes from a patched p p -adic automorphic representation, we show that π 1 ​ ( ρ L ) \pi_{1}(\rho_{L}) is a subrepresentation of the GL n ​ ( L ) {\rm GL}_{n}(L) -representation globally associated to ρ L \rho_{L} , under some mild hypothesis.



1
Introduction

This paper aims to generates the discussion for crystabelline (resp., crystalline)
p
-adic Galois representation in
[
18
]
(resp., the recently work in
[
8
]
) to more general potentially crystalline case. For simplicity, let
\rho_{p}:{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
be a de Rham
p
-adic Galois representation, where
{\rm Gal}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}
is the absolute Galois group of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
E
is a finite extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. By the Fontaine’s theory, we can associate an
n
-dimensional Weli-Deligne representation
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})
and thus an irreducible smooth representation
\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(\rho_{p})
of
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
over
E
(via the classical local Langlands correspondence). Assume that
\rho_{p}
has regular Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}=({\mathbf{h}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{n})
. Then the locally algebraic representation
\pi_{{\rm lalg}}(\rho_{p}):=\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(\rho_{p})\otimes_{E}L({\mathbf{h}}-\theta)
is expected to be the locally algebraic subrepresentation of the conjectural locally analytic representation
\pi^{{\rm an}}(\rho_{p})
via the
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence, where
\theta=(0,-1,\cdots,1-n)
and
L({\mathbf{h}}-\theta)
is the algebraic representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
with highest weights
{\mathbf{h}}-\theta
. The representation
\pi_{{\rm lalg}}(\rho_{p})
only encodes the information of the
F
-semi-simplification of
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})
and
{\mathbf{h}}
, which loses the information of Hodge filtration of
\rho_{p}
. A basic problem (and a starting point) in locally analytic
p
-adic local Langlands program is to find the Hodge filtration in the locally
{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
{\rm GL}_{n}({{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p})
.

After the pioneer work of Breuil, Colmez, etc, this question was completely settled for
{\rm GL}_{2}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-case. For a long time, it remains mysterious for general
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
case. Recently, a breakthrough in understanding the
p
-adic Hodge parameters in the
non-critical generic
crystabelline representations (i.e., become crystalline when restrict to the absolute Galois group of a certain abelian extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
) of
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
was made by Yiwen Ding
[
18
]
. More precisely, an explicit locally (
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
)-analytic representation
\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})
was constructed, which determines
\rho_{p}
uniquely and has the following form:

\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})=\left[\pi_{1}({\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p}),{\mathbf{h}})-\pi_{{\rm lalg}}(\rho_{p})^{\oplus(2^{n}-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1)}\right],

where
\pi_{1}({\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p}),{\mathbf{h}})
is certain amalgamated sum of locally analytic principal series and
{\rm soc}_{{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})=\pi_{{\rm lalg}}(\rho_{p})
, so that
\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})
is an non-split extension of
\pi_{{\rm lalg}}(\rho_{p})^{\oplus(2^{n}-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1)}
by
\pi_{1}({\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p}),{\mathbf{h}})
. Moreover, when
\rho_{p}
comes from a
p
-adic automorphic representation, then
\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})
is a subrepresentation of the
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
-representation
\widehat{\pi}(\rho_{p})^{{\rm an}}
globally associated to
\rho_{p}
.

The existence and importance of extra locally algebraic consistents (together with their huge multiplicity) in the cosocle of
\pi_{1}(\rho_{p})
is a new phenomenon. It seems that such phenomenon appears widely for general de Rham
p
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{p}
and may encode some information of Hodge filtration of
\rho_{p}
. In this article, we explore this question for non-critical potentially crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation (
without generic
assumption on
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})
, or equivalently,
\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(\rho_{p})
). For potentially crystalline case, the local-global compatibility results are proved under the framework of the so-called Patched Bernstein eigenvariety in
[
6
]
.

In a forthcoming work
[
26
]
, we will discuss this question for
semistable
case, which gives more observation of
p
-adic Hodge parameters. Indeed, this work “reduces” the general semistable cases to the crystalline case and the so-called Steinberg case (another extreme case in semistable case, i.e., the Weil-Deligne representation
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})
associated to the
\rho_{p}
has maximal monodromy rank). This result, more or less, is the same as the Bernstein-Zelevinsky classification theorem for smooth representations. Some results in
[
26
]
are also suitable for potentially semistable case.

In the sequel, let
\Delta:=\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
be the set of simple roots of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. Fix
S_{0}\subseteq\Delta
, and let
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}\subseteq{\rm GL}_{n}
(for some integers
r_{1},\cdots,r_{s}
such that
r_{1}+\cdots+r_{s}=n
) be the (standard) Levi subgroup associated to
S_{0}
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
S_{0}^{u}
be the subset of
\Delta
such that the associated Levi subgroup satisfies
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm GL}_{r_{u^{-1}(i)}}
. For
i=1,\cdots,s
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, put
t_{0}^{u}=0
and
t_{i}^{u}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}r_{u^{-1}(i)}
(thus
\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}=\{t_{1}^{u},\cdots,t_{s-1}^{u}\}
and
t_{s}^{u}=n
). Let
{\mathbf{B}}
be the Borel subgroup of
{\rm GL}_{n}
of upper triangular matrices, and
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
be the standard parabolic subgroup associated to
S_{0}^{u}
(thus
{\mathbf{B}}\subseteq{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
and
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}\subseteq{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
). For
1\leq i\leq s
, fix a cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{i}
of
{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
. Then for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}\Omega_{u^{-1}(i)}
is a cuspidal Bernstein component of the Levi subgroup
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{E}
be the Robba ring associated to
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
with
E
-coefficient. From now on, for
i=1,\cdots,s
, we fix a
p
-adic differential equation
\Delta_{i}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
of rank
r_{i}
with type
\Omega_{i}
(i.e. a de Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r_{i}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
of constant Hodge-Tate weights
0
such that the associated smooth representation
\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(\Delta_{i})
lies in the cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{i}
). In the sequel, we put
\underline{\Delta}=(\Delta_{i})_{1\leq i\leq s}
(thus the
F
-semi-simplification
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})^{\mathrm{F-ss}}\cong\oplus_{i=1}^{s}{\textbf{r}}(\Delta_{i})
) and assume that
\underline{\Delta}
is regular, i.e.,
\Delta_{i}\neq\Delta_{j}
if
i\neq j
. In this paper, we do not assume the so-called generic assumption:
\Delta_{i}\neq\Delta_{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(p))
for any
i\neq j
, where
{\rm unr}(p)
is the unramified character of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
sending uniformizers to
p
and
\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(p))
is the rank one
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
associated to the character
{\rm unr}(p)
. We say
\rho_{p}
is generic if such generic assumption holds, equivalently,
\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(\rho_{p})
(resp.,
{\textbf{r}}(\rho_{p})
) is a generic smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
(resp., Weil-Deligne representation).

Let
\rho_{p}
be a potentially crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation and assume that the associated potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
of rank
n
has the so-called (non-critical)
\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
(in the language of
[
6
]
) by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
(for each
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
):

\mathcal{F}_{u}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}}

such that we have an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
r_{u^{-1}(i)}
:

\mathbf{I}^{u}_{i}:{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{u^{-1}(i)}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{t^{u}_{i}}),

(1.1)

where
\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{t^{u}_{i}})
is the rank one
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E}
associated to the character
z^{t^{u}_{i}}
which sends
z\in{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
to
z^{t^{u}_{i}}\in E
, and the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}
) are equal to
({{\mathbf{h}}}_{1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{t_{i}^{u}})
(resp.,
({{\mathbf{h}}}_{t^{u}_{i-1}+1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{t^{u}_{i-1}+2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{t_{i}^{u}})
). In particular, if
r_{i}=1
for all
1\leq i\leq s
(i.e.,
S_{0}=\emptyset
), then
{\mathbf{D}}
is crystabelline and
\mathcal{F}_{u}
becomes the classical triangulation. Fix such
\rho_{p}
through this introduction.

Put
\lambda={\mathbf{h}}-\theta
and
G:={\rm GL}_{n}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. When dropping the generic assumption, the constructions of locally analytic representations need more discussion. Thanks for the computation of the extension groups of certain Orlik-Strauch representations in
[
12
]
, which gives a basic framework. Consider the following locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic principal series of
G
:

\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}):=\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{\Delta}^{u})\eta_{S_{0}^{u}}\otimes_{E}L_{S_{0}^{u}}(\lambda)\Big)^{{\rm an}},\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{\Delta}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{s}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\Delta_{u^{-1}(i)}),

where
L_{S_{0}^{u}}(\lambda)
is the unique irreducible algebraic representation of
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
with highest weights
\lambda
and
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
is the parabolic subgroup opposite to
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
and
\eta_{S_{0}^{u}}
is the square root of the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
. We can manage
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{s}
so that the unique generic irreducible consistent
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
in
\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
(the locally algebraic vectors of
\mathrm{PS}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
) becomes its unique cosocle (so that
{\rm soc}_{G}\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{w_{0,s}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})=\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
by the theory of Bernstein-Zelevinsky segments, where
w_{0,s}
is the longest element in
\mathscr{W}_{s}
). In the sequel, we fix such ordering of
\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{s}
, i.e., if
\Delta_{i}\cong\Delta_{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(p))
, then
i=j+1
.

From now on, we put
\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}):=\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}):=\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{w_{0}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
for simplicity. Let
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. Let
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
be “the first two layers” of the socle filtration of

\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. Taking certain ”amalgamated sum” of
\{\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}
leads a locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
which lies in the following short exact sequence (note that
\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}=\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}
is the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}_{s}
generated by simple reflections
s_{1},\cdots,s_{j-1},s_{j+1},\cdots,s_{s}
):

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq s-1\\
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\end{subarray}}C_{t_{j}^{u},u}\rightarrow 0,

(1.2)

where
C_{t_{j}^{u},u}
are explicit (pure) locally analytic representations given by the Orlik-Strauch functor (see
[
31
, The main theorem]
), and the number of such representations is
2^{s}-2
. Throughout the paper, we will use
\bullet-\bullet
to denote an extension of two objects (for example,
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules or
G
-representations etc.), where the first (resp, second) object is the sub (resp., quotient).

We now turn to the Galois side. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})\cong{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{s}
be the center of
{\mathbf{L}}_{S^{u}_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
. Let

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
) be the
E
-vector space of locally analytic
E
-valued characters on
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
(resp.,
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
). Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
be the
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
-parabolic deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
which respect to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. There are several deformation subspaces of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{u}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
which are closely related to the Bernstein eigenvariety. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
) be the subspace of certain parabolic deformations which respect to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, i.e.,
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
belongs to
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
) iff (where we identity elements in
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
with
deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
)

\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(1)}-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(s)},\;

where
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(i)}\cong{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
(resp.,
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(i)}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{u^{-1}(i)}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(z^{t^{u}_{i}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon))
) for some
\psi_{i}\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}^{\times},E)
and
1\leq i\leq s
. For
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, we have a natural map

g^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}).

(1.3)

Moreover, we have a natural map

\kappa_{u}^{?}:{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{{u}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E),

(1.4)

which sends the above
\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(1)}-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(s)}

to the deformation parameters
(\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq s}
. If
\rho_{p}
is generic,
\kappa_{u}^{?}
is surjective and the kernel is easy to describe. Without generic assumption,
\kappa_{u}^{?}
is not necessary a surjection. The following lemma discuss the kernel and the image of
\kappa_{u}^{?}
, and we prove it by using the so-called (parabolic) higher
\mathscr{L}
-invariants and the Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula.

Lemma 1.1
.

(See Lemmas
3.5
and
3.6
) For
?\in\{0,\circ\}
, we have
\ker(\kappa^{?}_{u})=\ker\kappa^{?}_{1}\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and

\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{\circ}_{u})=\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{0}_{u})=\left\{(\psi_{i})\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{u}_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\;|\;\psi_{j}-\psi_{i}\in E\val_{p},\text{if\;}i<j,u^{-1}(j)=u^{-1}(i)+1\right\}.

Put
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\ker\kappa^{\circ}_{1}
. For any subspace
V\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, put
\overline{V}:=V/V\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. In particular, we obtain an isomorphism

\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{\circ}_{u})
.

In particular, if
S_{0}=\emptyset
, the three extension groups
{\rm Ext}_{u}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{u}^{1,0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
coincide. In this case, we drop the subscripts
\{\circ,0\}
.

We then relate such deformation spaces with the auotomorphic side, in terms of
{\rm Ext}^{1}
-groups of locally analytic representations. We have a natural morphism:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{u}_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\big),\;

(1.5)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}^{u}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{\Delta}^{u})\eta_{S_{0}^{u}}\otimes_{E}L_{S_{0}^{u}}(\lambda)\otimes_{E}(1+(\psi\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}})\epsilon)\Big)^{{\rm an}},\;

which leads to a map (push forward via
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
and pullback via a natural map
\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
)

\zeta_{u}:{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{u}_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

Then the composition
\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}(\zeta_{u}\circ\kappa^{\circ}_{u})
gives

\displaystyle\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

(1.6)

Theorem 1.2
.

(1)

(Theorem
4.13
) There exists a map
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
such that
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. The extension of
\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}\otimes_{E}\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
by
\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
gives a locally analytic representation
\pi^{+,\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
, and let
\pi^{\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
be the unique quotient of
\pi^{+,\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
with socle
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. We can also replace
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
with
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.

(2)

(Theorem
4.32
: restrict to crystabelline case) Assume
S_{0}=\emptyset
. Then
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}
are surjective. In this case,
\ker(t_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and
\pi_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
determines
{\mathbf{D}}
and
\dim_{E}\ker(t_{{\mathbf{D}}})=2^{n}-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1
.

(3)

(Theorem
5.6
: local and global compatibility for the patched setting) Let
\Pi_{\infty}
be the patched
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation, where
R_{\infty}
is the patched Galois deformation ring. If
\rho_{p}
comes form a maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}
of
R_{\infty}[1/p]
, then
\pi^{\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{\rho}]
.

Remark 1.3
.

(1)

The surjectivity of
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}
in
(2)
follows from the infinite fern for crystabelline case (without generic assumption), we prove it in Corollary
4.30
, or see
(2)
in Remark
1.5
.

(2)

For non-generic
\rho_{p}
, we have much more extra locally algebraic consistents than generic case, so that the interior structure of
\pi^{\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
still not very clear. We mention that in general each factor
C_{t^{u}_{j},u}
in
(\ref{firstwholeintro})
only has an extension with certain quotient
V^{{\rm sm}}_{t_{j}^{u},u}
of
\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}
inside
\pi^{\circ}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
. There exist many “extensions lines” in
\pi_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
with the following form:

\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})-C_{t^{u}_{j},u}-V^{{\rm sm}}_{j,u}.

They are basic blocks that encodes parameters (i.e., the extension group
