(1.7)

where
{\rm St}^{\infty}_{3}(\lambda)
,
v^{\infty}_{P_{1}}(\lambda)
and
v^{\infty}_{P_{2}}(\lambda)
and
L(\lambda)
be the four locally algebraic generalized Steinberg representation of
{\rm GL}_{3}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
, and we refer to
[
32
, Introduction]
for the same notation of Orlik-Strauch locally analytic representations
C^{2}_{s_{2},1}
,
C_{s_{2},s_{2}}
,
C^{2}_{s_{2},s_{2}s_{1}}
and
C^{2}_{s_{1},1}
,
C_{s_{1},s_{1}}
,
C^{2}_{s_{1},s_{1}s_{2}}
.

We next describe
\mathrm{Im}(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and the information of Hodge filtration of
\rho_{p}
which
\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
determines, by following the new route in the recent work
[
8
]
. Let
L^{\prime}
be a finite Galois extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
such that
{\mathbf{D}}|_{L^{\prime}}
is a crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
of rank
n
, where
\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
is the Robba ring associated to
L^{\prime}
with
E
-coefficient. We consider the Deligne-Fontaine module associated to
{\mathbf{D}}
(by Fontaine’s theory):

{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=(\varphi,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}}))

where
D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})=D_{\mathrm{cr}}^{L^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}})
is a finite free
L^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank
n
,
L^{\prime}_{0}
being the maximal unramified subextension of
L^{\prime}
over
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is potentially crystalline (so de Rham), then
D:=D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})\cong(D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}
is a free
E
-module of rank
n
. The module
D
is equipped with a natural Hodge filtration, which can be expressed by the following complete flag:

{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}(D):\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n}}^{H}(D)\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n-1}}^{\mathcal{F}}(D)\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{1}}}^{H}(D)=D.

For
1\leq i\leq s
, fix a
E
-basis
\{e_{i,j}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{i}}
of
D_{{\rm dR}}(E_{i})
. Then
\{e_{1,j}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{1}},\cdots,\{e_{s,j}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{s}}
(and we rebel them with
e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n}
by using lexicographical ordering) form a
E
-basis of
D
. For
1\leq i\leq n
, there exist
E
-lines
\mathcal{L}_{l}\in E\langle e_{l},e_{l-1},\cdots,e_{1}\rangle
such that

{\rm Fil}^{H}_{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-i}}(D)=\oplus_{l=n-i+1}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{l}.

Let
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
be the subspace of endomorphism of
D
which respect to the filtration
{\rm Fil}^{H}_{\bullet}(D)
. Using the theory of almost de-Rham, the monodromy-operator (induced by the
\nu_{{\rm pdR}}
-operator on the period ring
B_{{\rm pdR}}
) on any deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
induce a natural morphism

\nu:{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)

which has kernel
{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(the subspace of de-Rham deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
).

Denoted by
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
the image of
g^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
in (
1.3
), such subspaces are closely related to the infinite-fern problem (see
(3)
of Remark
1.5
for more argument). Put
\nu^{?}:=\nu|_{{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})}
for
?\in\{\circ,0\}
.

Theorem 1.4
.

(1)

(Proposition (
4.19
) and Proposition
4.21
) There exists a map (which only depends on the choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
):

{t}^{\circ}_{D}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}({\mathbf{D}}[1/t],{\mathbf{D}}[1/t])\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D),

(1.8)

where
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}({\mathbf{D}}[1/t],{\mathbf{D}}[1/t]):=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])\cong{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p}),E)
and
{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
is certain explicit subspace of
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
.

(2)

(Proposition
3.16
) We have a splitting (which only depends on the choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
):

f^{\circ}:{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}({\mathbf{D}}[1/t],{\mathbf{D}}[1/t])\oplus\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}).

(3)

(Proposition
4.25
) The restriction
{t}^{\circ}_{D}|_{\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})}
coincides with the composition
f^{\circ}\circ{t}^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(recall
{t}^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
in
(1)
of Theorem
1.2
) .

(4)

(Theorem
4.28
) Define
\Delta^{\prime}=\bigcup_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}\subseteq\Delta
. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, put
\mathcal{I}_{i}:=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:i\notin S^{u}_{0}\}
. For any
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, there exists a unique integer
1\leq l_{i}(u)\leq s
such that
r_{u^{-1}(1)}+r_{u^{-1}(2)}+\cdots+r_{u^{-1}(l_{i}(u))}=i
. Define

D_{(i)}:=E\langle e_{u^{-1}(i),j}:l_{i}(u)+1\leq i\leq s,1\leq j\leq r_{u^{-1}(i)},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}\rangle\subseteq D.

Let
\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{i}
be the corresponding
E
-line of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D_{(i)}
(i.e., by deleting all the terms
e_{u^{-1}(i),j}
of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
that not belong to
D_{(i)}
). Then
\ker({t}^{\circ}_{D})
determines
\{\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{i}\}_{i\in\Delta^{\prime}}
.

Remark 1.5
.

(1)

Theorem
1.2
gives an explicit reconstruction (and an extension) of the previous map
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. The inductive construction in Theorem
1.2
only defines
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
on
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
.

(2)

Under the fixed basis
e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n}
, we can identity the space
{\rm Hom}_{E}(D,D)
with the
E
-Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
of
{\rm GL}_{n}
. If we choose
g\in{\rm GL}_{n}(E)
such that
g{\mathbf{B}}\in{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}}
gives the “coordinate” of the Hodge complete flag
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}D
in such basis, then
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
is identified with
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})
, where
{\mathfrak{b}}
is the
E
-Lie algebra of
{\mathbf{B}}
. Moreover, Let
{\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0}}
(resp.,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0}}
) be the
E
-Lie algebra of
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}
(resp, unipotent radical of
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
). Put
\tau_{S_{0}}={\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0}}\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0}}
. Then
{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D)
is isomorphic to the following envelope of intersection of Borel
\&
parabolic subalgebras (see Proposition
4.20
, (
4.46
) and Proposition
4.24
)

\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}:=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0}})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})

in
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})
. Therefore,
\dim_{E}\ker(t^{\circ}_{D})=2^{s}-1-\dim_{E}\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
. In particular, if
S_{0}=\emptyset
, then
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}=\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})
and
\dim_{E}\ker(t^{\circ}_{D})=2^{n}-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-1
.

(3)

The map
\nu
(resp.,
\nu^{\circ}
) surjects onto
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Ad}_{g}({\mathfrak{b}})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
) iff
{\mathbf{D}}
is generic, and in general
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{?})
is described by the Lemma
1.1
(see Lemma
3.19
for detail).

(4)

(An example for Theorem
1.4
-
(4)
) Assume
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}\cong\GL_{1}^{\oplus(n-m)}\times{\rm GL}_{m}
. As
E
-vector spaces,
D=D^{1}\oplus\cdots D^{n-m}\oplus D^{n-m+1}
with
\dim_{E}D^{j}=1
for
1\leq j\leq n-m
and
\dim_{E}D^{n-m+1}=m
. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, we have

D_{(i)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D/D^{n-m+1},&n-i<m\\
D,&n-i\geq m\end{array}\right.

(1.9)

(i)

m\leq n-m
. In this case,
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta
. For
n-i\geq m
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\in E\langle e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n-m}\rangle
. If
n-i<m
,
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}
is equal to the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D/D^{n-m+1}
, so that
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}\in E\langle e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n-m}\rangle
.

(ii)

m>n-m
. In this case,
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta\backslash\{n-m+1,\cdots,m-1\}
. For
n-i\geq m
(so that
i\leq n-m
and
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
), we have
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\in E\langle e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n-m}\rangle
. If
i>m-1
(so that
n-i<m
),
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}\in E\langle e_{1},e_{2},\cdots,e_{n-m}\rangle
is equal to the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D/D^{n-m+1}
.

(5)

(Infinite-fern for potentially crystalline cases, see Theorem
3.21
and Theorem
6.5
) The natural map
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is surjective. We also study
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
for
?\in\{\circ,0\}
.

(6)

Assume that
\max_{1\leq i\leq s}r_{i}\leq 2
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, write the
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
with the form
{\mathbf{D}}=[E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(1)}-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(s)}]
(a successive extensions of
\{E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(j)}\}_{1\leq j\leq s}
) satisfies the
E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(j)}[1/t]=\Delta_{u^{-1}(j)}[1/t]
. For each
1\leq i\leq s
, we can associate a locally analytic representation
\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})
that determines
E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}
(via the known cases of
p
-adic local Langlands correspondence). Consider the locally analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm an}}(\underline{\Delta}^{u})\eta_{S_{0}^{u}}\right)^{{\rm an}},\;\pi_{{\rm an}}(\underline{\Delta}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{s}\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(j)}).\;

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
\mathrm{ST}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. We have injections of locally analytic
G
-representations
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. By taking certain amalgamated sum of
\{\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\}_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}
, we obtain a locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}^{+}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
such that
\pi_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{+}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})
. Then we can extend (
1.6
) to

\displaystyle\gamma^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{+}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

(1.10)

It is worth to extend the map in (
1.8
) to (such map is predicted in
[
8
, (6)]
for general de Rham
{\mathbf{D}}
)

t^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{+}_{1}(\underline{\Delta},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\Big)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

and explore its kernel and image (of course
t^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is still not a surjection in general, see Section
6.4
), and then describe the information of Hodge filtration of
\rho_{p}
its determines. Moreover, we have a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

In general, we suspect that
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
do not admits a similar splitting in Theorem
1.4
-
(2)
.

2
Preliminaries

2.1
General notation

Let
L
(resp.
E
) be a finite extension of
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
with
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{E}
) as its ring of integers and
\varpi_{L}
(resp.
\varpi_{E}
) a uniformizer. Suppose
E
is sufficiently large containing all the embeddings of
L
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Put
\Sigma_{L}:=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}\}=\{\sigma:L\hookrightarrow E\}
. Let
\val_{L}
(resp.
\val_{p}
) be the
p
-adic valuation on
\overline{{\mathbf{Q}}}_{p}
normalized by sending uniformizers of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp., the ring
{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}
of integers in
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
) to
1
. Let
d_{L}:=[L:{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}]=|\Sigma_{L}|
and
q_{L}:=p^{f_{L}}=|\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi_{L}|
. Let
{\rm Gal}_{L}:={\rm Gal}(\overline{L}/L)
be the absolute Galois group of
L
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{L}:={\mathbf{B}}_{{\rm rig},L}^{\dagger}
be the Robba ring associated to
L
. Let
A
be an
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-affinoid algebra (for example
A=E
or
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
), and let
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}:=\mathcal{R}_{L}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}A
for the Robba ring associated to
L
with
A
-coefficient. We write
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A})
for the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
of rank one associated to a continuous character
\delta_{A}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
. Let
D
be a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
, we write
D(\delta_{A}):=D\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{A,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}(\delta_{A})
for simplicity. If
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, we omit the symbol “
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
”.

For any algebraic group
\mathbf{H}
over
L
, we write
\mathbf{H}_{L}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbf{H}}\cong{\mathbf{H}}^{d_{L}}
as algebraic groups over
E
. For a Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
over
L
, and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}:={\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{L,\sigma}E
(which is a Lie algebra over
E
). For
J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathfrak{g}}_{J}:=\prod_{\sigma\in J}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{L}:={\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
.

Let
\GL_{n}
be the general linear group over
L
. Let
\Delta:=\Delta_{n}
be the set of simple roots of
\GL_{n}
(with respect to the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}}
of upper triangular matrices), and we identify the set
\Delta
with
\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
such that
i\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
corresponds to the simple root
\alpha_{i}:\;(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in{\mathfrak{t}}\mapsto x_{i}-x_{i+1}
, where
{\mathfrak{t}}
denotes the
L
-Lie algebra of the torus
{\mathbf{T}}
of diagonal matrices. Each
I\subseteq\Delta
gives rise to a (standard) Levi subgroup
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}\subseteq\GL_{n}
such that
{\mathbf{T}}\subseteq{\mathbf{L}}_{I}
and
I
is equal to the set of simple roots of
\mathbf{L}_{I}
. Let
\mathbf{P}_{I}:=\mathbf{L}_{I}{\mathbf{B}}
be the (standard) parabolic subgroup of

\GL_{n}
containing
{\mathbf{B}}
. In particular, we have
\mathbf{P}_{\Delta}=\GL_{n}
,
\mathbf{P}_{\emptyset}=\mathbf{B}
. Let
\overline{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
be the parabolic subgroup opposite to
\mathbf{P}_{I}
. Let
{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}_{I}
) be the unipotent radical of
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}_{I}
). We have Levi decompositions
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}={\mathbf{L}}_{I}{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
,
\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}_{I}={\mathbf{L}}_{I}\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}_{I}
and
\Delta\backslash I
are precisely the simple roots of the unipotent radical
{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
. Let
{\mathbf{Z}}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
) be the center of
\GL_{n}
(resp.,
\mathbf{L}_{I}
). Let
{\mathfrak{g}}
,
{\mathfrak{b}}
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{l}}_{I}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{I}
and
{\mathfrak{z}}_{I}
be the
L
-Lie algebras of
\GL_{n}
,
{\mathbf{B}}
,
\mathbf{P}_{I}
,
\mathbf{L}_{I}
,
{\mathbf{N}}_{I}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}
respectively. We put
G:=\GL_{n}(L)
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
, and
\pi
be an irreducible smooth admissible representation of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
, let
{\rm rec}_{L}(\pi)
be the
m
-dimensional absolutely irreducible
F
-semi-simple Weil-Deligne representation of the Weil group
W_{L}
via the normalized classical local Langlands correspondence (normalized in
[
34
]
). We normalize the reciprocity isomorphism
{\rm rec}_{L}:L^{\times}\rightarrow W_{L}^{\mathrm{ab}}
of local class theory such that the uniformizer
\varpi_{L}
is mapped to a geometric Frobenius morphism, where
W_{L}^{\mathrm{ab}}
is the abelization of the Weil group
W_{L}\subset{\rm Gal}_{L}
.

For a group
A
and
a\in A
, denote by
{\rm unr}(\alpha)
the unramified character of
L^{\times}
sending uniformizers to
\alpha
. If
{\mathbf{k}}:=({\mathbf{k}}_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{L}}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbb{Z}}
, we denote
z^{{\mathbf{k}}}:=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau(z)^{{\mathbf{k}}_{\tau}}
. For a character
\chi
of
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
, denoted by
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}
the character of
L^{\times}
such that
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}|_{\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}}=\chi
and
\chi_{\varpi_{L}}(\varpi_{L})=1
. Let
{{\epsilon}}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\mathbf{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
be the
p
-adic cyclotomic character (i.e., the character defined by the formula
g(\epsilon_{n})=\epsilon_{n}^{{{\epsilon}}(g)}
for any
n\geq 1
and
g\in{\rm Gal}_{L}
). Then we have
{{\epsilon}}\circ{\rm rec}_{L}={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1})\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\tau
by local class theory. Let
A
be an affinoid
E
-algebra. A locally
{\mathbf{Q}}
-analytic character
\delta:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
induces a
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-linear map
L\rightarrow A
,
x\mapsto\frac{d}{dt}\delta(\exp(tx))|_{t=0}
and hence it induces an
E
-linear map
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E=\prod_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}E\rightarrow A
. There exist
{\rm wt}(\delta):=({\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
such that the latter map is given by
(a_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\mapsto\sum_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}a_{\tau}{\rm wt}_{\tau}(\delta)
. We call
{\rm wt}(\delta)
the weight of
\delta
.

Let
\underline{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{\sigma,1},\cdots,\lambda_{\sigma,n})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a weight of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
. For
I\subseteq\Delta
, we call that
\underline{\lambda}
is
I
-dominant (resp., strictly
\underline{I}
-dominant) if
\lambda_{\sigma,i}\geq\lambda_{\sigma,i+1}
(resp.,
\lambda_{\sigma,i}>\lambda_{\sigma,i+1}
) for all
i\in I
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. We denote by
X_{I}^{+}
the set of
I
-dominant integral weights of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{\Sigma_{L}}
. For
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
, there exists a unique irreducible algebraic representation, denoted by
L_{I}(\underline{\lambda})
, of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}
with highest weight
\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}\cap{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
, so that
\overline{L}_{I}(-\underline{\lambda}):=(L_{I}(\underline{\lambda}))^{\vee}
is the irreducible algebraic representation of
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}
with highest weight
-\underline{\lambda}
with respect to
({\mathbf{L}}_{I})_{L}\cap{\overline{{\mathbf{B}}}}_{L}
. If
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{\Delta}^{+}
, let
L(\underline{\lambda}):=L(\underline{\lambda})_{\Delta}
. A
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation of
G
over
E
is the induced action of
G\subset\GL_{n,L}(E)
on an algebraic representation of
\GL_{n,L}
. By abuse of notation we will use the same notation to denote
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations induced from an algebraic representation of
\GL_{n,L}
. Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X_{I}^{+}
be an integral weight, denote by
M_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{L})\otimes_{\text{U}({\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L})}L_{I}(\underline{\lambda})
(resp.
\overline{M}_{I}(\underline{\lambda}):=\text{U}({\mathfrak{g}}_{L})\otimes_{\text{U}(\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L})}L_{I}(-\underline{\lambda})
), the corresponding Verma module with respect to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,L}
(resp.
\overline{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{I,L}
). Let
L(\underline{\lambda})
(resp.
\overline{L}(-\underline{\lambda})
) be the unique simple quotient of
M(\underline{\lambda}):=M_{\emptyset}(\underline{\lambda})
(resp. of
\overline{M}_{\emptyset}(-\underline{\lambda})
).

Denote by
\mathscr{W}_{n}
(
\cong S_{n}
) the Weyl group of
\GL_{n}
, and denote by
s_{i}
the simple reflection corresponding to
i\in\Delta
. For any
I\subset\Delta
, define
\mathscr{W}_{I}
to be the subgroup of
\mathscr{W}_{n}
generated by simple reflections
s_{i}
with
i\in I
(so that
\mathscr{W}_{I}
is the Weyl group of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}
). Let
I,J
be subsets of
\Delta
, recall that
\mathscr{W}_{I}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{n}/\mathscr{W}_{J}
has a canonical set of representatives, which we will denote by
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n}
(resp.,
\mathscr{W}^{I,J}_{n,\max}
), given by taking in each double coset the elements of minimal (resp., maximal) length. Let
w_{0}:=w_{0,n}
be the longest elements in
\mathscr{W}_{n}
. Moreover, the Weyl group of
\GL_{n,L}
is
\mathscr{W}_{n,L}:=\Pi_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}
, where
\mathscr{W}_{n,\sigma}\cong\mathscr{W}_{n}
.

If
V
is a continuous representation of
G
over
E
, we denote by
V^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}
its locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic vectors. If
V
is
locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
G
, we denote by
V^{\mathrm{sm}}
(resp.
V^{\mathrm{lalg}}
) the smooth (resp, locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) subrepresentation of
V
consists of its smooth (locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic) vectors (see
[
22
]
for details). Let
\pi_{P}
be a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations of
P
on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type (resp., smooth representations of
P
over
E
), we denote by

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}:=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{\;locally ${\mathbf{Q}}_{p}$-analytic representations},\;f(pg)=pf(g)\},

(2.1)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}(\mathrm{Ind}_{P}^{G}\pi_{P})^{\infty}=\{f:G\rightarrow\pi_{P}\text{\;smooth},\;f(pg)=pf(g)\}

the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction (resp., the un-normalized smooth parabolic induction) of
G
. It becomes a locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representation (resp., smooth representations) of
G
over
E
on a locally convex
E
-vector space of compact type by endowing the left action of
G
by right translation on functions:
(gf)(g^{\prime})=f(g^{\prime}g)
.

Let
r
be an integer, and let
\Omega_{r}
be a cuspidal Bernstein component of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
. Let
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}
be the (rational) Bernstein centre over
E
(see
[
16
, Section 3.2]
). For a closed point
x\in\Spec{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{r}}
, let
\pi_{x}
be the associated irreducible cuspidal smooth representation over
k(x)
of type
\Omega_{r}
. Then
\pi_{x}
gives rises a
r
-dimensional absolutely irreducible
F
-semi-simple Weil-Deligne representation
{\textbf{r}}_{x}:={\rm rec}_{L}(\pi_{x})
of
W_{L}
over
k(x)
via the normalized classical local Langlands correspondence (see
[
34
]
), a Deligne-Fontaine module
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}
(by Fontaine’s equivalence of categories as in
[
14
, Proposition 4.1]
), and a
p
-adic differential equation
{\Delta_{x}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}
, i.e., a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}
which is de Rham of constant Hodge-Tate weights
0
such that
D_{\mathrm{pst}}(\Delta_{x})
is isomorphic to
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}
by forgetting the Hodge filtration, by Berger’s theory
[
3
, Theorem A]
). Assume that
{\textbf{r}}_{x}
is unramified when restricted to
W_{L^{\prime}}
for some finite Galois extension
L^{\prime}
of
L
. Then the associated (absolutely) irreducible Deligne-Fontaine module has the form
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}=(\varphi_{x},N=0,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),{\mathrm{DF}}_{x})
, where

\varphi_{\pi}:{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}\rightarrow{\mathrm{DF}}_{\pi}
is the Frobenius semi-linear operator on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x}
.

Throughout the paper, we will use
\bullet-\bullet
to denote an extension of two objects (for example,
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules or
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
-representation etc.), where the first (resp, second) object is the sub (resp., quotient).

2.2
Potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}

We give a quick review of the structure of potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
(in particular,
p
-adic Galois representations of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
over
E
), as in
[
6
, Section 2.3]
. Throughout this article, fix
S_{0}\subseteq\Delta
and write
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
for some integers
r_{1},\cdots,r_{s}
such that
n=r_{1}+\cdots+r_{s}
. For
1\leq i\leq s
, fix a cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{i}
of
{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}
. Then
\Omega_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}\Omega_{i}
is a cuspidal Bernstein component of
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}
. In this paper, we further assume that
r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq\cdots\leq r_{s}
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm GL}_{r_{u^{-1}(i)}}
, and let
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}^{u}
be the standard parabolic subgroup of
{\rm GL}_{n}
containing
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
. Put

\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{s}\Omega_{u^{-1}(i)}
, which is a cuspidal Bernstein component of
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
. Let
S_{0}^{u}
be the subset of
\Delta
such that
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}={\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
(and thus
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}^{u}={\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}^{u}}
). For
1\leq i\leq s
, put
t_{0}^{u}=0
and
t_{i}^{u}=\sum_{j=1}^{i}r_{u^{-1}(i)}
(so that
t^{u}_{s}=n
). Then
\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}=\{t_{i}^{u}:i\in\Delta_{s}\}
(recall that
\Delta_{s}=\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
). We also identity
\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
with
\Delta_{s}
via the map (denoted by
J_{u}
)
i\in\Delta_{s}\mapsto t^{u}_{i}\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}
, so that
\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}=J_{u}(\Delta_{s})
.

Let
{\mathbf{D}}
be a potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
n
. Let
L^{\prime}
be a finite Galois extension of
L
such that
{\mathbf{D}}|_{L^{\prime}}:={\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
is a crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
of rank
n
. We consider the Deligne-Fontaine module associated to
{\mathbf{D}}
:

{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=(\varphi,\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})),

where
D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})=D_{\mathrm{cr}}^{L^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}}|_{L^{\prime}})
is a finite free
L^{\prime}_{0}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank
n
, and
L^{\prime}_{0}
is the maximal unramified subextension of
L^{\prime}
(over
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
). The
\varphi
-action on
D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})
is induced from the
\varphi
-action on
B_{\mathrm{cris}}
, and the

\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
-action on
D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})
is the residual action of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. Moreover,
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
admits a filtered structure which is induced by the one on
B_{\mathrm{cris}}
.

Let
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the Weil-Deligne representation associated to
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(by Fontaine’s equivalence of categories, see
[
14
, Proposition 4.1]
). We say that
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
admits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
if
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
admits an increasing filtration
\mathcal{F}
by Weil-Deligne subrepresentations:

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}={\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}},

such that the irreducible smooth cuspidal representation associated to
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
lies in the cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{i}
, for all
1\leqslant i\leqslant s
. By
[
14
, Proposition 4.1]
, the
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
corresponds to an increasing
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(still denoted by
\mathcal{F}
) by Deligne-Fontaine submodules

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}={\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}},

such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is associated to
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
via

[
14
, Proposition 4.1]
. In this paper, we assume the regular assumption:
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\ncong{\rm gr}_{j}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
if
i\neq j
(equivalently,
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\ncong{\rm gr}_{j}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
if
i\neq j
).

Let
{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
be the
p
-adic differential equation over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
associated to
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. The
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
induces an
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}=\{{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\}
on
{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
is the
p
-adic differential equation over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
associated to
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. Consider the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}}[1/t]\cong\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}[1/t]
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]
. By inverting
t
, the filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}
induces an increasing filtration
\mathcal{F}:={\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}[1/t]
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
by
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}\big[\frac{1}{t}\big]
. Therefore, the filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
induces an increasing
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
-parabolic filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
:

\mathcal{F}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}},\;{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}=({\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\mathbf{D}},

by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
.

Since
{\mathbf{D}}
is potentially crystalline, it is de Rham (in this article, we define the Hodge-Tate weights of a de Rham representation as the opposite of the gaps of the filtration on the covariant de Rham functor, so that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{{\epsilon}}
is
1
). Hence we have
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})\cong(D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)}
,  which is a free
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-module of rank
n
. The module
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
is equipped with a natural Hodge filtration. We assume that
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
has distinct Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Denote
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,i})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
. Hence, for each
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, the natural Hodge filtration can be expressed by the following complete flag (recall that
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}}):=({\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)}
):

{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}:\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n}}_{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-1}}_{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,1}}}_{H}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}=D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}.

On the other hand, the
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}

induces an
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
on
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})
by free
L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-submodules
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}}):=({\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\otimes_{L_{0}^{\prime}}L^{\prime})^{\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)}
. For each
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we now fix a basis of
D_{{\rm dR}}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
over
E
. Then the Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H}
(resp.,
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}}
) corresponds to an
E
-point
(g_{2,\tau}{\mathbf{B}}(E))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{B}}_{L}
(resp.,
(g_{1,\tau}{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}(E))_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\in{\rm GL}_{n,L}/{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0},L}
). For each
\tau\in\Sigma_{L}
, there exists thus a unique
w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}\in\mathscr{W}^{S_{0},\emptyset}_{n,\max}
such that

(g_{1,\tau}{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}(E),g_{2,\tau}{\mathbf{B}}(E))\in{\rm GL}_{n}(E)(1,w_{\mathcal{F},\tau})({\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}\times{\mathbf{B}})(E)\subset({\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}\times{\rm GL}_{n}/{\mathbf{B}})(E).

Now by Berger’s equivalence of categories, we see that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
corresponds to the filtered Delingen-Fontaine module
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
equipped with the induced filtration from the Hodge filtration on
D_{{\rm pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})
. In this case, we see that the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}
) are given by

\displaystyle\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(1)},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(2)},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(t_{i})}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}},

(2.2)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(t_{i-1}+1)},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(t_{i-1}+2)},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(t_{i})}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}.\;

We put
w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{j}:=\{{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,(w_{\mathcal{F},\tau}{w}_{0})^{-1}(j)}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
. Using Berger’s equivalence of categories
[
3
, Theorem A]
and comparing the weights (or see
[
6
, (2.4)]
), we have an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
r_{i}
:

\mathbf{I}_{i}:{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{t_{i}}}),

(2.3)

for
i=1,\cdots,s
(thus for
1\leq i\leq s
,
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}
is equal to the
p
-adic differential equation
\Delta_{E_{i}}
associated to the
E_{i}
). In the language of
[
6
, Definition 4.1.6]
, (
2.3
) implies that
{\mathbf{D}}
admits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
. Throughout this paper, for simplicity, we write
{\mathbf{D}}
with the following form

{\mathbf{D}}:=E_{1}-E_{2}-\cdots-E_{s},\;E_{i}:={\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}{\mathbf{D}}.\;

(2.4)

For
1\leq i\leq s
, let
{\mathrm{DF}}_{E_{i}}:=(\varphi_{i},\Gal(L^{\prime}/L),D_{{\rm pcr}}(E_{i}))
be the underlying Deligne-Fontaine module associated to
E_{i}
. In the sequel, we manage
E_{i}
in (
2.4
) in following rules: if
\varphi_{j}=p\varphi_{i}
, then
j=i+1
. Therefore, there exist integers
0=l_{0}<1\leq l_{1}<l_{2}<\cdots<l_{m-1}\leq l_{m}=s
such that
\varphi_{l+1}\neq p\varphi_{l}
iff
l=l_{i}
for some
1\leq i\leq m-1
. Put
I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})=\Delta_{s}\backslash\{l_{1},\cdots,l_{m-1}\}\subseteq\Delta_{s}
, which describes the non-generic relations among
\{E_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq s}
inside in
{\mathbf{D}}
. Set
I_{0}({\mathbf{D}}):=\Delta\backslash J_{u}(\{l_{1},\cdots,l_{m-1}\})\subseteq\Delta
. More precisely, set
n_{j}:=r_{l_{j}+1}=r_{l_{j}+2}=\cdots=r_{l_{j+1}}
for each
0\leq j\leq m-1
, then
{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0}({\mathbf{D}})}={\rm GL}_{l_{1}n_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{2}-l_{1})n_{2}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{m}-l_{m-1})n_{m}}
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we also have an
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
on
{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
by Deligne-Fontaine submodules

\mathcal{F}_{u}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}={\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}},

such that
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathrm{DF}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is associated to
{\rm Fil}_{u^{-1}(i)}^{\mathcal{F}}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(thus the above
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}
equals to
\mathcal{F}_{1}
). Applying the above discussion to

\mathcal{F}_{u}
, we see that
{\mathbf{D}}
admits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, and for
1\leq i\leq s
, we have an injection of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
r_{u^{-1}(i)}
:

\mathbf{I}^{u}_{i}:{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}{{\otimes}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{w_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{h}})_{t^{u}_{i}}}),

(2.5)

with
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}_{u}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\cong\Delta_{E_{u^{-1}(i)}}
. We usually write
\mathcal{F}_{u}
with the following form:

{\mathbf{D}}:=E_{u^{-1}(1)}^{\prime}-E_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-E_{u^{-1}(s)}^{\prime}.\;

(2.6)

We explain the notation
(-)^{\prime}
as follows. For any de-Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
B
, we usually use
B^{\prime}
to mean another
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module which has the same
p
-adic differential equation as
B
(so that
B[1/t]=B^{\prime}[1/t]
, but
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}D_{{\rm dR}}(B^{\prime})
may different from
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}D_{{\rm dR}}(B)
).

Definition 2.1
.

We call that
{\mathbf{D}}
is non-critical if
w_{\mathcal{F}_{u},\sigma}={w}_{0}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Suppose that
{\mathbf{D}}
is non-critical. In the sequel, let
\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}
) be the rigid space over
E
parametrizing continuous characters of
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)
(resp.,
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
). Suppose that
{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{F}}_{i}{\Delta_{\mathbf{D}}}\cong\Delta_{x_{i}}
for some
x_{i}\in\Spec{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{i}}(E)
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, put

\displaystyle\underline{x}^{u}:=(x_{u^{-1}(i)})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}},\underline{\delta}^{u}=(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t^{u}_{i}}})_{1\leq i\leq s}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}},

(2.7)

\displaystyle\underline{x}^{u}_{+}:=(x^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}},\underline{\delta}^{0,u}:=\underline{\delta}^{u}|_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}},\;

where
x_{i}^{\prime}\in\Spec{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{i}}
satisfies
\pi_{x^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}}\cong{\rm unr}(\varpi_{L}^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t^{u}_{i}}})\pi_{x_{u^{-1}(i)}}
for
1\leq i\leq s
. In the language of
[
6
, Definition 4.1.6]
, the parameters of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
in
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}}}
(resp.,
{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}
) is
(\underline{x}^{u},\underline{\delta}^{u})
(resp.,
(\underline{x}^{u}_{+},\underline{\delta}^{0,u})
).

Definition 2.2
.

We say that
{\mathbf{D}}
is generic if
S_{0}=I_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
(equivalently,
I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})=\emptyset
), equivalently,
\Delta_{i}\neq\Delta_{j}({\rm unr}(q_{L}))=\Delta_{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}({\rm unr}(q_{L}))
for any
i\neq j
. Moreover, this is equivalent to say that
\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}({\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
) is a generic smooth representation of
G
(resp., Weil-Deligne representation).

Remark 2.3
.

(Crystabelline case, i.e.,
S_{0}=\emptyset
) The
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration becomes the classical triangulation (note that
s=n
,
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}={\mathbf{T}}
,
{\mathbf{P}}^{u}_{S_{0}}={\mathbf{B}}
and
t_{i}^{u}=i
for all
1\leq i\leq n
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
). In this case,
I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})=I_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
. Let
\{\phi_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
be the
\varphi
-eigenvalues on
D_{\mathrm{pcr}}({\mathbf{D}})
. In precise, there exist
\alpha_{i}\in E^{\times}
and integers
l_{1}<\cdots<l_{m}
such that

\underline{\phi}:=\underline{\phi}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=(\phi_{1},\cdots,\phi_{n})=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1}p,\cdots,\alpha_{1}p^{l_{1}-1},\cdots,\alpha_{m},\alpha_{m}p,\cdots,\alpha_{m}p^{l_{m}-1}),

where the order here satisfies the condition: if
\alpha_{j}=\alpha_{i}p^{l_{i}}
, then
j=i+1
. If
{\mathbf{D}}
is non-critical, then for any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
,
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
:

{\mathbf{D}}:=\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\phi_{u^{-1}(1)}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{1}})-\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\phi_{u^{-1}(2)}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{2}})-\cdots-\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\phi_{u^{-1}(n)}z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{n}}).

(2.8)

Let
\mathcal{T}
be the rigid space over
E
parameterizing continuous character of
T(L)
. Then
\delta_{\underline{\phi},u}:=(\delta_{\underline{\phi},u,i})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\mathcal{T}
with
\delta_{\underline{\phi},u,i}:=\phi_{u^{-1}(i)}{z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}}}
is a parameter of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
.

2.3
Deformations of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

For a locally
L
-analytic group
H
, let
{\rm Hom}(H,E)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}(H,E)
) be the
E
-vector space of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic (resp., locally constant)
E
-valued characters on
H
. For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(H,E)\subseteq{\rm Hom}(H,E)
be the subspace of locally
\sigma
-analytic
E
-valued characters on
H
. In particular, if
H=L^{\times}
, by
[
10
, Section 1.3.1]
,
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
is general by
\val_{L}
and
\{\log_{p,\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
, where
\log_{p}:L^{\times}\rightarrow L
is the
p
-adic logarithm when restricted to
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
. Then
{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}(L^{\times},E)\cong E\val_{L}
,
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)=E\langle\val_{L},\log_{p,\sigma}\rangle
and
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)=E\langle\val_{L},\{\log_{p,\sigma}\}_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\rangle
. In particular, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)=1+d_{L}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}(L^{\times},E)=1
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)=2
. Moreover, a choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
(we may choose
\log_{p}(p)=0
) define a section
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
to the restriction map
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E)
by sending
\log_{p,\sigma}
to its unique extension to
L^{\times}
sending
p
to
\log_{p}(p)
.

We give general notation for parabolic deformations. Let
\mathcal{G}
be an increasing
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathcal{G}}
-parabolic filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
by saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
:

\mathcal{G}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{h}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{D}}.

Put
M_{i}:={\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}
, then
\mathcal{G}
is the parabolic filtration of
{\mathbf{D}}
corresponding to the form

{\mathbf{D}}=M_{1}-M_{2}-\cdots-M_{h}.

Let
{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{G}}
be the standard Levi subgroup of
{\mathbf{P}}_{\mathcal{G}}
. Let
{\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathcal{G}}\cong{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{h}
be the center of
{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathcal{G}}
. Denote by
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}
the deformation functor

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}:{\rm Art}(E):=\{\text{Artinian local $E$-algebra with residue field $E$}\}\longrightarrow\{\text{sets}\}

sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{G}_{A})\}/\sim
, where

(1)

D_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
of rank
n
with
\pi_{A}:D_{A}\otimes_{A}E\cong{\mathbf{D}}
;

(2)

\mathcal{G}_{A}={\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{\bullet}D_{A}
is an increasing filtration of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
on
D_{A}
, such that
{\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{i}D_{A}
are direct summand of
D_{A}
as
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
-modules, and
\pi_{A}({\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{i}D_{A})\cong{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{G}}{\mathbf{D}}=M_{i}
.

Moreover, we can identity
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}(E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2})
with a subspace
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
of
\mathcal{G}
-parabolic deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
with respect to
\mathcal{G}
, i.e.,
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
belongs to
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
iff

\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{M}_{2}-\cdots-\widetilde{M}_{h},\;

where
\widetilde{M}_{i}
is a deformation of
M_{i}
over
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
for
1\leq i\leq s
.

We next recall the contents of
[
6
, Section 4.1.1]
. For any
1\leq i\leq h
, let
\Delta_{M_{i}}
be the differential equation associated to
M_{i}
, which is an
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
h_{i}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, de Rham of constant Hodge-Tate weight
0
. Suppose that we have an injection
M_{i}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Delta_{M_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta_{i})
of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
for some character
\delta_{i}:L^{\times}\rightarrow E^{\times}
(for each
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we usually assume that
0
is the minimal
\sigma
-Hodge-Tate weights of
M_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta^{-1}_{i})
). In
[
6
, Sections 4.1.1 & 4.1.2]
, the authors consider the following functor:

F_{M_{i}}^{0}(\text{resp}.,\;F_{M_{i}}^{\circ}):{\rm Art}(E)\longrightarrow\{\text{sets}\}

sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\delta_{A})\}/\sim
, where

(1)

D_{A}
is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module of rank
r
over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
with
\pi_{A}:D_{A}\otimes_{A}E\cong M_{i}
;

(2)

{\delta}_{A}:L^{\times}\rightarrow A^{\times}
is a continuous character such that
{\delta}_{A}\equiv{\delta}_{i}{\rm\,mod\,}\mathfrak{m}_{A}
;

(3)

there exists an injection (resp.,  isomorphism):
D_{A}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Delta_{M_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A})
(resp.,
D_{A}\cong M_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A})
) of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
.

The map sending the data
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{G}_{A})\}
) to
\{({\rm gr}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{i}D_{A},\pi_{A}|_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}D_{A}}\}
induces a natural morphism
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}}\longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{h}F_{M_{i}}
. For
?\in\{0,\circ\}
, put

F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{?}=F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}\times_{\prod_{i=1}^{h}F_{M_{i}}}\prod_{i=1}^{h}F_{M_{i}}^{?},

i.e.,
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{0}
(
resp
.,
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{\circ}
) sends
A
to the set of isomorphism classes
\{(D_{A},\pi_{A},\mathcal{G}_{A},\delta_{A})\}/\sim
with the above three conditions
(1)-(3)
and

(4)

For
1\leq i\leq h
, there is an injection (resp. isomorphism) of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{A,L}
:

{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{i}D_{A}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\Delta_{M_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i}),\;\text{resp.\;}{\rm gr}^{\mathcal{G}_{A}}_{i}D_{A}\cong M_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{A,L}({\delta}_{A,i}).\;

(2.9)

We have inclusions of subfunctors
F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{\circ}\subseteq F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{0}\subseteq F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}
, and its
E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
-points correspond to inclusions of subspaces

{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

such that
\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{M}_{1}-\widetilde{M}_{2}-\cdots-\widetilde{M}_{h}
belongs to
{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
) if
\widetilde{F}_{i}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{M_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(\delta_{i}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon))
(resp.,
\widetilde{F}_{i}\cong M_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(\delta_{i}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon))
) for some
\psi_{i}\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
. We thus have natural maps

\displaystyle\kappa^{?}_{u}:{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{\mathcal{G}}(L),E),\;\widetilde{D}\mapsto(\psi_{i})_{1\leq i\leq h}.

(2.10)

for
?\in\{\circ,0\}
. In particular, if
\rank(M_{i})=1
for all
1\leq i\leq h
, the functors
\{F_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{G}}^{?}\}_{?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}}
and extensions groups
\{{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\}_{?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}}
coincide, and we drop the symbol
\{\circ,0\}
in all subscripts.

3
Hodge filtration and higher intertwining

Throughout this section, we fix a non-critical potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
with Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}
and keep the notation in Section
2.2
.

3.1
Hodge parameters

We have two short exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow E_{1}^{s-1}\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow E_{s}\rightarrow 0,

(3.1)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow E_{s}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}\rightarrow 0.\;

Denoted by

\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}:E_{1}^{s-1}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}.\;

(3.2)

the composition
E_{1}^{s-1}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\twoheadrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})
. As
{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},E_{s}^{\prime})=0
(in the sequel, the notation
{\rm Hom}(-,-)
usually means
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(-,-)
for simplicity),
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}
is injective.

For any
i\in\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
,
E_{1}^{s-1}
admits a quotient
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\widehat{i}}:=E_{1}^{s-1}/E_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
admits a saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}:\ker((E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow E_{i}^{\prime})
. The Hodge-Tate weights of
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{{\widehat{i}}}
(resp.,
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}
) are
{\mathbf{h}}_{r_{i}+1},\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s}-r_{s}}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{h}}_{r_{s}+1},\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s}-r_{i}}
). Thus
{\mathbf{D}}=[E_{i}^{\prime\prime}-(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\widehat{i}}-E_{s}]=[E_{s}^{\prime}-(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}-E_{i}^{\prime}]
. Note that
r_{i}\leq r_{s}
by our assumption in the beginning of Section
2.2
. An analogue of
[
18
, Proposition 2.2]
for potentially crystalline case is

Proposition 3.1
.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})\leq 2
.

(2)

\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})=2
iff
n\geq 3
, and for
i\in\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
we have a map
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\widehat{i}}\hookrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}
(if
r_{i}=r_{s}
, we have an isomorphism
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\widehat{i}}\cong(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}
). If these hold, the composition (which is not injective)

\alpha_{i}:E_{1}^{s-1}\twoheadrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{{\widehat{i}}}\hookrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}

(3.3)

are pair-wisely linearly independent as elements in
{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})
.

Proof.

The case for
s=2
is trivial since
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(E_{1},E_{1}^{\prime})=1
and we assume
s\geq 3
. Note that
(E_{1}^{s-3})^{\prime}
is non-critical of Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}_{r_{s}+1},\;\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{r_{s}+t_{s-3}}
. Consider

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-3})^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}/(E_{1}^{s-3})^{\prime}).

But
{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-2})^{\prime})\cong{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-2},(E_{1}^{s-2})^{\prime})=0
by comparing Hodge-Tate weights. On the other hand, by an easy dévissage, we have
{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}/(E_{1}^{s-3})^{\prime})={\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},E_{s-2}^{\prime}-E_{s-1}^{\prime})
, which has dimension
\leq 2
. For Part
(2)
, the “if” part is obvious. Conversely, assume that
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})=2
and let
\iota_{1},\iota_{2}
be its basis. Consider the induced map
f_{i}:E_{i}^{\prime\prime}\hookrightarrow E_{1}^{s-1}\xrightarrow{f_{i}}(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
. Note that
{\rm End}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime})\cong\mathcal{R}_{E,L}\oplus{\rm End}^{0}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime})
with
{\rm End}^{0}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime})={\rm End}(E_{i})/\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
, we see that
{\rm Hom}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime},E_{i}^{\prime\prime})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime}))={\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})
and thus
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime},E_{i}^{\prime\prime})=1
, so that
a_{1}f_{1}+a_{2}f_{2}=0
for some
a_{1},a_{2}\in E
. Consider
\iota=a_{1}\iota_{1}+a_{2}\iota_{2}
, then
\iota
factors through a non-zero map
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{{\widehat{i}}}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
. This map factors through
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
and thus we obtain a map
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{{\widehat{i}}}\hookrightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\widehat{i}}^{\prime}
(and thus an injection by comparing Hodge-Tate weights). If
r_{i}=r_{s}
, it is an isomorphism by comparing Hodge-Tate weights.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

(i)

When
r_{i}>r_{s}
, the map
\alpha_{i}
is zero by comparing Hodge-Tate weights. To guarantee the existence of the non-zero map
\alpha_{i}
, we need to assume that
r_{1}\geq r_{2}\geq\cdots\geq r_{s}
.

(ii)

Section
6.4
give an easy description of Hodge filtrations associated to the different refinements when
n=4
and
S_{0}=\{2\}
.

The following proposition is similar to
[
18
, Proposition 2.4]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

(1)

Consider the following cup products:

{\rm Ext}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{1}(E_{s},E_{1}^{s-1})\times{\rm Hom}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\cup_{{\mathbf{D}}}}{\rm Ext}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{1}(E_{s},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}).\;

(3.4)

Then
E[{\mathbf{D}}]\subseteq[\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}]^{\perp}
via
\cup_{{\mathbf{D}}}
.

(2)

Assume
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
r_{s}=1
. Then
E[{\mathbf{D}}]=[\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}]^{\perp}
and
{\mathbf{D}}
is determined by
E_{1}^{s-1}
,
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
,
E_{s}
and
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}
.

Proof.

We follow the route in
[
18
, Proposition 2.4]
. As
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}
factors through
{\mathbf{D}}
, the map induced by the paring
\langle-,\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rangle
is equal to the following composition

{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{1}^{s-1},E_{1}^{s-1})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{1}^{s-1},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{1}^{s-1},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}).\;

The first map sends
{\mathbf{D}}
to zero, so
\langle{\mathbf{D}},\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rangle=0
. This is
(1)
. By dévissage, the kernel of such composition is
{\rm Hom}(E_{s},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}/E_{1}^{s-1})
. By
[
11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}(E_{s},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}/E_{1}^{s-1})

(3.5)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}((E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}\otimes E_{s}^{\vee})/W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(E_{1}^{s-1}\otimes E_{s}^{\vee}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{1\leq i\leq t_{s-1}}\oplus_{t_{s-1}+1\leq j\leq t_{s-1}+r_{s}}t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i+r_{s},\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR}}^{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{t_{s-1}+1-r_{s}\leq i\leq t_{s-1}}\oplus_{t_{s-1}+1\leq j\leq i+r_{s}}E.\;

So
{\rm Hom}(E_{s},(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}/E_{1}^{s-1})
has dimension
d_{L}\sum_{j=1}^{r_{s}}j=d_{L}\frac{r_{s}(r_{s}+1)}{2}
. If
r_{s}=1
and
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, it has dimension
1
, so that this kernel is exactly generated by
[{\mathbf{D}}]
.
∎

Fix
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, let
{\mathbf{I}}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{h}})
be the weight of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
such that
{\mathbf{I}}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{h}})_{\sigma,i}={\mathbf{h}}_{\sigma,i}
and
{\mathbf{I}}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{h}})_{\tau,i}={\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n}
for
\tau\neq\sigma
. Similar to
[
18
, Proposition 2.5]
(i.e., using the theory of
E
-
B
-pairs), there exists a unique
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module (up to isomorphism)
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
such that
{\mathbf{D}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
,
{\mathbf{D}}[1/t]={\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}[1/t]
and the Hodge-Tate weights of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
are
{\mathbf{I}}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{h}})
. Similar to
[
18
, Propositions 2.7
\&
2.8]
, the results in Proposition
3.1
are also true by replacing
E_{1}^{s-1}
(resp.,
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
) with
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}
(resp.,
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}_{\sigma}
). In a similar way, we obtain
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}:(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}_{\sigma}
. We get the cup-product

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{1}((E_{s})_{\sigma},(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma})\times{\rm Hom}((E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma},(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{1}((E_{s})_{\sigma},(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}^{\prime}).

(3.6)

Under the cup product,
E[{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}]\subseteq[\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}]^{\perp}
. If
r_{s}=1
, we have
E[{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}]=[\iota^{-}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}]^{\perp}
, and
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
is determined by
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}
,
(E_{1}^{s-1})_{\sigma}^{\prime}
,
(E_{s})_{\sigma}
and
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}
.

3.2
Deformations of potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, recall the
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
:
{\mathbf{D}}:=E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(1)}-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(s)}
in (
2.6
). Applying the discussion in Section
2.3
to
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
. For
?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}
, write
{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
for simplicity. We have the following inclusions of extension groups:

{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and the natural maps

\displaystyle\kappa^{?}_{u}:{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)

(3.7)

where
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}\cong{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{s}
is the center of
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
. By definition, we see that
\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{\circ}_{u})=\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{0}_{u})
. We first compute the dimensions of the above extension groups.

Proposition 3.4
.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}n^{2}
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S^{u}_{0}}
.

(2)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}(\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S^{u}_{0}}+s)
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s)
.

Proof.

To prove
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}n^{2}
, we just need to show that
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{0}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))\cong E
and
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{2}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))=0
. We have a natural exact sequence of

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
(where we write
{\mathbf{D}}:=[E_{1}-E_{2}^{s}]
):

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}(E_{2}^{s},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}(E_{1},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow 0.

Since
{\mathbf{D}}=E_{2}^{\prime}-\cdots-E_{s}^{\prime}-E_{1}^{\prime}
, we see that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{i},E_{j}^{\prime})=0
for all
2\leq i\leq s
and
1\leq j\leq s
by comparing Hodge-Tate weights, so that
{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{2}^{s},{\mathbf{D}})=0
by dévissage. Thus we obtain an injection
{\mathrm{H}}^{0}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{1},{\mathbf{D}})\cong E
, which is an isomorphism by comparing dimensions since
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{0}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))\geq 1
. Write
{\mathbf{D}}=E_{s}^{\prime\prime}-\cdots-E_{2}^{\prime\prime}-E_{1}^{\prime\prime}
. Then
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{2}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime},E_{j}^{\prime\prime})=0
for any
1\leq i,j\leq s
, thus
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{2}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))=0
by dévissage. For the second assertion in
(1)
, we follow the route in the proof of

[
25
, Proposition 2.21]
. Let
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}})
be the saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of
{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})
that preserves the triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, note that the rank of
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}})
is equal to
\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S^{u}_{0}}
. Then we have a natural exact sequence of

(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
:

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}(E_{u(s)},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}}}((E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime})\rightarrow 0,\;

Since
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{0}({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}))\cong E
, we immediately get that
{\mathrm{H}}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}^{0}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}}))\cong E
. We are going to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}({\mathbf{D}}))=0
. By induction on
s
, we need to show that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},{\mathbf{D}}))=0
. We divide into two cases. If for any
i<s
,
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},E_{u^{-1}(i)})=0
, then
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm Hom}_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},{\mathbf{D}}))=0
by an easy dévissage argument. Otherwise, we may assume that
(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
admits a unique quotient
E_{u^{-1}(s)-1}^{\prime\prime}
such that
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},E_{u^{-1}(s)-1}^{\prime\prime})\cong E
. Now we return to the same situation in
[
25
, Proposition 2.21]
and the vanishing result follows from the non-split assumption. Moreover, note that the codimension of the subsapce
\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{u^{-1}(i)},E_{u^{-1}(i)})\cong{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
(resp.,
\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1,0}(E_{u^{-1}(i)},E_{u^{-1}(i)})
) in
\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{u^{-1}(i)},E_{u^{-1}(i)})
is
s+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}^{u}-s(1+d_{L})=d_{L}(\dim{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}^{u}-s)
(resp.,
s+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}^{u}-\sum_{i=1}^{s}(1+d_{L}(1+\frac{r_{i}(r_{i}-1)}{2}))=d_{L}(\dim{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}^{u}\cap{\mathbf{B}}-s)
), these deduce
(2)
.
∎

Let
R^{+}_{u}:=\{(i,j):1\leq i<j\leq s,u^{-1}(j)=u^{-1}(i)+1\}
. Put

{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E):=\left\{(\psi_{i})\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\;|\;\psi_{j}-\psi_{i}\in\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j},\forall\;(i,j)\in R^{+}_{u}\right\}.

(3.8)

where
\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}
is the so-called parabolic simple
\mathscr{L}
-invariants in
[
25
]
, which is a subspace of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
with codimension
1
and depends on
u,i,j
. Moreover, we have
E\val_{L}\subseteq\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
for any
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. See (
6.3
) in Appendix-I for its inductive definition. In particular, if
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
, then
\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}=E\val_{p}
. In the following discussion, we do not need its precise description.

We next study the images
\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{\circ}_{u})=\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{0}_{u})
. The following proposition follows from the definition of (parabolic) higher
\&
simple
\mathscr{L}
-invariants and the so-called Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula (see
[
7
, Theorem 2.7]
), see Propositions
6.2

\&

6.3
in Appendix-I for its definition and proof. In the sequel, we write
I^{\prime}_{0}:=I^{\prime}_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
for simplicity if this not causes ambiguity.

Proposition 3.5
.

We have

\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{\circ}_{u})=\mathrm{Im}(\kappa^{0}_{u})={\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
. Thus,
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{w_{0,s}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)=s(1+d_{L})
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)=s(1+d_{L})-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
, where
{S^{-}_{0}}:=S^{w_{0,s}}_{0}
and
{\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L)={\mathbf{Z}}^{w_{0,s}}_{S_{0}}(L)
.

Put
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E):=\{\psi\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E):\exists\;\psi_{0}\in{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),\text{such that\;}\psi-\psi_{0}\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\}
. Note that
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)=s+d_{L}
. It is clear that

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E).

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g^{\prime},u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
) be the preimage of
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
) of via
\kappa^{?}_{u}
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
be the subspace of de Rham deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proposition 3.6
.

(1)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{g,1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.

(2)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.

Proof.

By
[
20
, Appendix, Proposition A.3]
, for any
i<j
, we deduce that
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(E_{u^{-1}(i)}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}{E_{u^{-1}(j)}}))=0
, where
W(-)
means the associated
E
-
B
pair. The dévissage argument shows the inclusion
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, the second assertion in
(1)
follows from the fact that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,0}(E_{i},E_{i})
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
is de Rham iff
\psi\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}_{p},E)
, we deduce that
{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. On the other hand, for
(\psi_{i})\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
, we will show that

\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(1)}-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(s)},\;

with
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}(i)}\cong E_{u^{-1}(i)}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
and
\psi\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}_{p},E)
is de Rham. It suffices to show the de Rhamness of
\widetilde{D}_{1}^{j}:=\widetilde{E}_{1}-\widetilde{E}_{2}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{j}
(with
j
increasing) step by step. This is true for
j=1
obviously. Suppose that the
\widetilde{D}_{1}^{j}
is de Rham and we show the de Rhamness of
\widetilde{D}_{1}^{j+1}
. It suffices to show that each element in

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}\left({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\widetilde{D}^{\prime})\right),\;\widetilde{D}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{D}_{1}^{j}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{u^{-1}(j+1)}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}(1-\psi_{j+1}\epsilon)

is de Rham. However, since all the Hodge-Tate weights of
\widetilde{D}^{\prime}
are positive, we deduce from
[
20
, Proposition A.3]
that
{\mathrm{H}}_{g}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\widetilde{D}^{\prime}))={\mathrm{H}}^{1}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\widetilde{D}^{\prime}))
(note that
\widetilde{H}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\widetilde{D}^{\prime}))={H}^{2}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(\widetilde{D}^{\prime}))
). We thus obtain
{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.
∎

In the sequel, we write

{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\text{\;and\;}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g^{\prime},1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

(3.9)

The following lemma is an analogue of
[
18
, Lemma 2.11]
.

Lemma 3.7
.

For any
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, put
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,\{u_{1},u_{2}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,u_{1}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,u_{2}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Then the following diagram commutes

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern (3.10)

Proof.

It suffices to prove the statement for the case that
u_{2}u_{1}^{-1}
is a simple reflection
s_{k}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Choose
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,\{u_{1},u_{2}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Suppose that

\widetilde{D}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)}-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(2)}-\cdots-\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(s)},\;

with
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(i)}\cong E_{u^{-1}_{1}(i)}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi_{i}\epsilon)
. By assumption
u^{-1}_{1}(j)=u^{-1}_{2}(j)
for
j\neq k,k+1
. Thus, for
j<k
or
j>k+1
, we have
{\rm Fil}^{j}_{\mathcal{F}_{u_{1}}}\widetilde{D}={\rm Fil}^{j}_{\mathcal{F}_{u_{2}}}\widetilde{D}
. Since
{\rm Hom}(\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)},\widetilde{D})\cong{\rm Hom}(\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)},\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)})\cong E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
, using dévissage for
{\rm Fil}^{j}_{\mathcal{F}_{u_{2}}}\widetilde{D}
, we deduce that
{\rm Hom}(\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)},\widetilde{E}_{u_{2}(1)})\cong E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
, hence
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(1)}
. Now consider
\widetilde{D}/\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(1)}
, repeat the same argument, we will get that
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(j)}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(j)}
for
j<k
. For
j=k
, we see that
{\rm Hom}(\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(k)},\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(k+1)})\cong E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}
, so that
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(k)}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(k+1)}
. Exchanging
\mathcal{F}_{u_{1}}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u_{2}}
, we obtain
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(k+1)}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(k)}
. For
j>k+1
, we consider
\widetilde{D}/{\rm Fil}^{k+1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u_{1}}}
, and see that
\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{1}(j)}=\widetilde{E}_{u^{-1}_{2}(j)}
for
j>k+1
. We complete the proof.
∎

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):=\ker\kappa^{\circ}_{u}\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):=\ker\kappa^{\circ}_{1}
. Then we have
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. As a corollary, we obtain

Corollary 3.8
.

For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{{u}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Moreover, we have

\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|
.

Proof.

Note that we have a natural map
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})
, it has kernel
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. By Proposition
3.4
, we get
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\big(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s\big)
, and
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)
has co-dimension
sd_{L}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
in
{\rm Hom}_{1}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)
by Proposition
3.5
, we get
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|
.
∎

In particular, we obtain from Proposition
3.5
that

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S_{0}}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|,\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S_{0}}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|-s,

(3.11)

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,w_{0,s}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S^{-}_{0}},\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0,w_{0,s}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S^{-}_{0}}-s.

For any subspace
V\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, let
\overline{V}:=V/V\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Therefore,
\kappa_{u}^{\circ}
induces isomorphisms

\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E),\;\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{g^{\prime},u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E),

and
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
. We have (compare the short
exact sequence in (
3.29
))

\dim_{E}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=d_{L}\dim_{E}{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|+s.

We next discuss some paraboline deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
. Fix
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, and
I^{\prime}=\{f_{1},\cdots,f_{r-1}\}\subseteq\Delta_{s}
(set
I=J_{u}(I^{\prime})\subseteq\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
). Write
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}={\rm GL}_{h_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{h_{r}}={\mathbf{L}}_{I,1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbf{L}}_{I,r}
. We get a
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
-parabolic filtration

\mathcal{F}_{u,I}:0={\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}_{0}{\mathbf{D}}\subseteq{\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}_{1}{\mathbf{D}}\subseteq{\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}_{1}{\mathbf{D}}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq{\rm Fil}^{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}_{r}={\mathbf{D}}

(3.12)

of saturated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodules of
{\mathbf{D}}
such that
M_{u,I,j}:={\rm gr}^{j}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}{\mathbf{D}}=[E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(f_{j-1}+1)}-\cdots-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(f_{j})}]
. Suppose that
\{u^{-1}(f_{j-1}+1),\cdots,u^{-1}(f_{j})\}=\{r^{u}_{j,1}<\cdots r^{u}_{j,f_{j}-f_{j-1}}\}
. Let
(\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)}=(x_{r^{u}_{j,l}})_{1\leq l\leq f_{j}-f_{j-1}}
. Indeed, let
[u]_{I}
be the the minimal length representative in the coset
u\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}\in\mathscr{W}^{I^{\prime},\emptyset}_{s}
. Then
r^{u}_{j,l}=[u]_{I}^{-1}(f_{j-1}+l)
for
1\leq l\leq f_{j}-f_{j-1}
. In particular,
M_{u,I,j}
inherits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}|_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I,i}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}^{(i)}
(associated to the form
[E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(f_{j-1}+1)}-\cdots-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(f_{j})}]
) from
\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
1\leq j\leq r
. With respect to the ordering in
(\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)}
,
\mathcal{F}_{u}^{(i)}
is equal to an
\Omega_{S^{(i)}_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u^{(i)}}
for some
u^{(i)}\in\mathscr{W}_{f_{j}-f_{j-1}}
(i.e.,
u^{-1}(f_{j-1}+l)=(u^{(i)})^{-1}(r^{u}_{j,l})
), where
S^{(i)}_{0}=\Delta_{I,i}\backslash\{t^{u}_{f_{j-1}}+r^{u}_{j,1},t^{u}_{f_{j-1}}+(r^{u}_{j,1}+r^{u}_{j,2}),\cdots,t^{u}_{f_{j-1}}+(r^{u}_{j,1}+\cdots+r^{u}_{j,f_{j}-f_{j-1}-1})\}
. We have
a natural map:

\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j}).\;

When dropping the generic assumption,
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
is not necessary surjective, which is different from
[
18
, Proposition 2.20]
).

Proposition 3.9
.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}\cup I}
.

(2)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is the preimage of
\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}^{(j)}}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})=\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{u}^{(j)}}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})
via
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
.

(3)

The map
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
sends
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
to
\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})
, and induces an injection:

\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\prod_{j=1}^{r}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j}).\;

Proof.

Only the statement in
(3)
need more discussion and we use the results (and keep the notation) in Section
3.3
. Restricting (
3.29
) on
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(and applying (

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}}\hskip 20.0pt}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u,I}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=1}^{r}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus_{j=1}^{r}\nu_{j}\hskip 5.0pt}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D^{u,I,j}_{\sigma},D^{u,I,j}_{\sigma}),}

(3.13)

where
D^{u,I,j}_{\sigma}:=D_{{\rm dR}}(M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma}
. The non-critical assumption shows that
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}})\hookrightarrow\oplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{j})
. Indeed, similar to Remark
3.18
and (
3.39
), we have

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u,I}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\cong{\mathfrak{p}}_{I,\sigma}\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{b^{\prime}_{\sigma}w_{0}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{b^{\prime}_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{I,\sigma}\cap\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\sigma})

for some
