, the latter space can be identified with
\oplus_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D^{u,I,j}_{\sigma},D^{u,I,j}_{\sigma})
. In particular, we deduce that
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
is injective on
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.
∎

Let
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
be the preimage of
\prod_{j=1}^{r}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{g^{\prime}}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})
via
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
(see (
3.9
)). In this case, the map
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
sends
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
to
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)
, where
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E):=

\left\{\psi\in{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E):\;\exists\;\psi_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I}}:{\mathbf{Z}}_{I}(L)\rightarrow E,\;\text{such that\;}\psi-\psi_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I}}\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}}\in{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\right\}.

(3.14)

We say an
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u^{\prime}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}
is compatible with
\mathcal{F}_{I,u}
if
u^{\prime}=u^{\prime\prime}u
for some
u^{\prime\prime}=(u_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{1\leq i\leq r}\in\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}\cong\mathscr{W}_{f_{1}}\times\mathscr{W}_{f_{2}-f_{1}}\times\cdots\mathscr{W}_{s-f_{r-1}}\subseteq\mathscr{W}_{s}
. For
u^{\prime\prime}=(u_{1}^{\prime\prime},\cdots,u_{r}^{\prime\prime})
, then

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u^{\prime\prime}u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\kappa^{-1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u_{j}^{\prime\prime}u^{(j)}}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})\Big).

For any
u_{1}^{\prime\prime},u_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}
, we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime},\{u_{1}^{\prime\prime},u_{2}^{\prime\prime}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{u_{1}^{\prime\prime}u}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u_{1}^{\prime\prime}u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{u^{\prime\prime}_{2}(u^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-1}}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime},\{u_{1}^{\prime\prime},u_{2}^{\prime\prime}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{u_{2}^{\prime\prime}u}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u_{2}^{\prime\prime}u}_{S_{0}}(L),E).}

(3.15)

where
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime},\{u_{1}^{\prime\prime},u_{2}^{\prime\prime}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):=\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{g,\{u^{\prime\prime}_{1}u,u^{\prime\prime}_{2}u\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
.

The remainder of this section discuss partially de-Rham deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
. Now we fix
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. For any
{\rm Ext}^{?}_{\ast}(-,-)
, we denote by
{\rm Ext}^{?}_{\sigma,\ast}(-,-)
the subspace consisting of those are
\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}
-de Rham (we refer to
[
18
, Section 2.3.2]
). For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, put
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E):={\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
.

Proposition 3.10
.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+n^{2}+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+(\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S_{0}}+s)+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s)+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)
.

(2)

\kappa_{u}^{\circ}
induces an isomorphism

\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
.

(3)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
I\subseteq\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}\cup I}+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)
. Moreover, the following natural map is surjective (resp., and thus an isomorphism):

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},\sigma}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j}),\;

(3.16)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\sigma}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})

Proof.

By
[
20
, Proposition A.3]
, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g,\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})))
. It is easy to see that
\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})))=0
(but
\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})))\neq 0
!), we thus get that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+n^{2}+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)
. Replacing

{\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})
with
{\rm End}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}})
, we get the result in
(3)
. Applying
(3)
to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and using the same strategy as in the proof of
(2)
in Proposition
3.4
, we get the second assertion in
(1)
. Part
(2)
is obvious. The last assertion in
(3)
follows from the commutative diagram (
3.20
) below.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

By Lemma
6.1
in Appendix-I, the codimension of
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
in
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
is less than or equal to the codimension of
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
in
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
, and they are equal iff
\dim_{E}\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)=1
(i.e.,
\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)=E\val_{L}
) for all
(i,j)\in R^{+}_{u}
.

Proposition 3.12
.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+d_{L}n^{2}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|
.

(2)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+d_{L}(\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S_{0}}+s)
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s)
. Furthermore,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\sigma}^{1}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+n^{2}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+(\dim{\mathbf{N}}_{S_{0}}+s)
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s)
.

(3)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
I\subseteq\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+d_{L}\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}\cup I}
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})=1+\dim{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}\cup I}
. For
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
, the following natural map is surjective (resp., and thus an isomorphism)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I,\sigma},\star}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{\star,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}_{\star}^{1}((M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma},(M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma}),\;

(3.17)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,}

\displaystyle\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\star,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\star}((M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma},(M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma}).

Proof.

By
[
20
, Proposition A.3]
and the fact that
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|
, we see that
\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})))\cong\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W({\rm End}({\mathbf{D}})))=|I^{\prime}_{0}|
, the result for
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
then follows from
[
20
, Proposition A.3]
. The second assertion in
(3)
follows from
[
17
, Propositions 3.6
\&
3.7]
(vanishing of
{\mathrm{H}}^{2}
for
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
-case).
∎

We also have natural maps
\kappa^{?}_{u}:{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
for

?\in\{\circ,0\}
. For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}):=\ker\kappa^{\circ}_{u}\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
and
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}):=\ker\kappa^{\circ}_{1}
. Then we have
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
. Similarity, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\ast,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{{u}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
for
\ast\in\{g,0\}
.

Similar to
[
18
, Proposition 2.23]
, we obtain a natural translation map:

\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}:{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}),\;\widetilde{D}\mapsto\widetilde{D}_{\sigma},

(3.18)

where
\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}
is the unique
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module (up to isomorphism) over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
such that
\widetilde{D}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}
,
\widetilde{D}[1/t]=\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}[1/t]
and the Sen
\sigma
-weight (resp., Sen
\tau
-weight,
\tau\neq\sigma
) of
\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}
are equal to those of
\widetilde{D}
(resp., are constantly
{\mathbf{h}}_{\tau,n}
). In particular, we have a restriction
\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
. Similar to
[
18
, Proposition 2.24, Corollary 2.26]
, we have
\dim_{E}\ker(\mathscr{T}_{\sigma})=(d_{L}-1)\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
. By comparing the dimension results in Proposition
3.10
and Proposition
3.12
, we deduce that

\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})

(3.19)

is surjective, for
\ast\in\{\sigma,g,\{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}\}\}
and
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}^{-1}({\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}))
, which induces an isomorphism
\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
for
\ast\in\{\sigma,g,\{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}\}\}
. Moreover, there is a natural commutative diagram (similar to
[
18
, Corollary 2.27]
):

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I},\sigma}\hskip 30.0pt}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I,\sigma},\sigma}\hskip 30.0pt}
\textstyle{\prod_{j=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}((M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma},(M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma}).}

(3.20)

3.3
Reinterpretation and Supplements for defermations

We recall a little about Fontaine’s theory of almost de Rham representations. Let
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}:=B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}[\log t]
and
B_{{\rm pdR}}:=B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}[1/t]
. The
{\rm Gal}_{L}
-action on
B_{{\rm dR}}
extend uniquely to an action of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
-action on
B_{{\rm pdR}}
with
g(\log t)=\log t+\log({\epsilon}(g))
. Let
v_{{\rm pdR}}
denote the unique
B_{{\rm dR}}
-linear derivation of
B_{{\rm pdR}}
such that
v_{{\rm pdR}}(\log t)=-1
. Note that
v_{{\rm pdR}}
and
{\rm Gal}_{L}
commute and both preserve
B_{{\rm pdR}}^{+}
.

Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Rep}_{B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
) be the category of (free of finite rank)
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representations of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
(resp.,
B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}
-representations) of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
. If
W\in\mathrm{Rep}_{B_{{\rm dR}}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
, let
D_{{\rm pdR}}(W):=(B_{{\rm pdR}}\otimes_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W)^{{\rm Gal}_{L}}
, which is a finite-dimensional
L
-vector space of dimension no more than
\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
. The
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representation
W
is called
almost de Rham
if
\dim_{L}D_{{\rm pdR}}(W)=\dim_{B_{{\rm dR}}}W
. The
B^{+}_{{\rm dR}}
-representation
W^{+}
is called
almost de Rham
if
W^{+}[1/t]
is almost de Rham. Let
\mathrm{Rep}_{{\rm pdR}}({\rm Gal}_{L})
be the category of almost de Rham
B_{{\rm dR}}
-representations
W
of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
.

Keep the notation in Section
2.2
. In this section, we use the language of
E-B
-pairs (see
[
30
]
). Recall that the
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{D}}[1/t]
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]
admits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
with graded pieces
E_{u^{-1}(i)}[1/t]=\Delta_{u^{-1}(i)}[1/t]
for
1\leq i\leq s
. Let
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=W_{{\rm dR}}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}})
(resp,.
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}({\mathbf{D}})
) be the
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation (resp.,
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}^{+}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E
-representation) of
{\rm Gal}_{L}
associated to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{D}}
. In the sequel, write
D:=D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})\cong(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{\oplus n}
for simplicity. For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, put
D_{\sigma}:=D_{{\rm pdR},\sigma}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}):=D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})\otimes_{L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}(L\otimes_{L,\sigma}E)
. Then
D=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}D_{\sigma}
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
on
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
induces a
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration:

{\mathbf{F}}_{u}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}

on
{\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}}
by
({\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)
-subrepresentations of
{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. For
1\leq i\leq s
,
{\mathbf{F}}_{u}
admits graded pieces
{\rm gr}_{i}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}{\mathbf{W}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\cong({\mathbf{B}}_{\rm dR}\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{\oplus r_{u^{-1}(i)}}
. We thus get
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration on
D
and
D_{\sigma}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{u}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)

\displaystyle:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D)=(D)

(3.21)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}={\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})

\displaystyle:\ 0={\rm Fil}_{0}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{1}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}_{s}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})=D_{\sigma}.

Note that
{\rm gr}_{i}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{\mathbf{D}})\cong(L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E)^{\oplus r_{u^{-1}(i)}}
. We have
{\mathbf{F}}_{u}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}
. On the other hand, the
B_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-lattice
{\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}
induces another complete flags of
D_{\sigma}
and
D
respectively (see
[
6
, Section 6.3]
):

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma})

\displaystyle:\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma})\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-1}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma})=D_{\sigma},

(3.22)

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}(D)

\displaystyle:\ 0\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n}}_{H}(D)\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n-1}}_{H}(D)\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{1}}}_{H}(D)=D

with
{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{\sigma,n+1-i}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma}):=t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{\sigma,n+1-i}}\big({\mathbf{W}}^{+}_{\mathbf{D}}\otimes_{L\otimes_{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}}E}(L\otimes_{L,\sigma}E)\big)
and
{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{\sigma,n+1-i}}_{H}(D):=\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{\sigma,n+1-i}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{\sigma})
. Note that
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{\bullet}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{\bullet}
.

Let
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
be the subspace of endomorphism of
E
-vector spaces
D_{\sigma}
which respect to the filtration
{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{\bullet}(D_{\sigma})
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
be the subspace of endomorphism of
D_{\sigma}
which respect to the filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{u,\sigma}
. Put

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):={\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\cap{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let

{\rm Hom}^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):=\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):\;f|_{{\rm gr}_{i}^{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma})}\text{\;scalar,\;}\;\forall\;1\leq i\leq s\right\}.

By the same argument as in the discussion before
[
8
, (112)]
, we obtain canonical
E
-linear morphisms:

\displaystyle\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}),

(3.23)

\displaystyle\nu:=\oplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

By
[
8
, Lemma 2.4.1]
,
\nu
(resp.,
\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}
) has kernel
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,c}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, i.e., the subspace of
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
consisting of those are
\sigma
-de Rham). Using the same strategy as in the
(1)
of Proposition
3.10
, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,c}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+(d_{L}-1)n^{2}+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}
and a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,c}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\tau},D_{\tau})\rightarrow 0

by comparing dimensions. Moreover, we have the following short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\nu_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}}\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\tau},D_{\tau})\rightarrow 0

by comparing dimensions. Let
\nu_{\sigma}:=\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}|_{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})}
. In general,
\nu_{\sigma}
is not surjective (but
\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}
is surjective). Noting that
\nu\neq\oplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\nu_{\sigma}
and thus we distinguish
\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma}
and
\nu_{\sigma}
. Moreover, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, the above morphisms induce

\displaystyle\nu^{?}_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma,u}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}^{\circ}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

(3.24)

\displaystyle\nu^{?}_{u}:=\oplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\nu^{?}_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma,u}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}^{\circ}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

and
\nu^{?}_{\sigma,u}=\nu^{?}_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma,u}|_{{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})}:{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}^{\circ}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

For
1\leq i\leq s
, put
D^{i}:=D_{{\rm pdR}}({\mathbf{W}}_{E_{i}})
and
D^{i}_{\sigma}:=D_{{\rm pdR},\sigma}({\mathbf{W}}_{E_{i}})
. Then
D^{i}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}D^{i}_{\sigma}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

(1)

, we have the following commutative diagram:

(3.25)

where

, and we have a natural inclusion

. We also have a
\sigma
-version when dropping the symbol

and restricting to

(2)

For
1\leq i\leq s
,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)
. We have a natural map (by inverting

):

\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t]).

This map induces an isomorphism
\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])
. In particular,
such map is not injective if
S_{0}\neq\emptyset
(resp., is an isomorphism if
S_{0}=\emptyset
).

In the sequel, put

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}):=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t]),\;

(3.26)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})={\rm Ext}^{1,0}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}):=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i},E_{i}).

For any
1\leq i\leq s
, we have a decomposition
{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{i},E_{i})={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{i},E_{i})\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\rm End}^{0}(E_{i}))
, where

. Therefore,
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
is a direct summand of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and thus we have a natural projection
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
(by inverting
t
).

For
?\in\{\emptyset,0,\circ\}
and

for some

, consider the natural morphism

g_{{\mathbf{D}},\star}^{?}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}_{\star,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}_{\star}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}).\;

, let

{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
be the image of

and put

.

Lemma 3.14
.

(1)

There is an isomorphism

{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}).

(3.28)

(2)

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, there exists a short exact sequence of
E
-vector spaces:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}_{\star,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{?}_{\star,u})\rightarrow 0,

for
?\in\{\emptyset,0,\circ\}
and
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
.

(3)

For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, there exists a short exact sequence of
E
-vector spaces (for
?\in\{\emptyset,0,\circ\}
and
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
):

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}_{\star}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\star}^{?})\rightarrow 0.

(3.29)

Moreover,
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu)=d_{L}\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma})=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
(recall
I^{\prime}_{0}:=I^{\prime}_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
).

Proof.

Recall the dimension of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
in (
3.11
) and the dimension of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
in Corollary
3.8
. The latter subspaces lead to a natural morphism
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
. Thus, we get
\dim_{E}{{\rm Ext}}^{1,0}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=s+d_{L}\dim_{E}{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}\cap{\mathbf{N}}_{\emptyset}
, which is equal to the
E
-dimension of the right hand side of (
3.28
), thus we get the isomorphsim in
(1)
. Moreover, we see that
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu)=d_{L}\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
. For
\star=\sigma
, we deduce from
(1)
of Proposition
3.10
that
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma})=1+n^{2}+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}(d_{L}-1)-(1+d_{L}\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+|I^{\prime}_{0}|)=\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
.
∎

Remark 3.15
.

Note that
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\Sigma_{L},\sigma})={\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
. Moreover,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu)\neq\oplus_{\sigma}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma})
so that
\mathrm{Im}(\nu)
mixes different components
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
.

Moreover, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}_{\star,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{?}_{\star,u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\rm Ext}}_{\star}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{?}_{\star})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(3.30)

Similar to
[
8
, (121)]
and the commutative diagram below
[
8
, (123)]
, the map in (
3.24
) factors through a map (recall that
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:={\mathbf{D}}[1/t]
)

{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{E}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}),

and we thus obtain a commutative diagram of short exact sequences:

(3.31)

where
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
is the image of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
via the natural map
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
.

Recall that
L^{\prime}
is a finite Galois extension of
L
such that
{\mathbf{D}}|_{L^{\prime}}
is a crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}
of rank
n
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}}:=\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]}\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}[1/t]
and
E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}}:=E_{i}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}[1/t]}\mathcal{R}_{E,L^{\prime}}[1/t]
for
1\leq i\leq s
. Note that
E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}}
are crystalline for all
1\leq i\leq s
. For all
1\leq i\neq j<s
, we deduce from
[
30
, Proposition 2.7]
that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{e}(E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}},E_{j}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}})=0
, we thus obtain

\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}}\cong\oplus_{i=1}^{s}E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}}.

Similar to the argument in
[
8
, (122)-(123)]
, we have a commutative diagram of short exact sequences:

(3.32)

where
D_{\sigma}\cong D_{L^{\prime},\sigma}^{{\rm Gal}(L^{\prime}/L)}
by definition and
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}})\cong\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}},E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}})
. The first vertical map in (
3.32
) is an isomorphism since

. By comparing dimensions, the last morphism in the bottom exact sequence of (

) is thus surjective, since
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}})=s+d_{L}n^{2}
. Combining (
3.31
) with (
3.32
), we actually obtain the following commutative diagram (an analogue of the commutative diagram before

, Proposition 2.4.4]
for potentially crystalline case):

(3.33)

Similar to
[
8
, Proposition 2.4.4]
, we see that

Proposition 3.16
.

There is a splitting of the bottom exact sequences in (
3.32
) which only depends on the choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
. Therefore, for
\ast\in\{\emptyset,u\}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, we have

\displaystyle f^{\circ}:{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\ast}),

(3.34)

\displaystyle f^{\circ}_{\sigma}:{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,\ast}).\;

Proof.

By the same argument as in the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 2.4.4]
, the following map is also surjective:

{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}|_{L^{\prime}})\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}},E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}})\twoheadrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}},E_{i}[1/t]|_{L^{\prime}}).

We get the first assertion. The assertion in (
3.34
) follows by replacing the top exact sequence in (
3.33
) with (
3.29
) (put
?=\circ
and
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
).
∎

Remark 3.17
.

In particular, the bottom exact sequence in (
3.31
)
splits. For
1\leq i,j\leq s
, the natural injection
E_{i}[1/t]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and the surjection
{\mathbf{D}}\twoheadrightarrow E_{j}[1/t]
induce a natural map

{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow\prod_{i,j=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{j}[1/t]).

It gives the splitting surjection
{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])\twoheadrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])
. In general, the top exact sequence in (
3.33
) may not admits a splitting.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
be the subspace of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
-parabolic deformations of
\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}
, we have a similar commutative diagram:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus\limits_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})}

(3.35)

where
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
is the image of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
via the natural map
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
. We have a composition of canonical surjective map

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})/{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0,u}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\twoheadrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{u^{-1}(i)}[1/t],E_{u^{-1}(i)}[1/t]).

(3.36)

We have a natural surjection

\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{E}(D^{u^{-1}(i)}_{\sigma},D^{u^{-1}(i)}_{\sigma}).

In conclusion, we have a commutative diagram of maps:

(3.37)

In the sequel, for
1\leq i\leq s
, let
\{e_{i,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{i}}
be a fixed
E
-basis of
D^{i}_{\sigma}
. With respect to the
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
-filtration
{\mathbf{F}}_{1}
on
D_{\sigma}
, we use the following ordering of the basis
\{e_{1,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{1}},\cdots,\{e_{s,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{s}}
(and we rewrite them with
e_{1,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n,\sigma}
by using lexicographical ordering) of
D_{\sigma}
, and identify the space
{\rm Hom}_{E}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
with
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\sigma}
, so
{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
) is identified with
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})
), where
\tau_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}),

(3.38)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}),

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}^{\circ}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}).\;

Remark 3.18
.

For each
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we deduce from the non-critical assumption that
g_{\sigma}{\mathbf{B}}_{\sigma}=ub_{\sigma}(u)w_{0,\sigma}{\mathbf{B}}_{\sigma}
for some
b_{\sigma}(u)\in{\mathbf{B}}_{\sigma}
(of course
b_{\sigma}(u)
depends on the
u
). Note that
\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\sigma}=\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
coincides with the Borel algebra of lower triangular matrices. We get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}))=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\cap\overline{{\mathfrak{b}}}_{\sigma}),

(3.39)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}).

We can say more about
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})
,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})
,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})
. Note that

{\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\oplus{\mathfrak{l}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
, where
{\mathfrak{l}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}\subseteq{\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
is the subspace of trace zero matrices in
{\mathfrak{l}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
. Put
{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{l}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}\oplus{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
, then

{\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}
. Put
{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},L}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},L}:=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. Under the identifications in Remark
3.18
, we have

(1)

\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
.

(2)

\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},L})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},L})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},L})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},L})
.

Proof.

The results for
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})
follow from the definition of parabolic simple
\mathscr{L}
-invariants
\{\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\}_{(i,j)\in R_{u}^{+}}
and the Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula (we use an easy variant of the
[
25
, Theorem 2.17]
), which implies that
\mathrm{coker}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})
(resp.,
\mathrm{coker}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})
) only comes from
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},L})
). Moreover, by comparing dimensions, we see that the inclusion
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})
(resp.,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})
) induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{coker}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{coker}(\nu_{\sigma,u})
(resp.,
\mathrm{coker}(\nu^{\circ}_{u})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{coker}(\nu_{u})
). The results for
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})
follow
.
∎

Remark 3.20
.

Assume that
\mathscr{L}^{u}_{i,j}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)=E\val_{L}
in
(\ref{dfnforimagehomou})
for all
(i,j)\in R^{+}_{u}
. Put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ad}^{\flat}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}):=

\displaystyle\left\{(a_{i})\in\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}):\;a_{j}=a_{i}\;\text{if\;}(i,j)\in R^{+}_{u},u^{-1}(i)\in I^{\prime}_{0}\right\},

(3.40)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{z}}^{(u),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma}:=

\displaystyle\left\{(a_{i})\in{\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}:\;a_{u(i+1)}=a_{u(i)}\;\text{if\;}u(i)<u(i+1),i\in I^{\prime}_{0}\right\}.

Then
\mathrm{Ad}^{\flat}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}^{(u),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma})
. In particular, we have
{\mathfrak{z}}^{(w_{0,s}),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma}={\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
and
\mathrm{Ad}^{\flat}_{w_{0,s}}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,s}}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
. By Proposition
3.5
,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}^{(u),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma})
,
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}^{(u),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma}+{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},\sigma})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ}_{\sigma,u})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}^{(u),\flat}_{S_{0},\sigma}+{\mathfrak{n}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
.

By the proof of Theorem
6.5
, we get that
\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
. Put

\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}:=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})\subseteq\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}).\;

(3.41)

(it seems that
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
depends on the sharp of parabolic subgroup
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
, and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
is independent on the choice of
\sigma
). We deduce from Theorem
6.5
that

Theorem 3.21
.

(Infinite-fern for potentially crystalline case) We identity
\mathrm{Im}(\nu)\subseteq\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
with a subspace of
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
.

(1)

The natural morphism
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is surjective.

(2)

For
?\in\{0,\circ\}
, we have
\mathrm{Im}(\nu)\cong{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{{\rm Ext}}_{g}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and

\displaystyle\rm{{\displaystyle Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})/{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{g}({{\mathbf{D}}},{{\mathbf{D}}})\cong\Big(\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}\Big)\cap\mathrm{Im}(\nu),\;

(3.42)

where the latter intersection is taken in
\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
.

(3)

\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu^{\circ})=d_{L}\dim_{E}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
and
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma}^{\circ})=\dim_{E}\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}-|I^{\prime}_{0}|
.

3.4
Hodge parameters and higher intertwining for potentially crystalline case

Recall the following two short exact sequences (see Section
3.1
):

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow E_{1}^{s-1}\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow E_{s}\rightarrow 0,

(3.43)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow E^{\prime}_{s}\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}\rightarrow 0.\;

In the sequel, we write
M:=E_{1}^{s-1}
and
N:=(E_{1}^{s-1})^{\prime}
for simplicity. Let
\mathcal{F}
(resp.,
\mathcal{G}
) be the
{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{n-r_{s}}}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{r_{s}}}
) parabolic filtration
M\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
E^{\prime}_{s}\subseteq{\mathbf{D}}
). We have natural morphisms

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s}),

(3.44)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,N)\times{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s}^{\prime},E_{s}^{\prime}).\;

Recall the subspaces
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{s},E_{s})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s})
and
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E^{\prime}_{s},E^{\prime}_{s})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(E^{\prime}_{s},E^{\prime}_{s})
. Put

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\times_{{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s})}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{s},E_{s}),

(3.45)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\times_{{\rm Ext}^{1}(E^{\prime}_{s},E^{\prime}_{s})}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E^{\prime}_{s},E^{\prime}_{s}).\;

By
[
17
, Proposition 3.7]
, the natural morphism
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}=(\kappa_{\mathcal{G},1},\kappa_{\mathcal{G},2}):{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,N)\times{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E^{\prime}_{s},E^{\prime}_{s})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,N)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
is surjective since
{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{s}^{\prime},M)=0
. The map

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}=(\kappa_{\mathcal{F},1},\kappa_{\mathcal{F},2}):{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{s},E_{s})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),

need more discussion. Its image is described by the so-called (parabolic) higher
\mathcal{L}
-invariants, see
[
25
]
and
[
7
, Section 2]
. In the sequel, we assume that
s-1\in I_{0}^{\prime}:=I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})
so that
{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{s},E_{s-1})\cong E
, i.e.,
(s-1,s)\in R^{+}_{1}
. The following lemma is an analogue of
[
7
, Lemma 3.5, (3.5)]
.

Lemma 3.22
.

We have a short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)\xrightarrow{\iota}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\kappa}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},E_{s})\rightarrow 0.

(3.46)

The subspace
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is the kernel of the following composition of the maps:

{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\kappa}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},E_{s})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{2}}{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,E_{s}).

Moreover, we have
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\kappa^{-1}({\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s}))
.

Proof.

By the hypothesis on
{\mathbf{D}}
, we have a long exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}},M)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}({\mathbf{D}},E_{s})

(3.47)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)\xrightarrow{\iota}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\kappa}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},E_{s})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},M).\;

We see that
\iota
is injective since the third arrow is an isomorphism (using the fact that
{\mathbf{D}}
is not split and noting that both source and target are
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces). We show that
\kappa
is surjective, i.e.,
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},M)=0
. By dévissage argument, it is enough to show that
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},E_{i})=0
for
1\leq i\leq s-1
. If
(i,i+1)
not belongs to
R^{+}_{1}
, this is obvious. Otherwise, we can write
{\mathbf{D}}=[E_{i+1}^{\prime\prime}-{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}]
, then
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},E_{i})=0
follows from the fact that
{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{i+1}^{\prime\prime},E_{i})={\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}}^{\prime},E_{i})=0
(of course, by Tate duality, we have isomorphisms
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},E_{i})={\mathrm{H}}^{2}(E_{i}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}{\mathbf{D}}^{\vee})={\mathrm{H}}^{0}((E_{i})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}{\mathbf{D}}(\epsilon))
, the vanishing of
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathbf{D}},E_{i})
also follows from the non-split assumption on
{\mathbf{D}}
). On the other hand, the kernel of
\kappa_{2}
is
{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s})
since
{\rm Hom}(M,E_{s})=0
. Therefore,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=\kappa^{-1}({\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s}))
.
∎

Consider the following commutative diagram of cup-products (note that
M=[E_{1}^{s-2}-E_{s-1}]
):

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 184.46017pt\raise-65.28003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{s},E_{s-1})\cong E}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

(3.48)

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M):=(E[{\mathbf{D}}])^{\perp}\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)
(resp.,
l_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M):=(E[{\mathbf{D}}])^{\perp}\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,E_{s-1})
) via the middle (resp. bottom) cup product. By definition,
\ker(f_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
. Since
{\rm Ext}^{2}(M,E_{1}^{s-2})=0
, we deduce that
f_{2}
is surjective. Therefore, we get a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\ker(f_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\rightarrow l_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\rightarrow 0.\;

We give the so-called Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula. See Proposition
6.2
in Appendix-I for its proof.

Proposition 3.23
.

For
(\widetilde{M},\psi)\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, there exists a
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
such that
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}(\widetilde{D})=(\widetilde{M},\psi)
iff

\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon)\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
.

Remark 3.24
.

We say more about the bottom cup-product in (
3.48
). Recall in (
2.3
) the injections
\mathbf{I}_{j}:E_{j}\hookrightarrow\Delta_{E_{j}}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{j}}})
of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
for
j=s-1,s
. On the other hand, we have an injection
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{s}:\Delta_{E_{s}}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+1}})\hookrightarrow E_{s}
of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
. Note that
\Delta_{E_{s-1}}({\rm unr}(q_{L}))=\Delta_{E_{s}}
, so we have a map
\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{s}(\epsilon):\Delta_{E_{s-1}}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+1}+1})\hookrightarrow E_{s}(\epsilon)
. Considering the composition:

\displaystyle g^{\prime}:=\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{s}(\epsilon)\circ g\circ\mathbf{I}_{s-1}:E_{s-1}\xrightarrow{\mathbf{I}_{s-1}}\Delta_{E_{s-1}}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}}})\rightarrow\Delta_{E_{s-1}}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+1}})\xrightarrow{\mathbf{I}^{\prime}_{s}(\epsilon)}E_{s}(\epsilon),

(3.49)

where the second map is induced by the injection
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(z^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+1}+1})
, which induces the following commutative diagram of cup products:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 237.61465pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-30.85245pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 54.85245pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\times}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 92.63025pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 170.73027pt\raise-27.64001pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.94446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cup\;\;\;\;\;}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 189.61642pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 189.61642pt\raise-32.58447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{s},E_{s}(\epsilon))\cong E}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.\;

(3.50)

The bottom cup-product is perfect by Tate-duality. By
[
11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}\big(M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{s-1},M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{s}(\epsilon)\big)

(3.51)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}\big({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{s}(\epsilon))/W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{s-1}\big)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}\big({\rm Gal}_{L},\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{1\leq i\leq r_{s}}\oplus_{1\leq j\leq t_{s-1}}t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+i+1,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR},\tau}^{+}/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-2}+i,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR},\tau}^{+}\big).\;

This implies
\dim_{E}\ker g^{\prime}=|\{(\tau,i,j):t_{s-2}+i<j\}|=\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}-1)r_{s}
. We deduce that
\ker(g^{\prime})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)^{\perp}

and
\ker(g^{\prime})\subseteq l_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
. In particular, the bottom cup-product is perfect (resp., not perfect) if
r_{s}=1
(resp.
r_{s}>1
). Moreover, we also deduce that
l_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
(resp.,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
) has co-dimension
1
in
{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,E_{s-1})
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)
).

Proposition 3.25
.

There is a natural exact sequence:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)/[{\mathbf{D}}]\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\oplus{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\rightarrow 0.\;

(3.52)

Proof.

By the proof of Lemma
3.22
, we get a short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\xrightarrow{\iota}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\kappa}{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s})\rightarrow 0.

(3.53)

In particular,
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is the inverse image of
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}(E_{s},E_{s})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},E_{s})
via
\kappa
. So for
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
with
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}(\widetilde{D})=(\widetilde{M},\psi)\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
,
\widetilde{D}\in\ker\kappa
iff
\psi=0
. On the other hand, the composition

\ker(\kappa)\xrightarrow{\iota^{-1}}{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)\xrightarrow{\kappa_{1}}{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M),

has image
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
. On the other hand, consider the exact sequence (note that
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{D}},M)
by non-split assumption):

0\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(M,M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},M).\;

(3.54)

The image of the first map is
E[{\mathbf{D}}]
. Thus we get an injection
{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)/[{\mathbf{D}}]\hookrightarrow\ker(\kappa)
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
has co-dimension
1
in
{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)
. We deduce a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{s},M)/[{\mathbf{D}}]\rightarrow\ker(\kappa)\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\rightarrow 0

by comparing dimensions. Therefore, we obtain a short exact sequence
0\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\kappa_{\mathcal{F}})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\rightarrow 0
. But we can construct an injection
{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\kappa_{\mathcal{F}})
, by sending
\psi
to the unique deformation
\widetilde{D}_{\psi}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
such that
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}(\widetilde{D}_{\psi})=({M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon),\psi)
(since
{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon)=0\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
), so such exact sequence splits.
∎

Remark 3.26
.

For
?\in\{g,0\}
, let
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}):={\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. By the last projection
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}
in (
3.52
), we have
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}({{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{g}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))={{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{g}(M,M)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}(L^{\times},E)
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}({{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{0}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}))={{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{0}(M,M)
.

Proposition 3.27
.

Put
\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)}:=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)/{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{0}(M,M)
. We have

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(N,N)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),

(3.55)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}:\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)}\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E),\;

Proof.

By
(3)
of Proposition
3.9
,
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}
are injective. The surjectivity for
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}
is obvious and the surjectivity for
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}
follows from Proposition
3.25
.
∎

For any
\iota\in{\rm Hom}(M,N)
, we have the following pull-back (resp., pull-forward) maps:

\displaystyle\iota^{-}:{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\text{\;and\;}\iota^{+}:{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,N).

(3.56)

Put
{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(M,M)=\iota^{-}({\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M))
and
{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(N,N)=\iota^{+}({\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M))
.

Lemma 3.28
.

For any integer
h
, put

T_{h}:=\big\{(i,j):1\leq j+h\leq i\leq\min\{t_{s-1},j+r_{s}-1\},1\leq j\leq t_{s-1}-h\big\}.\;

Then
|T_{h}|=a(t_{s-1}-\frac{r_{s}+h-1}{2})
for
a=\max\{0,r_{s}-h\}
.

Proof.

Assume that
h\geq r_{s}
. Note that
j+h\geq j+r_{s}-1
, we must have
t_{s-1}<j+r_{s}-1\leq t_{s-1}-h+r_{s}-1\leq t_{s-1}-1
, this is impossible. If
h\leq r_{s}
, we see that
|T_{h}|
equals to

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t_{s-1}+1-r_{s}}(r_{s}-h)+\sum_{j=t_{s-1}+2-r_{s}}^{t_{s-1}-h}(t_{s-1}-h-j+1)=(r_{s}-h)(t_{s-1}+1-r_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}-h)(r_{s}-h-1).

It equals to
(r_{s}-h)\times\Big(t_{s-1}+\frac{1}{2}(1-r_{s}-h)\Big).

∎

The following proposition is an analogue of
[
18
, Proposition 2.29]
.

Lemma 3.29
.

Let
\iota\in{\rm Hom}(M,N)
be an injection.

(1)

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(M,M)=\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(N,N)=1+\Big((n-r_{s})(n-2r_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s})\Big)d_{L}
(note that this dimension equals to
1+(n-1)(n-2)d_{L}
if
r_{s}=1
).

(2)

{\rm Ext}_{g}^{1}(M,M)\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(M,M)
and
{\rm Ext}_{g}^{1}(N,N)\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(N,N)
.

(3)

Assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is crystabelline. For
\iota^{\prime}\in{\rm Hom}(M,N)
,
{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(M,M)={\rm Ext}_{\iota^{\prime}}^{1}(M,M)
iff
{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(N,N)={\rm Ext}_{\iota^{\prime}}^{1}(N,N)
iff
E[\iota^{\prime}]=E[\iota]
.

Proof.

We just prove the result for
M
. The same strategy is also suitable for
N
. By an easy dévissage argument, the dimension of the kernel of
\iota^{-}
is equal to
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}((N)^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M,D^{\vee}_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)-1
. By
[
11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
and
[
18
, Lemma 2.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}(N^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M,M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)/W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(N^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M))

(3.57)

has dimension
|T_{0}|=r_{s}t_{s-1}-\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s})
(by Lemma
3.28
). Then we get
\dim_{E}\ker(\iota^{-})=d_{L}|T_{0}|-1
and thus
\dim_{E}\mathrm{Im}(\iota^{-})=1+((n-r_{s})(n-2r_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s}))d_{L}
. For
(2)
, the map
\iota^{-}
induces
\iota_{g}^{-}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(N,M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}_{g}^{1}(M,M)
. For any
M^{\prime}\in\ker(\iota^{-})
,
M\oplus M\subseteq M^{\prime}
implies
M^{\prime}
is also de Rham, so that
\ker(\iota^{-})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(N,M)
and then
\ker(\iota^{-})=\ker(\iota_{g}^{-})
. By
[
20
, Corollary A.4]
, we get that
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(N^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)=1+t+\frac{(n-r_{s})(n-r_{s}-1)}{2}d_{L}+|T_{0}|
and
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(M^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)=1+t+\frac{(n-r_{s})(n-r_{s}-1)}{2}d_{L}
, where
t:=\dim_{E}\widetilde{{\mathrm{H}}}^{2}_{\Sigma_{L}}({\rm Gal}_{L},W(N^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M))
, this shows that
\iota_{g}^{-}
is surjective. Part

(3)
is the same as
[
18
, Proposition 2.29]
.
∎

Remark 3.30
.

Assume that
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(M,N)=2
. We explain why we do not discuss
(3)
for potentially crystalline case. For
i\in\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
, let
\alpha_{i}
be as in Proposition
3.1
. We have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\alpha_{i}}^{1}(M,M)=\big((n-r_{s})(n-2r_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}-r_{i})(r_{s}+r_{i}-1)\big)d_{L}
. For
1\leq i,j\leq s-1
,
\dim_{E}({\rm Ext}_{\alpha_{i}}^{1}(M,M)+{\rm Ext}_{\alpha_{j}}^{1}(M,M))
depends on the sizes between
r_{i}+r_{j}
and
r_{s}
, and the strategy in the proof of
[
18
, Proposition 2.29]
may fails for potentially crystalline case. The same statement holds with
M
replaced by
N
. In precise, the map
\alpha_{i}
factors through the composition
{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(N_{{\widehat{i}}},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M^{{\widehat{i}}},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)
. The second map is an injection since
{\rm Hom}(M,M)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime},M)
(note that
M=[E_{i}^{\prime\prime}-M^{{\widehat{i}}}]
). The first map is surjective since
{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime},M)=0
(note that
N=[N_{{\widehat{i}}}-E_{i}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
). Therefore,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\alpha_{i}}^{1}(M,M)
is equal to the dimension of the image of
{\rm Ext}^{1}(N_{{\widehat{i}}},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}(M^{{\widehat{i}}},M)
. By
[
11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
and Lemma
3.28
, we have (note that
r_{i}\leq r_{s}
)
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}(N_{{\widehat{i}}}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M,(M^{{\widehat{i}}})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}M)=d_{L}|T_{r_{i}}|
. Note that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}(M^{{\widehat{i}}},M)=(n-r_{s})(n-r_{s}-r_{i})d_{L}
, we get the right dimension of
{\rm Ext}_{\alpha_{i}}^{1}(M,M)
.

By the same argument as
[
18
, Theorem 2.32, Corollary 2.33]
, we get the higher intertwining result for potentially crystalline case:

Theorem 3.31
.

For any
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
with
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}(\widetilde{D})=(\widetilde{M},\psi)
. The followings are equivalent:

(a)

\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
,

(b)

\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon)\in{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}^{1}(M,M)
(moreover, lies in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\cap{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}^{1}(M,M)
).

When
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, there exists
M_{0}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{-}(M_{0})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon),\widetilde{N}:=\kappa_{\mathcal{G},1}(\widetilde{D})=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{+}(M_{0})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon).\;

(3.58)

Remark 3.32
.

For simplicity, we assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is generic. Put
D_{M,\sigma}:=D_{{\rm pdR},\sigma}(W(M))
and
D_{N,\sigma}:=D_{{\rm pdR},\sigma}(W(N))
. As filtered
E
-vector spaces, we have

\displaystyle 0\subsetneq

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}+1}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})

\displaystyle\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,r_{s}+1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})={\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})=D_{N,\sigma},

(3.59)

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma})\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,r_{s}+1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma})\subsetneq{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma})=D_{M,\sigma}.\;

Write
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma}):={\rm Hom}({\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma}),{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma}))
, which is equal to the homomorphism space of the induced filtered spaces

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subsetneq

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})/{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}+1}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})

\displaystyle\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,r_{s}+1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})/{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}+1}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma}),

(3.60)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\subsetneq

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma})

\displaystyle\subsetneq\cdots\subsetneq

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}^{{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,r_{s}+1}}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma}).

Then we deduce that
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})=\frac{1}{2}(n-2r_{s})(n-2r_{s}+1)
. This dimension is also equal to
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)/{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(N,M)
(we can check that
1+d_{L}(n-r_{s})^{2}-(1+\frac{(n-r_{s})(n-r_{s}-1)}{2}d_{L}+|T_{0}|d_{L})=\frac{1}{2}(n-2r_{s})(n-2r_{s}+1)
). The injection
\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}
induces an injection of filtered
E
-vector spaces:
{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H,\sigma}(D_{M,\sigma})\hookrightarrow{\rm Fil}^{\bullet}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma})
. By Lemma
3.29
, we have the following commutative diagram of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(N,N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(N,N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{N,\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(N,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{-}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{-}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(M,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(M,M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\in\Sigma_{L}}{\rm Hom}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(D_{M,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

(3.61)

For
\star\in\{M,N\}
, we see that
{\rm Hom}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(D_{\star,\sigma},D_{\star,\sigma})=\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\star,\sigma},D_{\star,\sigma}):f({\rm Fil}^{-{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\sigma,n-r_{s}+1}}_{H,\sigma}(D_{N,\sigma}))=0\right\}
. They are subspaces of
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{N,\sigma})
and
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{M,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})
of dimension
\frac{1}{2}(n-2r_{s})(n-2r_{s}+1)
respectively. Therefore, the map
\iota^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(resp.,
\iota^{-}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
) sends
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})
as an isomorphism to the subspace
{\rm Hom}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(D_{N,\sigma},D_{N,\sigma})
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}(D_{M,\sigma},D_{M,\sigma})
).

For any injection
\iota\in{\rm Hom}(M,N)
, we define the set (similar to
[
18
, (2.36)]
)

\mathscr{J}_{\iota}=\left\{(\widetilde{M},\widetilde{N})\in{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Ext}_{\iota}^{1}(N,N):\exists\;M_{0}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\text{\;s.t.,\;}\iota^{-}(M_{0})=\widetilde{M},\iota^{+}(M_{0})=-\widetilde{N}\right\}.

(3.62)

If
\iota=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}
, write
\mathscr{J}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=\mathscr{J}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
. Similar to
[
18
, Corollay 2.33]
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
be the image of
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\oplus{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
in
{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Define

\displaystyle V^{\circ}(M,N):=

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)}\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\right)\oplus\left(\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(N,N)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\right),

(3.63)

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim,(\kappa_{\mathcal{F}},\kappa_{\mathcal{G}})}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\oplus\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}).\;

Then we get the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}},M,N)\rightarrow V^{\circ}(M,N)\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow 0,\;

(3.64)

where the kernel
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}},M,N)
consists of
(([\widetilde{M}],\psi),([\widetilde{N}],-\psi))\in V^{\circ}(M,N)
such that
(\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon),\widetilde{N}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon))\in\mathscr{J}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
.

Remark 3.33
.

The higher intertwining is also related to the infinite-fern for potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules. In the beginning, we try to use the higher intertwining to study
{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
for
?\in\{0,\circ\}
(recall (
3.27
)). Indeed, it not easy to describe
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
through the inductive method except
S_{0}=\emptyset
. Note that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\star}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+((n-r_{s})^{2}+r_{s}^{2}+r_{s}(n-r_{s}))d_{L}=1+\big(n^{2}+r_{s}^{2}-nr_{s}\big)d_{L}
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\star}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+(n^{2}+r_{s}^{2}-nr_{s}-(r_{s}^{2}-1))d_{L}=1+(n^{2}-nr_{s}+1)d_{L}
for
\star\in\{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\}
. By Theorem
3.31
, since

M_{0}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(N,M)\cap(\iota_{D}^{-})^{-1}\big(\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon)\big)
, we see that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\leq 1+\big(1+(n-r_{s})^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s})\big)d_{L}
, we only know that
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
has dimension at least
1+(n^{2}-r_{s}^{2}+1-\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s}))d_{L}
.

Remark 3.34
.

Fix
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. All discussion are suitable for
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
when we replace
M
(resp.,
N
) with
M_{\sigma}
(resp.,
N_{\sigma}
). More precisely, For any injection
\iota_{\sigma}\in{\rm Hom}(M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})
, we obtain the following pull-back (resp., pull-forward) maps by restricting to
\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}
-de Rham extension groups,

\displaystyle\iota_{\sigma}^{-}:{\rm Ext}_{\sigma}^{1}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma}),\iota_{\sigma}^{+}:{\rm Ext}_{\sigma}^{1}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma}).

(3.65)

Put
{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})=\iota_{\sigma}^{-}({\rm Ext}_{\sigma}^{1}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})),{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})=\iota_{\sigma}^{+}({\rm Ext}_{\sigma}^{1}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma}))
. We have surjections

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{G}}:{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E),\;

(3.66)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}:{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},\sigma}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}:M_{\sigma})}\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E),

where
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},\sigma}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}:M_{\sigma})=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}:M_{\sigma})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(M,M)
. The surjectivity of

\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}
follows from that the preimage of any element in
{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},\sigma}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}:M_{\sigma})}\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
is still
\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}
-de Rham. For any
\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
with
\kappa_{\mathcal{F}}(\widetilde{D}_{\sigma})=(\widetilde{M}_{\sigma},\psi)
. Then
\widetilde{D}_{\sigma}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma,\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
iff
\widetilde{M}_{\sigma}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}(\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon))\in{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})
. When
\widetilde{D}\in{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
, then there exists
M\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})
such that
\widetilde{M}_{\sigma}=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}^{-}(M)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon)
and
\widetilde{N}_{\sigma}:=\kappa_{\mathcal{G},1}(\widetilde{D})=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}^{+}(M)\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon)
. For any injection
\iota_{\sigma}\in{\rm Hom}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})
, we have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})=\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})=1+(n-r_{s})(n-2r_{s})+\frac{1}{2}(r_{s}^{2}-r_{s})
and
{\rm Ext}_{g}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})
and
{\rm Ext}_{g}^{1}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})
. When
{\mathbf{D}}
is crystabelline, for
\iota_{\sigma}^{\prime}\in{\rm Hom}(M,N)
,
{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(M,M)={\rm Ext}_{\iota^{\prime}_{\sigma}}^{1}(M,M)
iff
{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(N,N)={\rm Ext}_{\iota^{\prime}_{\sigma}}^{1}(N,N)
iff
E[\iota_{\sigma}^{\prime}]=E[\iota_{\sigma}]
. Consider the set

\displaystyle\mathscr{J}_{\iota_{\sigma}}=\left\{(\widetilde{M}_{\sigma},\widetilde{N}_{\sigma})\in{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(M_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})\times{\rm Ext}_{\iota_{\sigma}}^{1}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma}):\exists\;(M_{0})_{\sigma}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(N_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})\right.

(3.67)

\displaystyle\left.\text{\;such that,\;}\iota_{\sigma}^{+}((M_{0})_{\sigma})=-\widetilde{N}_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma}^{-}((M_{0})_{\sigma})=\widetilde{M}_{\sigma}\right\}.

If
\iota_{\sigma}=\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}
, write
\mathscr{J}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}=\mathscr{J}_{\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}}
. Finally, we define

\displaystyle V^{\circ}(M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})_{\sigma}:=

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},\sigma}({\mathbf{D}}:M)}\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\right)\oplus\left(\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\sigma}(N_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\right),

(3.68)

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim,(\kappa_{\mathcal{F}},\kappa_{\mathcal{G}})}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\oplus\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}).\;

Therefore, we get the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})\rightarrow V^{\circ}(M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})_{\sigma}\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow 0,\;

(3.69)

where the kernel
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})
consists of
(([\widetilde{M}_{\sigma}],\psi),([\widetilde{N}_{\sigma}],-\psi))\in V^{\circ}(M_{\sigma},N_{\sigma})
such that
(\widetilde{M}_{\sigma}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon),\widetilde{N}_{\sigma}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon))\in\mathscr{J}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}
.

Put
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},{\sigma}}({\mathbf{D}}:M):=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}(M,M)
and
V^{\circ}(M,N)_{\sigma}:=\left(\overline{\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM},{\sigma}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)}\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\right)\oplus\left(\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\sigma}(N,N)\times{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\right)\xleftarrow{\sim,(\kappa_{\mathcal{F}},\kappa_{\mathcal{G}})}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\oplus\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ,+}_{\sigma,\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}},M,N)_{\sigma}=\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}},M,N)\cap V^{\circ}(M,N)_{\sigma}
. Then we have the following commutative diagram of short exact sequences:
