(3.70)

4
Locally analytic representations for potentially crystalline case

Throughout this section, fix a non-critical potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
with Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}
and keep the notation in Section
2.2
. Recall the point
\underline{x}^{u}=(x_{u^{-1}(i)})_{1\leq i\leq s}\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}
for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, and the subset
S_{0}\subseteq I_{0}:=I_{0}({\mathbf{D}})\subseteq\Delta
and
I^{\prime}_{0}:=I^{\prime}_{0}({\mathbf{D}})\subseteq\Delta_{s}
and
{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0}}:={\rm GL}_{l_{1}n_{1}}\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{2}-l_{1})n_{2}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{(l_{m}-l_{m-1})n_{m}}:={\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},1}\times{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},2}\times\cdots\times{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},m}
. For
1\leq j\leq m
, let
\Delta_{I_{0},j}
be the set of simple roots of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}
, so that the set of simple roots of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0}}
is
\Delta_{I_{0}}:=\Delta_{I_{0},1}\times\Delta_{I_{0},2}\times\cdots\times\Delta_{I_{0},m}\subseteq\Delta
. For
1\leq j\leq m
, put
S_{0,j}:=\Delta_{I_{0},j}\cap S_{0}
.

Let
\theta=(n-1,\cdots,0)\in X_{\Delta}^{+}
and put a dominant weight
\lambda={\mathbf{h}}-\theta=(\lambda_{i}={\mathbf{h}}_{i}-(n-i))_{1\leq i\leq n}
of
{\mathfrak{t}}_{L}
.

Define a subset
\Delta^{\prime}=\{t_{j}^{u}:u\in\mathscr{W}_{s},j\in\Delta_{s}\}\subseteq\Delta
with
\Delta_{s}=\{1,\cdots,s-1\}
. Thus
\Delta^{\prime}=\cup_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, put
\mathcal{I}_{i}:=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:t_{l_{i}(u)}^{u}=i\text{\;for some\;}1\leq l_{i}(u)\leq s-1\}=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:i\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}\}
.

For admissible locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations
V_{1},V_{2}
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{D(G,E)}(V_{2}^{\vee},V_{1}^{\vee})
, where
D(G,E)
is the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic distribution algebra (so that the continuous strong duals
V_{1}^{\vee}
and
V_{2}^{\vee}
are coadmissible modules over
D(G,E)
), and the latter extension groups is defined in the abelian category of abstract
D(G,E)
-modules. In this case,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
is equal to the extension group of admissible locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations. Suppose that
V_{1},V_{2}
has the same central character
\chi
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}(V_{1},V_{2})
be the subspace of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic extensions with central character
\chi
. For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
and any
U({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}})
-finite representation
V_{1},V_{2}
, we denote by
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}(V_{1},V_{2})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
the subspace of extensions which are

U({\mathfrak{g}}_{\Sigma_{L}\backslash\{\sigma\}})
-finite. Put
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma,Z}(V_{1},V_{2}):={\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}(V_{1},V_{2})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}(V_{1},V_{2})
.

For any
I\subseteq\Delta
, let
\eta_{I}
be the square root of the modulus character of
{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}
. More precisely, write
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}={\rm GL}_{h_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{h_{r}}={\mathbf{L}}_{I,1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbf{L}}_{I,r}
, then
\eta_{I}=\prod_{i=1}^{r}|\mathrm{det}_{{\rm GL}_{h_{i}}}|^{\frac{1}{2}(-\sum_{j=i+1}^{r}h_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}h_{j})}
.

4.1
Preliminary on smooth and locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representations

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, put (note that
\pi_{x_{i}}=\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(E_{i})
, i.e., the smooth representation of
{\rm GL}_{r_{i}}(L)
over
E
associated to the
E_{i}
)

\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{s}\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(E_{u^{-1}(j)}).

For
S_{0}^{u}\subseteq I\subseteq\Delta
, consider the smooth principal series of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)
(if
I=\Delta
, we drop the symbol
\Delta
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I,u}(\underline{x})

\displaystyle:=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\right)^{\infty}

(4.1)

The first goal of this section is to study the irreducible consistents of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{x}):=\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{\Delta,1}(\underline{x})
(equivalently,
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{u}(\underline{x}):=\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{\Delta,u}(\underline{x})
). Put
I=I_{0}
and
u=1
, then

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I_{0},1}(\underline{x})\cong\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}\Big({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}(L)}\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(E_{u^{-1}(j)})\otimes_{E}\eta_{S_{0}}|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}(L)}\Big)^{\infty}.

Note that
\eta_{S_{0}}|_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}(L)}={\rm unr}(\alpha_{j})\circ\det_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{0},j}(L)}
for some
\alpha_{j}\in E^{\times}
by definition of
I_{0}
and
\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}
. For
J\subseteq I_{0}\backslash S_{0}
, put

v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{x}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}{\rm unr}(\alpha_{j})\otimes_{E}v^{\infty}_{J\cap\Delta_{I_{0},j},\Delta_{I_{0},j}\backslash S_{0,j}}(x_{j}).

Then the irreducible components of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1,I_{0}}
are
\{v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{x})\}_{J\subseteq I_{0}\backslash S_{0}}
(see Appendix-III). By
[
35
]
, the (smooth) parabolic induction
v^{\infty}_{J,\Delta}(\underline{x}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I_{0}}(L)}v^{\infty}_{J,I_{0}}(\underline{x})\right)^{\infty}
is irreducible, so that the Jordan Holder factors of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{x})\cong\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I_{0}}(L)}\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I_{0},1}\right)^{\infty}
are
\{v^{\infty}_{J,\Delta}(\underline{x})\}_{J\subseteq I_{0}\backslash S_{0}}
.

For
1\leq i\leq m
and subsets
I^{\prime}_{i},J^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\Delta_{I_{0},i}\backslash S_{0,i}
, by Appendix-III Lemma
6.7
, there exists a unique multiplicity free finite length
Q_{\Delta_{I_{0},j}}(I^{\prime}_{i},J^{\prime}_{i})
which has simple socle
v^{\infty}_{I^{\prime}_{i},\Delta_{I_{0},i}\backslash S_{0,i}}
and simple cosocle
v^{\infty}_{J^{\prime}_{i},\Delta_{I_{0},i}\backslash S_{0,i}}
. Furthermore, for any
I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}\backslash S_{0}
, we put

Q^{\diamond}_{I_{0}}(I_{1},I_{2}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{m}Q_{\Delta_{I_{0},i}}(I_{1}\cap\Delta_{I_{0},i},I_{2}\cap\Delta_{I_{0},i})

For
I_{0}\subseteq J\subseteq\Delta
, put

Q^{\diamond}_{J}(I_{1},I_{2}):=\Big({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)}_{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}_{I_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{J}(L)}Q^{\diamond}_{I_{0}}(I_{1},I_{2})\Big)^{\infty}.

In particular, if
I_{1}=I_{2}=I
, we write
Q^{\diamond}_{J}(I):=Q^{\diamond}_{J}(I_{1},I_{2})
for simplicity. Note that
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{x})=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(I_{0}\backslash S_{0},\emptyset)
and
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{w_{0,s}}(\underline{x})=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(\emptyset,I_{0}\backslash S_{0})
and the unique irreducible generic consistent of

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{1}(\underline{x})
is equal to
Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(\emptyset)
.

Let
\underline{\lambda}\in X^{+}_{\Delta}
. For any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
j\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
, put
\widehat{j}=\Delta\backslash\{j\}
. Let
\pi_{\emptyset,\widehat{j},u}
be the unique generic irreducible consistent of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{x})
. For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, put (see
[
31
, The main theorem]
)

C_{j,u,\sigma}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\widehat{j}}}\Big(\overline{L}(-s_{j,\sigma}\cdot\underline{\lambda}),\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,\widehat{j},u}\Big),

which is an locally
\sigma
-analytic representation of
G
. Note that
\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}=\{t_{1}^{u},\cdots,t_{s-1}^{u}\}=J_{u}(\Delta_{s})
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq s
and
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{L}
, then
C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\cong C_{t^{u^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}},u^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}}
iff
t^{u}_{j}=t^{u^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}
,
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
,
j=j^{\prime}
and
\{u(1),\cdots,u(j)\}=\{u^{\prime}(1),\cdots,u^{\prime}(j^{\prime})\}
.

From now on, put

\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})=\Big\{C_{j,u,\sigma}:u\in\mathscr{W}_{s},j\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u},\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\Big\}=\Big\{C_{{t_{j}^{u}},u,\sigma}:j\in\Delta_{s},u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset},\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\Big\}.\;

Note that
|\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})|=d_{L}(2^{s}-2)
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}
and
j\in\Delta_{s}
, let
I_{u}^{+}
and
I_{u}^{-}
be the subset of
I_{0}\backslash S_{0}\cong J_{1}(\Delta_{s})
such that

\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}}(L)}\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,\widehat{t_{j}^{u}},u}\Big)^{\infty}=Q^{\diamond}_{\Delta}(I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-}).

(the reason of such representation has simple
G
socle and cosocle is similar to
[
12
, Remark
(iv)
]
, since this representation is
G
-basic, see Appendix-III). For
j\in\Delta_{s}
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, let
[u]_{j}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}
be the minimal length representative in the double coset
\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}u\in\mathscr{W}_{\widehat{j}}\backslash\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Then
\pi_{\emptyset,\widehat{j},u}=\pi_{\emptyset,\widehat{j},[u]_{j}}
and thus
(I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-})=(I_{[u]_{j}}^{+},I_{[u]_{j}}^{-})
.

For any
I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq I_{0}
, put locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-algebraic representation
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{1},I_{2},\lambda):=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{1},I_{2})\otimes_{E}L(\lambda)
. In the sequel, write

\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0}\backslash S_{0},\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{0}\backslash S_{0},\lambda).

4.2
Preliminary on extension groups and principal series

The following proposition generalizes
[
8
, Section 2.1]
to potentially crystalline case. Write
{S^{-}_{0}}:=S^{w_{0,s}}_{0}
and
\underline{x}^{-}:=\underline{x}^{w_{0,s}}
for simplicity.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. We have isomorphisms of finite-dimensional
E
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right),

(4.2)

\displaystyle\text{resp.,\;}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

which have
E
-dimension
s+d_{L}
(resp.,
s+1
). The first term of the first (resp., second) isomorphism is isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma,g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{-}}(L),E):={\rm Hom}_{g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{-}}(L),E)\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}^{-}}(L),E)
).

Proof.

Write
\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}:=\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}:=\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
for simplicity. We prove that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
is
s+d_{L}
and define the maps. We define a canonical injection:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{w_{0,s}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big),\;

(4.3)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}^{-}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{-})\eta_{S_{0}^{-}}\otimes_{E}\big(1+\big(\psi\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{-}}}\big)\epsilon\big)\Big)^{\mathrm{sm}}\otimes_{E}L(\lambda).

Composed with the pull-back map for the natural maps
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}
, we get a map

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{w_{0,s}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right).

Let
Q^{\prime}=\ker(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda))
. We have the following two short exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right),\;

(4.4)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right),\;

where the last isomorphism follows from
[
32
, Lemma 3.2]
. By
[
33
, Proposition 4.7]
and the adjunction formula between (smooth) Jacquet functor and parabolic induction, we see that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,{\rm lalg}}_{G,Z}\left(Q^{\prime},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)=0
. Thus,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\star}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\star}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
for
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
. On the other hand, we have

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,{\rm lalg}}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)

is
s-1
-dimensional by using the (smooth) adjunction formula. Therefore, the restriction of (
4.3
) on the subspace
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(\overline{{\mathbf{Z}}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)
is an injection onto
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
, where
\overline{{\mathbf{Z}}}_{S^{-}_{0}}:={\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}/{\mathbf{Z}}
. We define a second canonical injection:

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big),\;\psi\mapsto\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\otimes_{E}\big(1+\big(\psi\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\rm GL}_{n}}\big)\epsilon\big).

(4.5)

Composed with the pull-back map for the natural maps
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}
, we get maps (we can check that such composition is also injective)

{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right).

(4.6)

The two injections (
4.3
) and (
4.6
) coincide on
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)
. It remains to prove that

\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)=s+d_{L},\;\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)=s+1.

If they are true, then we get the first isomorphism in lemma, and its restriction on
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
gives the second isomorphism. Write
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)-Q^{\prime\prime}
. We have the following two exact sequences:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q^{\prime\prime}\right),

(4.7)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q^{\prime\prime}\right)\rightarrow 0,

since
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\right)=0
for
i=1,2
. Thus, we get from the second exact sequence that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q^{\prime\prime}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,{\rm lalg}}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q^{\prime\prime}\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,Z}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
and the last map in the first exact sequence is surjective. By the same argument as in the proof of
(2)

[
7
, Lemma 3.17]
, we see that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\right)\cong{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
(resp.,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\right)\cong{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
). We get the desired dimensions.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
j\in\Delta_{s}
and
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}
. For
I\in[I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-}]:=\{I\subseteq\Delta_{s}\;|\;I_{u}^{+}\cap I_{u}^{-}\subseteq I\subseteq I_{u}^{+}\cup I_{u}^{-}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\right)=1,\;\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda),C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\right)=1,

(4.8)

which leads to a unique representations of the form
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}
(resp.,
C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}-Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda)
). The same holds when
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
(resp.,
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I,\lambda)
) replaced by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
(resp.,
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
).

Proof.

The first assertion follows from
[
12
, Proposition 5.1.14]
. We prove the existence of the unique representations of the forms
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}-C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}
and
C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}-\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}
. Indeed, for any irreducible consistent
V
appearing in
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}/Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
, we also get from
[
12
, Proposition 5.1.14]
that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma},V\right)=0
since
{\rm Hom}(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-},\lambda),V)=0
, so that the injection
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}
induces an isomorphism

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)
. Similarity, the map
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},\emptyset,\lambda),C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\Big)
induced by the surjection
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},\emptyset,\lambda)
is also an isomorphism.
∎

Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}L_{S_{0}^{u}}(\lambda)\Big)^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.\;

(4.9)

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. Note that the locally algebraic vectors in
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is equal to
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
. Let
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique subrepresentation of
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
and satisfies that all the irreducible constituents of
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
belong to
\mathcal{OS}^{1}_{{\rm gen}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
.

Lemma 4.4
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
i=t^{u}_{j}\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
for some
j\in\Delta_{s}
, we have a short exact sequence of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic representations

0\rightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\rightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}C_{i,[u]_{j},\sigma}\rightarrow 0.

(4.10)

Proof.

We have

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(C_{i,[u]_{j},\sigma},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\right)={\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(C_{t_{j}^{u},u,\sigma},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\right)\neq 0
by Lemma
4.3
. For any other non-generic irreducible consistent
V
of
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{\widehat{j},u}(\underline{x})
, since
{\rm Hom}\big(\big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{i}}(L)}V\big)^{\infty},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\big)=0
, we obtain that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{i}}}\big(\overline{L}(-s_{i,\sigma}\cdot{\lambda}),V\big),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\big)=0
by
[
12
, Proposition 5.1.14]
. Therefore, only the non-split extension
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{i,[u]_{j},\sigma}
lies in
\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. The result follows.
∎

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
i=t^{u}_{j}\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
for some
j\in\Delta_{s}
, fix
V_{i,u,\sigma}
a locally
\sigma
-analytic representation of
G
, which is isomorphic to a non-split extension of
C_{i,u,\sigma}
by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
(see Lemma
4.3
,
V_{i,u,\sigma}
is unique up to a scalar), i.e.,

V_{i,u,\sigma}:=[\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})-C_{i,u,\sigma}],

and fix an injection
\iota_{i,u,\sigma}:\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow V_{i,u,\sigma}
. We have
C_{i,u,\sigma}\cong V_{i,u,\sigma}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}\subseteq{\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}\cong{\rm GL}_{i}\times{\rm GL}_{n-i}
.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
and
i=t^{u}_{j}\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
for some
1\leq j\leq s-1
. There is a canonical isomorphism of
(s+2)
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces associated to
(V_{i,u,\sigma},\iota_{i,u,\sigma})
:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\right).

(4.11)

and the restriction of (
4.11
) on
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)
only depends on
(V_{i,u,\sigma},\iota_{i,u,\sigma})
. Moreover, there is a short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)\xrightarrow{\iota_{i,u,\sigma}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow 0.

(4.12)

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
4.3
and
an exact sequence
0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)
that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\leq s+2
. We construct the desired map and show that the last map in such exact sequence is surjective. We first define the injection (by Lemma
4.2
):

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\Big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\Big).\;

(4.13)

We next construct a canonical injection
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
. Put

R_{u}:=\mathcal{F}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\widehat{i}}}\Big(\overline{M}_{\widehat{i}}(-\lambda_{\sigma}),\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,\widehat{i},u}\Big).\;

(4.14)

Let
R_{u}^{\prime}
be the unique quotient of
R_{u}
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
. Consider the following injection:

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(R_{u},R_{u}\right)

(4.15)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{i}}(L)}\pi^{\infty}_{\emptyset,\widehat{i},u}\otimes L_{\widehat{i}}(\lambda_{\sigma})\otimes(1+\psi\epsilon)\right)^{\sigma-{\rm an}}.

Composed with the pull-back map for the natural map
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},I_{u}^{-},\lambda)\hookrightarrow R_{u}
and the push-forward map via the map
R_{u}\twoheadrightarrow R_{u}^{\prime}
, we get a map
f_{u}:{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},R_{u}\big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},R^{\prime}_{u}\big)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\big)
(it is still an injection and we can prove that it only depends on
(V_{i,u,\sigma},\iota_{i,u,\sigma})
). The map
f_{u}
induces an injection

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E))\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big),

which is compatible with the injection
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\big)
. Thus, we get the desired map. On the other hand, by the second isomorphism in Lemma
4.5
, we see that the image of
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)
in
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\big)
is not contained in
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
, so that the second map in (
4.11
) is an surjection and thus an isomorphism by comparing the dimensions.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

For any non-zero
E
-line
\mathcal{L}
in such extension group, we obtain a locally
\sigma
-analytic representation of the form
\pi_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}(\mathcal{L}):=[\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})-C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}-\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})]
. We can show that
I_{u}^{+}\cap I_{u}^{-}=\emptyset
, so that we can choose
I=\emptyset
in Lemma
4.3
. Indeed,
\pi_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}(\mathcal{L})
is obtained from a locally analytic representation
[Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}-Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},\emptyset,\lambda)]
by adding exceptional factors. But it is more natural to consider the extensions groups in (
4.11
), since
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{u}^{+},\emptyset,\lambda),Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)-C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\right)
is usually depends on
u
, which leads confusion if
we take certain amalgamated sum of
V_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}
over
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
j\in\Delta_{s}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

We have a perfect paring of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces:

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)\times{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E).\;

(4.16)

and a canonical isomorphism of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector space
E\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)
,
1\mapsto\log_{p,\sigma}\circ\det
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 2.1.10]
, we have the following (formal) isomorphism of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces:

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)\otimes_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(C_{i,u,\sigma}\otimes{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right),

where the
G
-representation
C_{i,u,\sigma}\otimes{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
has the trivial action of
G
on
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
. Any
representative
V^{\prime}_{i,u,\sigma}
of the image of the canonical vector of the left hand side is isomorphic to a non-split extension of
C_{i,u,\sigma}
by
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}
. Then
V^{\prime}_{i,u,\sigma}
corresponds to an injection
\iota:\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}_{i,u,\sigma}
and an isomorphism
\kappa:V^{\prime}_{i,u,\sigma}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\xrightarrow{\sim}C_{i,u,\sigma}\otimes_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
. By the same strategy as in the
[
8
, (37)]
, we have a canonical isomorphism of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces (where the embedding
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)
is induced by
\det:{\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
):

{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)/{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E).

By the proof of Proposition
4.5
(similar to
[
8
, (34)]
), the latter space is isomorphic to
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
and thus isomorphic to (via
\kappa
)
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\otimes_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)\right)\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)\otimes_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}},C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
, the result follows.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

Similar to the Step
1
in the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 2.2.4]
, the surjection
{\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm GL}_{n-i}(L)
gives a canonical injection
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(L),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)
. A choice of
\log_{p}(p)
define a section
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
to the restriction map
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times},E)
, and thus a section
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(L),E)
to the restriction map
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(L),E)\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)
, which induces an isomophism:

\displaystyle\Big({\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\Big)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E).

We thus get (similar to
[
8
, (59)]
) isomorphsims
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}\right)
and

\displaystyle\Big({\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\Big)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}},V_{i,u,\sigma}\right).

4.2.1
Constructions of locally analytic representations

Consider the amalgamated sums:

\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s},u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}V_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma},\;\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}:=\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}V_{t^{u}_{j},[u]_{j},\sigma}.\;

for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Note that
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is a locally analytic representation which lies in the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s},u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\rightarrow 0,

(4.17)

By Lemma
4.4
, we get that
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is certain ”amalgamated sum” of
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
when
u
are taken over
\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Moreover,
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}
is the unique locally analytic subrepresentation of
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
which lies in the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}C_{t_{j}^{u},[u]_{j},\sigma}\rightarrow 0,

(4.18)

In particular, we have injections
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we consider the natural map

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\big(\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\big),\;

(4.19)

\displaystyle\psi

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}L_{S^{u}_{0}}(\lambda)\otimes_{E}(1+(\psi\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}^{u}}})\epsilon)\Big)^{{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}-{\rm an}}.

In particular, by Schraen’s spectral sequence
[
33
, (4.37)]
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})).

(4.20)

Then the natural quotient map
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
induces a map

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

By
[
19
, Lemma 2.26]
, this map factors through (induced by the injection
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
)

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right),

then we obtain a map
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
. Composed with the pull-back map for the natural map

p_{u}:\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\backslash I_{u}^{-},\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\backslash I_{u}^{-},I_{u}^{-},\lambda)=\mathrm{PS}_{u}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),

we get a map:

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

(4.21)

Composing with the push-forward map via the injection
\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
, we actually obtain a map:

\zeta_{u}:{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

Remark 4.9
.

The Schraen’s spectral sequence
[
33
, (4.37)]
gives an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})).

(4.22)

Thus, the above morphism
\zeta_{u}
is equal to the composition
{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
.

Proposition 4.10
.

(1)

We have
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)=s(1+d_{L})
and an exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)

(4.23)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{t_{j}^{u},[u]_{j},\sigma}\right)\rightarrow 0.

Thus, we get an ismorphism
\zeta_{u}:{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)
.

(2)

We have
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)=s+(2^{s}-1)d_{L}
and an exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)

(4.24)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s},u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\right)\rightarrow 0.

Proof.

For
(1)
, by Proposition
4.5
, the second last map in (
4.23
) is surjective. On the other hand, we can see that
the natural morphism (by Remark
4.8
)

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow\Big({\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)}{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\Big)\oplus\Big(\oplus_{\sigma,i\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}),E)\Big)

is an isomorphism, therefore
\zeta_{u}
is an ismorphism. When
u
varying, we prove that the second last map in
(2)
is also surjective since it is covered by the last term in (
4.23
) with
u
varying. The dimension of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
is equal to
s+d_{L}+d_{L}\sum_{j\in\Delta_{s}}\#\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}=s+(2^{s}-1)d_{L}
.
∎

Therefore, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
be the image of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\right)
in the full space
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
via the injection
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. From Proposition
4.10
, we see that
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
is also equal to
\mathrm{Im}(\zeta_{u})
. Denote by

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\subset{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g^{\prime}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\Big(\subset{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\Big)

the respective image of
{\rm Ext}^{1,{\rm lalg}}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
via (
4.23
). We have a neater commutative diagram (compare with the commutative diagram in Lemma
3.7
fro Galois side):

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 42.83913pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.83913pt\raise (4.25)

for any
u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
.

Remark 4.11
.

Fix
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
, replacing the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction in (
4.9
) by locally
\sigma
-analytic parabolic induction, we obtain a
\sigma
-analytic version of all above discussion. We get a locally
\sigma
-analytic representation
\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
which lies in the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\end{subarray}}C_{t_{j}^{u},u,\sigma}\rightarrow 0.\;

(4.26)

Note that
\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is also equal to the amalgamated sum
\bigoplus^{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}_{j\in\Delta_{s},u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}}V_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}
. In this case, we have an isomorphism
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma,g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
and a short exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)

(4.27)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta_{s}\\
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\end{subarray}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{t^{u}_{j},u,\sigma}\right)\rightarrow 0.

Moreover,
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)=s+(2^{s}-1)
. In a similar way, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we get a map:

\zeta_{u,\sigma}:{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

The remainder of this section talks about some parabolic inductions. Fix
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and keep the notation around (
3.12
). Fix
I^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{s}
and
I=J_{u}(I^{\prime})
. Recall
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}={\rm GL}_{h_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\rm GL}_{h_{r}}={\mathbf{L}}_{I,1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbf{L}}_{I,r}
. For
i=1,\cdots,r
, we let
{\mathbf{h}}^{i}:=({\mathbf{h}}_{t^{u}_{i-1}+1},\cdots,{\mathbf{h}}_{t^{u}_{i}})
and
\lambda^{i}:=(\lambda_{t^{u}_{i-1}+1},\cdots,\lambda_{t^{u}_{i}})
. Recall that
\mathcal{F}_{I,u}
is the associated
{\mathbf{P}}_{I}
-parabolic filtration on
{\mathbf{D}}
. We apply all above constructions to
\{M_{u,I,i}\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
, and obtain
{\rm GL}_{n_{i}}(L)
-representations
\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i})\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i})
. Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{I}(L)}\left(\widehat{\boxtimes}_{i=1}^{r}\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\right)\eta_{I}\right)^{{\rm an}}.

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. The locally algebraic vectors
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}
of
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is a subrepresentation of
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{u}^{-},\lambda)
.

Moreover, the representation
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
contains a unique subrepresentation
\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
of
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with
\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}=\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}
and
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}=\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}
. Let
\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique subrepresentation of
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
such that
\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}=\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})=\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm lalg}}
. The number of the irreducible constituents in
\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is equals to
d_{L}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}(2^{f_{i}-f_{i-1}}-2)+(r-1)\big)
. We have an injection of locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic
G
-representations
\pi^{-}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
be the image of the injective push-forward map

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

On the other hand, by Schraen’s spectral sequence (see
[
33
, Corollary 4.5]
), there is a bijection

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{P_{I},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\left({\mathrm{H}}_{0}(\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}_{I},\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})),\widehat{\boxtimes}_{i=1}^{r}\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\right).

(4.28)

On the other hand, we have a natural map

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{h_{i}}(L)}

\displaystyle\Big(Q_{\Delta_{I,i}}^{\diamond}\big((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\cap\Delta_{I,i},\emptyset,\lambda^{i}\big),\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\Big)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\left({\mathrm{H}}_{0}(\overline{{\mathbf{N}}}_{I},\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})),\widehat{\boxtimes}_{i=1}^{r}\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\right).

So we get a map

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}:\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{h_{i}}(L)}\Big(Q_{\Delta_{I,i}}^{\diamond}

\displaystyle\big((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\cap\Delta_{I,i},\emptyset,\lambda^{i}\big),\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\Big)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)

(4.30)

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

Proposition 4.12
.

The map
\zeta_{P_{I},u}
is bijective. For any
u^{\prime\prime}=(u_{i}^{\prime\prime})_{1\leq i\leq r}\in\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}
(i.e., the
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u^{\prime\prime}u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}
is compatible with
\mathcal{F}_{I,u}
), the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u^{\prime\prime}u}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I,i}(L),E\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{extenforPSw})}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u^{\prime\prime}u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{extenforPSw})}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u^{\prime\prime}_{i}u^{(i)}}\Big(Q_{\Delta_{I,i}}^{\diamond}\big((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\cap\Delta_{I,i},\emptyset,\lambda^{i}\big),\pi_{1}\big((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}\big)\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u^{\prime\prime}u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).}

(4.31)

Proof.

The commutativity follows by definition and the transitivity of the parabolic induction. Since

\bigoplus_{u^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u^{\prime\prime}u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right),

We see that
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}
is surjective. The dimension of left hand side of
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}
is equal to
s+rd_{L}+d_{L}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(2^{f_{i}-f_{i-1}}-2)
, while the dimension of right hand side of
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}
is equal to this number, hence
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}
is bijective.
∎

As in
[
18
, (3.30)]
, let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},g^{\prime}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
be the image of

\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{n_{i}}(L),g^{\prime}}(Q_{\Delta_{n_{i}}}^{\diamond}((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\cap\Delta_{I,i},\emptyset,\lambda^{i}),\pi_{1}((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}))
via
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}
. We have the following commutative diagram :

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 51.63976pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-51.63976pt\raise 75.63976pt\raise-33.9245pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u},g^{\prime}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

(4.32)

for any
u^{\prime\prime}_{1},u^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in\mathscr{W}_{I^{\prime}}
.

Finally, we apply the above discussion to
{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{n-r_{s}}}
-parabolic filtration
\mathcal{F}
and
{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{r_{s}}}
-parabolic filtration
\mathcal{G}
corresponding to the short exact sequences
0\rightarrow M\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow E_{s}\rightarrow 0
and
0\rightarrow E^{\prime}_{s}\rightarrow{\mathbf{D}}\rightarrow N\rightarrow 0
respectively (see Section
3.4
). Put
\underline{x}^{1}=(x_{i})_{1\leq i\leq s-1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{M}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{h}}_{N}
) the Hodge-Tate weights of
M
(resp.,
N
). Let
{\mathbf{h}}_{E_{s}}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{h}}_{E_{s}^{\prime}}
) the Hodge-Tate weights of
E_{s}
(resp.,
E_{s}^{\prime}
). Consider the locally analytic parabolic inductions:

\displaystyle\pi_{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\widehat{n-r_{s}}}}\big(\pi_{1}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{M})\boxtimes\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(x_{s},{\mathbf{h}}_{E_{s}})\big)\eta_{\widehat{n-r_{s}}}\right)^{{\rm an}},

(4.33)

\displaystyle\pi_{1}({\mathbf{D}},N)=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{\widehat{r_{s}}}}\big(\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(x_{s},{\mathbf{h}}_{E^{\prime}_{s}})\boxtimes\pi_{1}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{N})\big)\eta_{\widehat{r_{s}}}\right)^{{\rm an}},

By the discussion before (
4.28
),
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
contains a unique subrepresentation
\pi_{\mathcal{F},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
(resp.,
\pi_{\mathcal{G},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
) which is associated to
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{D}},M)
(resp.,
\pi_{1}({\mathbf{D}},N)
). Moreover, we have a natural exact sequence

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{\mathcal{F},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\oplus\pi_{\mathcal{G},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow 0.\;

(4.34)

Applying (
4.30
) and Proposition
4.12
to
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathcal{F}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{n-r_{s}}(L)}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{M}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{M})\right)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{\mathcal{F},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)

(4.35)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathcal{G}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{n-r_{s}}(L)}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{N}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{N})\right)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{\mathcal{G},1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

4.3
Main theorems

We have the following composition

\displaystyle\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\text{surjection}]{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\kappa_{u}}\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)

(4.36)

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\zeta_{u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

Denote by
\kappa^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
the composition of the first two maps and
\zeta_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\zeta_{u}
(thus
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=\zeta_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ\kappa^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
). We state the first main theorem.

Theorem 4.13
.

The map
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
factors through
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
, and there exists a unique map

t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

such that
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. Note that
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
factor through
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\hookrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
by definition.

Proof.

We follow the route of
[
18
, Theorem 3.21]
and prove the theorem by induction on the number
s
. For
s=1
, this is obvious. Suppose it holds for
s-1
. By induction hypothesis, the map
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s-1}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}(M,M)\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(M,M)
factors through the following map (we can identity
\mathscr{W}_{s-1}
with
\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:u^{-1}(s)=s\}
)

t^{\circ}_{M}:\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{M})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\sharp}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{M}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{M})\right),\;

where. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s-1}
, the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)\cap{\rm GL}_{n-r_{s}},E)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}(M,M)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

(4.37)

Thus, by (
4.35
), the map
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s-1}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
factors through
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{M}))
. Applying the same discussion to
N
, we obtain
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}^{\prime}_{s-1}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
factors through
\zeta_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{N}))
, where
\mathscr{W}_{s}^{\prime}:=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:u^{-1}(s)=1\}\cong\mathscr{W}_{s-1}
. Therefore, we get that

\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s-1}\cup\mathscr{W}^{\prime}_{s-1}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

factors through the composition
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\zeta_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{M}))\oplus\zeta_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{N}))\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. It suffices to show that
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{M}))\oplus\zeta_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{N}))\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and for other
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, the map
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
still factors through
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. Thus two results can be proved at the same time by using the same strategy as the proof of
[
18
, Theorem 3.21]
.

Suppose that
u^{-1}(s)=i
. For
j\in\Delta_{s}
, we can still find an element
u_{1}
in the subgroup
\mathscr{W}_{\Delta_{s}\backslash\{j\}}\subseteq\mathscr{W}_{s}
such that
u_{1}^{-1}(i)=1
or
u_{1}^{-1}(i)=s
, so that
uu_{1}\in\mathscr{W}_{s-1}\cup\mathscr{W}^{\prime}_{s-1}
. In a similar way, we consider the following commutative diagram (using commutative diagrams (
3.15
) and (
4.32
)):

\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime},\{u,uu_{1}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{u}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{u_{2}u_{1}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{u}\hskip 15.0pt}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime},\{u,uu_{1}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{uu_{1}}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{uu_{1}}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{uu_{1}}\hskip 15.0pt}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(4.38)

The map
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{uu_{1}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
(resp.,
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{uu_{1}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
) is equal to
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ(\zeta_{uu_{1}}\circ\kappa_{uu_{1}})
(resp.,
\zeta_{uu_{1}}\circ\kappa_{uu_{1}}
), and its restriction on
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime},\{u,uu_{1}\}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is equal to
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ(\zeta_{uu_{1}}\circ\kappa_{uu_{1}})=t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ(\zeta_{u}\circ\kappa_{u})
(resp.,
\zeta_{uu_{1}}\circ\kappa_{uu_{1}}=\zeta_{u}\circ\kappa_{u}
) by above diagram. Since

{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)=\oplus_{j=1}^{s-1}{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{\widehat{t_{j}^{u}}},g^{\prime}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{Z}}(L),E),

we complete the proof.
∎

Similar to the argument below
[
18
, Lemma 3.23]
, let
\pi^{-,\#}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
be the tautological extension of
\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and let
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
be the unique quotient of
\pi^{-,\#}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. Similar to
[
18
, Corollary 3.25]
, we have a bijection
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Im}(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
.

Remark 4.14
.

The induction method only defines the map
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\circ}
on
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
. By definition, we have
\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)=0
for any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. By the reconstruction
\&
reinterpretation of
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
in Section
4.4
, the map
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
can be extended to the whole extension group
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
. We describe the image of
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\circ}
more explicitly in Proposition
4.25
.

Proposition 4.15
.

The map
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\circ}
restricts to a map

t_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}^{\circ}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}}).\;

Moreover, the following diagram commutes (recall (
4.30
) and Proposition
3.9
)

\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{r}{\rm Ext}^{1}(Q_{\Delta_{n_{i}}}^{\diamond}((I_{0}\backslash S_{0})\cap\Delta_{I,i},\emptyset,\lambda^{i}),\pi_{1}((\underline{x}^{u})^{(i)},{\mathbf{h}}^{i}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 50.0pt(t^{\circ}_{M_{u,I,j}})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}}
\textstyle{\prod_{i=1}^{r}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}(M_{u,I,j},M_{u,I,j})}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}^{\circ}}
\textstyle{\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}}

(4.39)

In particular,
\zeta_{\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}
induces a natural isomorphism

\oplus_{i=1}^{r}\ker(t^{\circ}_{M_{u,I,i}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\ker(t_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}_{u,I}}^{\circ})=\ker(t_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\circ})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}_{I,u}}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

Let
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. The map
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
restricts to a map

\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
.

Corollary 4.16
.

The map
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
factors through
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}
, and there exists a unique map

t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}:\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

such that
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}=t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}\circ\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}
.

Similar to
[
18
, Remark 3.30, Corollary 3.31]
, let
\pi^{-,\#}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
be the extension of
\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},{\sigma}})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and let
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
be the unique quotient of
\pi^{-,\#}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
.

Corollary 4.17
.

We have
\oplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},{\sigma}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})=\bigoplus^{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}_{\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
.

Remark 4.18
.

The same results hold by replacing
{\mathbf{D}}
with
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
. Define (see (
3.19
) for the map
\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}
)

t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}:=\mathscr{T}_{\sigma}\circ t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},{\sigma}}:\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}).\;

(4.40)

Then the map
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1,\circ}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})
factors through
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}}
, and
g^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}=t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}\circ\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
, where
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
is the composition (see Remark
4.11
):

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\zeta_{u,\sigma}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

(4.41)

In this case, we have
\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}})=\ker(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},{\sigma}})
. The
\sigma
-analytic version for Proposition
4.15
holds by replacing
{\mathbf{D}}
(resp.,
M_{u,I,j}
) with
D_{\sigma}
(resp.,
(M_{u,I,j})_{\sigma}
), etc.

4.4
Reconstructions and extensions of the map
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
and its property

Throughout this section, fix
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
. This section follows the route in the recent work
[
8
, Section 2.4]
of Breuil-Ding and aims to describe explicitly the image of
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}
(resp.,
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
) and the information of Hodge parameters which the kernel of
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},{\sigma}}
(resp.,
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
) determines.

See Section
3.3
for the spaces
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
and
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
. Recall that we have fixed a basis
\{e_{1,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{1}},\cdots,\{e_{s,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{s}}
(and we rewrite them with
e_{1,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n,\sigma}
by using lexicographical ordering) of
D_{\sigma}
.

Recall
\Delta^{\prime}=\cup_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}
and
\mathcal{I}_{i}=\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:i\in\Delta\backslash S_{0}^{u}\}
for
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
. In particular, if
S_{0}=\emptyset
, then
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta
and
\mathcal{I}_{i}\cong\{(a_{1},\cdots,a_{i}):\;a_{j}\in\{1,\cdots,n\},\;1\leq j\leq i\}
. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, we define
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}^{i}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
by

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}^{i}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):=\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):\exists\;a,b\in E\;\text{such that\;}f(e_{(u^{\sharp})^{-1}(j),\sigma})=ae_{(u^{\sharp})^{-1}(j),\sigma},\right.

(4.42)

\displaystyle\left.\forall\;1\leq j\leq i,f(e_{(u^{\sharp})^{-1}(j),\sigma})-be_{(u^{\sharp})^{-1}(j),\sigma}\in\oplus_{l=1}^{i}Ee_{(u^{\sharp})^{-1}(l),\sigma},\forall\;i+1\leq j\leq n\right\},

where
u^{\sharp}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
is the unique element sending
e_{i,j,\sigma}
for
1\leq j\leq r_{i}
to
e_{u^{-1}(i),j,\sigma}
for
1\leq j\leq r_{i}
, which thus induces a permutation on
e_{1,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n,\sigma}
.

Keep the notation in Section
3.3
. Then we have (note that
\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(i)},\sigma}\subseteq\tau_{S_{0},\sigma}\subseteq{\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma}
, see Remark
3.18
)

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(i)},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}).\;

(4.43)

As in
[
8
, (134),(137)]
, sending
f
to
(a\log_{p,\sigma},b\log_{p,\sigma})
(
a,b
are given in the definition (
4.42
)) induces a canonical map

f_{i,\sigma}:{\rm Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E),

which is an isomorphism by comparing dimensions. Indeed, we have (see Remark
3.18
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(i)},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{z}}_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(i)},\sigma}),

(4.44)

and thus
\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})=2
. Consider the following composition of isomorphisms (similar to
[
8
, (142)-(144)]
):

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus_{{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)}{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(L),E)

(4.45)

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\oplus_{\sigma}f_{i,\sigma}^{-1}]{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

Put

{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}):=\sum_{i\in\Delta^{\prime},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}{\rm Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

(4.46)

When
(\ref{tDmapforiusigma})
are taken over all the
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, we get

Proposition 4.19
.

There is a surjection of finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces which only depends on on a choice of
\log_{p}(p)\in E
(recall the representation
\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
in Remark
4.11
):

\displaystyle t^{\circ}_{D_{\sigma}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).

(4.47)

We have

Proposition 4.20
.

{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\cong\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
.

Proof.

It suffices to study the envelope
\sum_{i\in\Delta^{\prime},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(i)},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
in
\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
. The left hand side equals to

\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq s}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(t^{u}_{j})},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
. By (
3.39
), we have
\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(t^{u}_{j})},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{z}}_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(t^{u}_{j})},\sigma})

for some
b_{\sigma}(u)\in{\mathbf{B}}(E)
. Therefore, we deduce that

\sum_{1\leq j\leq s}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{\widehat{u^{\sharp}(t^{u}_{j})},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\sum_{1\leq j\leq s}\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{z}}_{\widehat{t^{u}_{j}},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{ub_{\sigma}(u)}({\mathfrak{z}}^{u}_{S_{0},\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}(\tau_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}),

so that
{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})_{S_{0}}^{\circ}
.
∎

We end this section with the third consideration for the reconstruction of the map
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma},\sigma}
(see
[
8
, Section 2.2]
). For
J\subseteq\{1,\cdots,n\}
, put

e_{J}:=\wedge_{j\in J}e_{j,\sigma}\in\bigwedge_{E}\nolimits^{\!|J|}\!D_{\sigma}.

For
1\leq i\leq s
, we put
I_{i}:=\{q:e_{q}=e_{i,j,\sigma},1\leq j\leq r_{i}\}
. Put

{\mathbf{e}}_{i}:=\wedge_{j=1}^{r_{i}}e_{i,j,\sigma}=\wedge_{t\in I_{i}}e_{t}=e_{I_{i}}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!r_{i}}\!D^{i}_{\sigma}.\;

For
I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,s\}
, put
t_{I}:=\sum_{i\in I}r_{i}
and

{\mathbf{e}}_{I}:=\wedge_{i\in I}{\mathbf{e}}_{i}\in\bigwedge_{E}\nolimits^{\!t_{I}}\!D_{\sigma}.\;

For
i\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, let
I_{u,i}=\{u(1),\cdots,u(l_{i}(u))\}
(recall that
1\leq l_{i}(u)\leq s
is the integer with the property
t_{l_{i}(u)}^{u}=i
) and
I_{u,i}^{c}=\{1,\cdots,s\}\backslash I_{u,i}
. For each
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, we fix an
isomorphism of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces (as in
[

, (43)]
):

\epsilon_{u,i}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}(C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))\xrightarrow{\sim}E{\mathbf{e}}_{I_{u,i}^{c}}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}.

(4.48)

Then we get for each
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
a map:

\epsilon_{i}:=\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}\epsilon_{u,i}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}.\;

Let
\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}
be the image of
\epsilon_{i}
and let
\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{c}
be its complement in
\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}D_{\sigma}
. The latter is generated by
\{e_{J}:=\wedge_{j\in J}e_{j,\sigma}:J\subseteq\{1,\cdots,n\},|J|=n-i,e_{J}\neq{\mathbf{e}}_{I_{u,i}^{c}},\;\forall u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}\}
. Then
\dim_{E}\big(\bigwedge_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}=|\mathcal{I}_{i}|
. For
1\leq i\leq n
, we define the following
E
-line of
\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}D_{\sigma}
:

\displaystyle{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})

\displaystyle:={\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-1,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\wedge{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-2,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\wedge\cdots\wedge{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})

(4.49)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!{\rm Fil}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\subseteq\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}.\;

The non-critical assumption implies that the coefficient of
{\mathbf{e}}_{I_{u,i}^{c}}
(for each
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
) in
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})
is non-zero. Sending

to zero induces an isomorphism

{\rm pr}^{\flat}:{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat},

(4.50)

i.e.,

is the image of
