. Similar to
[
8
, (46)]
, we consider the morphisms of
E
-vector spaces:

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\otimes{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)^{\vee}

(4.51)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\otimes{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big),\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\otimes_{E}{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big),

where the second morphism is the push-forward induced by the composition

{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\hookrightarrow\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}\xrightarrow{\epsilon_{i}^{-1}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big).

Let
\pi^{\flat}_{s_{i}}(D_{\sigma})
(resp.,
\pi^{\flat}_{s_{i},u}(D_{\sigma})
) be a representative of the image for the canonical vector of the first term of (
4.51
) by the composition (
4.51
), which lies in the extension group

We have injection

and a canonical isomorphism

For
S\subseteq\Delta^{\prime}
, put

\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\bigoplus_{i\in S,\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}\pi^{\flat}_{s_{i}}(D_{\sigma}).

Proposition 4.21
.

There is a surjection of finite dimensional
E
-vector spaces which only depends on the
(\epsilon_{u,i})_{i\in\Delta^{\prime},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and on a choice of

t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\sharp}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).

See the proof for the precise definition of the subspace
{\rm Hom}^{\sharp}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\subseteq{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
.

Proof.

We define
t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}
in restriction to
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))
firstly. This is essentially the Step 2 in the proof of
[

, Proposition 2.2.4]
. It suffices to use
[
8
, (63)]
and replace

8
, (64) & (65)]
with

\displaystyle t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}|_{{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S^{-}_{0}}(L),E)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{g}(E_{i}[1/t],E_{i}[1/t])

(4.52)

\displaystyle(\psi_{1},\cdots,\psi_{s})

\displaystyle\mapsto(E_{s-i}[1/t]\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2},L}(1+\psi_{s-i}(\varpi_{L})\epsilon)_{1\leq i\leq s}.

The argument Step 3 in
[
8
, Proposition 2.2.4]
need more argument. Choose
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
. We have isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{s_{i}}({\mathbf{D}})/\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\otimes_{E}{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big)

(4.53)

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma}\Big)\otimes_{E}{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[(\ref{dualpairing})]{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u,\sigma},\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)^{\vee}\otimes_{E}{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big).

The last space can be viewed as the subspace of
{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma},\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\Big)
of maps which vanish on

. Recall the composition of the surjections in

, (68) & (69)]

, (69)]

\displaystyle g_{i,\sigma}:{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma},

\displaystyle\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\Big)\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-1}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\wedge D_{\sigma},\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\Big)

(4.54)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{E}\big(D_{\sigma},{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\big):f|_{{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})}\text{scalar}\right\}\hookrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}\big(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}\big),

the third inclusion is given by
[
8
, Lemma 2.2.5 (ii)]
. By

8
, Lemma 2.2.5 (ii)]
, we have a surjection

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{E}\big(D_{\sigma},{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\big):f|_{{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})}\text{scalar}\right\}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).

∎

Remark 4.22
.

Similar to
[
8
, Proposition 2.2.7]
, we can also show that
t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}
does not depends on the choices of
(\epsilon_{u,i})_{i\in\Delta^{\prime},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
.

We next compare
{\rm Hom}^{\sharp}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
with
{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
.

Lemma 4.23
.

For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, define
D_{\sigma}^{(i)}:=E\langle e_{t}:t\not\in I_{j},\forall\;j\in I^{c}_{u,i}\text{\;and\;}u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}\rangle
and
D_{\sigma,(i)}:=D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{(i)}
. Thus,

D_{\sigma,(i)}:=E\langle e_{u^{-1}(i),j,\sigma}:l_{i}(u)+1\leq i\leq s,1\leq j\leq r_{u^{-1}(i)},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}\rangle\subseteq D_{\sigma},

and
D_{\sigma}=D_{\sigma}^{(i)}\oplus D_{\sigma,(i)}
. We have

g_{i,\sigma}\Big({\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big)\Big)=\left\{f\in{\rm Hom}_{E}\big(D_{\sigma,(i)},{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\big):f|_{{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})}\text{scalar\;}\right\}.

Proof.

For each
e_{t}\in D_{\sigma}^{(i)}
, we have
x\wedge e_{t}\notin\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}
for any
x\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-1}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})
, then
f
vanishes on
D_{\sigma}^{(i)}
by definition. On the other hand, let
f
be a map in the right hand side subspace, there exists
g\in{\rm Hom}_{E}\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma},\bigwedge_{E}^{n-i}{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\big)
such that
f_{i}(g)=f
, then
f_{i}\big(g|_{\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}}\big)=f
.
∎

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
. The composition (given in (
4.45
))

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)\xrightarrow[f_{i,\sigma}^{-1}]{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{i}_{{\rm Fil},{\mathbf{F}}_{u}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})

(4.56)

coincides with the composition

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E)\oplus{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\mathbf{L}}_{\widehat{i}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\right)

(4.57)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\sharp}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma}).\;

In particular, we have
{\rm Hom}^{\sharp}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\cong{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
8
, Proposition 2.5.4]
. Only need to show that the key statement of
[
8
, Lemma 2.2.9 (iii)]
in the last paragraph of the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 2.5.4]
is also suitable for our setting. Keep the notation in the proof of
[
8
, Proposition 2.5.4]
. For
c_{u}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),C_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
, we claim that:

(1)

For
t\in I_{j}
and
j\in I_{u,i}
,
t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(c_{u})(e_{t})=0
.

(2)

For
t\in I_{j}
and
j\not\in I_{u,i}
, there exists a unique
\lambda_{u}\in E
such that
c_{u,i}=\lambda_{u,i}\log
where
\log_{p,\sigma}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),V_{i,u,\sigma}\right)
is the image of
\log_{p,\sigma}\in{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}({\rm GL}_{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{L}),E)
(see Remark
4.8
) and

t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(c_{u})(e_{t})-\lambda_{u,i}e_{t}\in\bigoplus_{t^{\prime}\in I_{q},q\in I_{u,i}}Ee_{t^{\prime}}

The argument in
[
8
, Page 33]
is still true for our setting, when we replace
\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}
(resp.,
e_{I^{c}}
, resp,
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})
, resp.,
C(I)
) with
\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{e}}_{I_{u,i}^{c}}
, resp.,
{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}
, resp.,
C_{i,u,\sigma}
). For any
t\in I_{j}
with
j\in I_{u,c}^{c}
, we also have
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})^{(t)}=n-i-1
and for any non-zero
x\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-1}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})^{(t)}
, we have

x\wedge e_{t}\notin\Big(\bigoplus_{J\neq I_{u,c}^{c}}E{\mathbf{e}}_{J}\Big)\oplus\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i,c}\!D_{\sigma},

which implies that
x\wedge e_{t}\notin\bigoplus_{J\neq I_{u,c}^{c}}E{\mathbf{e}}_{J}
. Note that

e^{\star}_{I_{u,i}^{c}}(x\wedge e_{t})-x\wedge e_{t}\in\Big(\bigoplus_{J\neq I_{u,c}^{c}}E{\mathbf{e}}_{J}\Big)\oplus\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i,c}\!D_{\sigma}.

By definition of
t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(\log_{p,\sigma})
, this deduces that
x\wedge(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(\log_{p,\sigma})(e_{t})-e_{t})\in\Big(\bigoplus_{J\neq I_{u,c}^{c}}E{\mathbf{e}}_{J}\Big)
, and thus

t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(c_{u})(e_{t})-e_{t}\in\bigoplus_{t^{\prime}\in I_{q},q\neq j}Ee_{t^{\prime}}

(4.58)

It remains to show that the coefficient of
e_{j^{\prime}}
in the right hand side is zero, if
t^{\prime}\in I_{q}
and
q\not\in I_{u,i}
. Indeed, choose the same elements
\{f_{t^{\prime\prime}}:{t^{\prime\prime}}\in I_{j},j\in I_{u,c}^{c}\}
as in
[
8
, (84)]
, which form a basis of
{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})
. Suppose that the coefficient of
{\mathbf{e}}_{t^{\prime}}
in the right hand side is non-zero. For any
t^{\prime}\neq t
, we also see that the coefficient of
{\mathbf{e}}_{I^{c}_{u,c}}
is zero in

\big(\wedge_{t^{\prime\prime}\neq t^{\prime}}f_{t^{\prime\prime}}\big)\wedge e_{t}\in\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i-1}\!{\rm Fil}_{H}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\wedge D_{\sigma}.

But
\big(\wedge_{t^{\prime\prime}\neq t^{\prime}}f_{t^{\prime\prime}}\big)\wedge t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}(\log_{p,\sigma})(e_{t})=0
in
\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D_{\sigma}
and
e_{t^{\prime}}
cannot appears in
f_{t^{\prime\prime}}
if
t^{\prime\prime}\neq t^{\prime}
, then by (
4.58
), the coefficient of
{\mathbf{e}}_{I^{c}_{u,c}}
in
\big(\wedge_{t^{\prime\prime}\neq t^{\prime}}f_{t^{\prime\prime}}\big)\wedge e_{t}
must be non-zero, this is impossible. This completes the proof.
∎

The same strategy as in
[
8
, Proposition 2.5.5, Corollary 2.5.6]
gives:

Proposition 4.25
.

Under the splitting in Proposition
3.16
, for
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
,
t^{\circ}_{D_{\sigma}}|_{\mathrm{Im}(\gamma_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}^{\circ})}
) coincides with
f^{\circ}_{\sigma}\circ t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}
, see (
3.34
) for the isomorphism
f^{\circ}_{\sigma}
). The same results hold when
{\mathbf{D}}
is replaced by
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}(t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma})=(f^{\circ}_{\sigma})^{-1}\left({\rm Ext}^{1,\circ}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})\right).

(4.59)

Let
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
be the unique quotient of the tautological extension of
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\pi_{1,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. Then
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
is isomorphic to
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
.

Remark 4.26
.

Similar to
[
8
, Corollary 2.2.13]
, we can show that
\pi^{-,\sharp}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
does not depends on any choices of
(\epsilon_{u,i})_{i\in\Delta^{\prime},u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}
and
\log_{p}(p)
.

For
S\subseteq\Delta^{\prime}
, we have the following composition:

\displaystyle{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

(4.60)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{i\in S}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{s_{i}}(D_{\sigma})/\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

\displaystyle\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{s_{i}}(D_{\sigma})/\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big).\;

The remainder this section describe what information of
p
-adic Hodge parameters is determined by the kernel of
t^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}
(equivalently,
t^{\circ}_{D_{\sigma}}
or
t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}
or
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
).

Proposition 4.27
.

Let
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma,\mathrm{inf}}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)
be the subspace of
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)
of locally
\sigma
-analytic extensions with an infinitesimal characters. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, we have:

(1)

The image under (
4.60
) of the subspace
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma,\mathrm{inf}}\Big(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)
is

{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}/{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big).

(2)

The image under (
4.60
) of the subspace
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}}|_{{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G,\sigma,\mathrm{inf}}(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{S,\sigma}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))})
is

{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!W_{\sigma}\big)^{\flat}/{\rm Fil}_{S,i}^{2^{\mathrm{nd}}-\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big).

See
[
8
, (97)]
for the precise definition of
{\rm Fil}_{S,i}^{2^{\mathrm{nd}}-\max}(D_{\sigma})
.

Let
\Delta^{\prime}:=\{i_{1}<i_{2}<\cdots<i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|}\}
. In precise, for
0\leq i\leq n-1
, write
{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{n-i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})=\oplus_{l=n-i}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{l}
for some
E
-line
\mathcal{L}_{l}\in E\langle e_{l,\sigma},e_{l-1,\sigma},\cdots,e_{1,\sigma}\rangle
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{i}
be the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{(i)}=D_{\sigma,(i)}
.

Theorem 4.28
.

\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})
determines
\{\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{i}\}_{i\in\Delta^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Similar to the Step 2 of the proof of
[
8
, 2.3.10]
, the following data is determined by
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})
:

{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|}}\!W^{\flat}_{\sigma}/{\rm Fil}_{\Delta^{\prime}_{j},_{|\Delta^{\prime}|}}^{2^{\mathrm{nd}}-\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat},{\rm Fil}_{i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|}}^{\max}(D_{\sigma})^{\flat}\Big),

for
0\leq j\leq|\Delta^{\prime}|-1
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{j}:=\{i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{j},i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|}\}
. By Lemma
4.23
,
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})
determines the data of:

\displaystyle\left\{{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(D_{\sigma}/(D_{\sigma}^{(i_{j})}+{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{j},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})),{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\Big)\right\}_{1\leq j\leq|\Delta^{\prime}|-1}

(4.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{{\rm Hom}_{E}\Big(D_{\sigma,(i)}/{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{j},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})_{(i_{j})},{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{|\Delta^{\prime}|},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})\Big)\right\}_{1\leq j\leq|\Delta^{\prime}|-1}.

where
{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{j},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})_{(i_{j})}
is the image of
{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i_{j},\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})
in
D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{(i_{j})}=D_{\sigma,(i_{j})}
. Therefore,
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})
determines
\{{\rm Fil}_{H,\sigma}^{-{\mathbf{h}}_{i,\sigma}}(D_{\sigma})_{(i)}\}_{i\in\Delta^{\prime}}
. In particular,
\ker(t^{\circ,\sharp}_{D_{\sigma}})
determines
\{\mathcal{L}^{\flat}_{i}\}_{i\in\Delta^{\prime}}
.
∎

Example 4.29
.

A typical example is
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{0}}\cong\GL_{1}^{\oplus(n-m)}\times{\rm GL}_{m}
. As
E
-vector spaces,
D_{\sigma}=D_{\sigma}^{1}\oplus\cdots D_{\sigma}^{n-m}\oplus D_{\sigma}^{n-m+1}
with
\dim_{E}D_{\sigma}^{j}=1
for
1\leq j\leq n-m
and
\dim_{E}D_{\sigma}^{n-m+1}=m
. For
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
, we have

D_{\sigma,(i)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{n-m+1},&n-i<m\\
D_{\sigma},&n-i\geq m\end{array}\right.

(4.62)

We divide into two cases.

(1)

m\leq n-m
. In this case,
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta
. For
n-i\geq m
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\in E\langle e_{1,\sigma},e_{2,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n-m,\sigma}\rangle
. If
n-i<m
,
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}
is equal to the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{n-m+1}
, so that
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}\in E\langle e_{1,\sigma},e_{2,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n-m,\sigma}\rangle
.

(2)

m>n-m
. In this case,
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta\backslash\{n-m+1,\cdots,m-1\}
. For
n-i\geq m
(so that
i\leq n-m
and
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
), we have
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}=\mathcal{L}_{i}\in E\langle e_{1,\sigma},e_{2,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n-m,\sigma}\rangle
. If
i>m-1
(so that
i>n-m
and thus
n-i<m
),
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\flat}\in E\langle e_{1,\sigma},e_{2,\sigma},\cdots,e_{n-m,\sigma}\rangle
is equal to the image of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
D_{\sigma}/D_{\sigma}^{n-m+1}
.

We end this section with a relationship between our discussion with the functors constructed in
[
11
]
. Keep the notation in
[
11
, Conjecture 5.3.1]
and assume that
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Put
i\in\Delta^{\prime}
and set
\alpha_{i}=e_{i}-e_{i+1}
. Put

(D(\wedge_{E}^{n-i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}))^{\flat}:=\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}\wedge_{i\in I^{c}_{u,i}}(\wedge_{E}^{r_{i}}\Delta_{E_{i}}),

which is a
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-submodule of the
p
-adic differential equation
D(\wedge_{E}^{n-i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}})
associated to the Weil-Deligne representation
\wedge_{E}^{n-i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. By
[
11
, Theorem 1.3]
, for
u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}
, we obtain (our
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{n})
is the
``-\lambda"
in the definition before
[
11
, (216)]
)

\displaystyle F_{\alpha_{i}}(C_{i,u})

\displaystyle\simeq E_{\infty}(\chi_{\lambda})\otimes_{E}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}((-\lambda)\circ\lambda_{\alpha^{\vee}_{i}})/t^{1-\langle-\lambda,\alpha^{\vee}_{i}\rangle},-\Big),

(4.63)

\displaystyle\simeq E_{\infty}(\chi_{\lambda})\otimes_{E}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},-\Big)

where
\lambda_{\alpha^{\vee}_{i}}(x)=\diag(x,\cdots,x,1,\cdots,1)
(the number of
x
is
i
) for
x\in{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. Moreover, put (we refer to
[
11
, (217)]
)

\displaystyle D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{?}:=(D(\wedge_{E}^{n-i}{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}))^{?}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}((-s_{i}\cdot(-\lambda))\circ\lambda_{\alpha^{\vee}_{i}})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E}}\mathcal{R}_{E}(|\cdot|^{\sum_{j=n-i}^{n-1}}\chi^{-1}_{{\textbf{r}}_{{\mathbf{D}}}})

(4.64)

for
?\in\{\emptyset,\flat\}
. Note that
D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{\flat}\hookrightarrow D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
, which induces an injection

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{\flat}\Big)\hookrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big).

We have a natural isomorphism

{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D
by
[
11
, Remark 5.1.3, (218)]
. This map identities
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{\flat}\Big)
with the subspace
\big(\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D\big)^{\flat}
of
\bigwedge\nolimits_{E}^{\!n-i}\!D
exactly. Put
\pi^{\alpha_{i},\flat}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\bigoplus_{u\in\mathcal{I}_{i}}C_{i,u}
, we deduce from
[
11
, Theorem 1.3]
that

\displaystyle F_{\alpha_{i}}(\pi^{\alpha_{i},\flat}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))

\displaystyle\simeq E_{\infty}(\chi_{\lambda})\otimes_{E}{\rm Hom}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{\flat},-\Big)

(4.65)

and obtain an isomorphism:

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha_{i}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\Big(\pi^{\alpha_{i},\flat}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}\Big(\mathcal{R}_{E}(z^{-\sum_{j=1}^{i}\lambda_{i}})/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{i}-{\mathbf{h}}_{i+1}},D_{\alpha_{i}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{\flat}\Big).\;

(4.66)

4.5
Restriction to crystabelline case (without the generic assumption)

The remainder of this section restrict all above discussion to crystabelline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules (where we do not assume the generic assumption), i.e.,
S_{0}=\emptyset
(note that
I_{0}^{\prime}=I_{0}
in this case). For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we have a triangulation
\mathcal{F}_{u}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Corollary 4.30
.

(Infinite fern for crystabelline case) Assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is crystabelline. For
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
, the natural maps

g_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\star,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\star}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
\overline{g}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\star,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}_{\star}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
are sujective.

Proof.

We have two ways to prove these results. Firstly, we apply Remark
3.33
. When
r_{s}=1
, we get that
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\geq 1+d_{L}n^{2}
, so that
{\rm Ext}^{1,\circ,+}_{\mathcal{F}+\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})={\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
\dim_{E}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\cap{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})=1+(n^{2}-2n+2)d_{L}
. We prove it by induction on the rank
n=\rk({\mathbf{D}})
. Supposet that
g_{M}
and
g_{N}
(and thus
\overline{g}_{M}
and
\overline{g}_{N}
) are surjective. We deduce from Proposition
3.27
that the natural map
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n},u(n)=n}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n},w(n)=1}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
are surjective, thus we obtain the surjectivity of the two maps
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n},u(n)=n}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n},w(n)=1}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
, now the induction step follows from the fact
{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\oplus{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Secondly, we can apply Theorem
6.5
to
S_{0}=\emptyset
directly. The

\sigma
-version is similar to
[
18
, Corollary 2.40]
or apply
Remark
6.6
to
S_{0}=\emptyset
.
∎

The results in
[
18
, Corollays 2.31, 2.34 ,2.36 and 2.38]
also hold (see
(3)
of Lemma
3.29
).

Corollary 4.31
.

For
\star\in\{\emptyset,\sigma\}
,
\mathscr{J}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\star}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{L}({\mathbf{D}}_{\star},M_{\star},N_{\star})
) determines the
E
-line
E[\iota_{{\mathbf{D}}_{\star}}]\subseteq{\rm Hom}(M_{\star},N_{\star})
each other.

In this case,
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{0},\lambda)
and
\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0},\emptyset,\lambda)
. We obtain a locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and a short exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\oplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in\Delta,u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}^{\widehat{j},\emptyset}\\
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\end{subarray}}C_{j,u}\rightarrow 0.\;

Moreover,
{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))=n+(2^{n}-1)d_{L}
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we have a morphism

\zeta_{u}:{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}(L),E)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
. Thus the composition

\displaystyle\gamma_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}{\rm Hom}({\mathbf{T}}(L),E)\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}\zeta_{u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).

Theorem 4.32
.

We have the following facts.

(1)

The surjection
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
factors through
\gamma_{{\mathbf{D}}}
and there exists a unique surjection
t_{{\mathbf{D}}}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(I_{0},\emptyset,\lambda),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\twoheadrightarrow\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})

such that
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}=t_{{\mathbf{D}}}\circ\gamma_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. Similar results (and its
\sigma
-de Rham version) holds for
{\mathbf{D}}
and
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
.

(2)

\dim_{E}\ker(t_{{\mathbf{D}}})=2^{n}-\frac{n(n+1)}{2}d_{L}-1
.

(3)

For
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}
,
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})_{\sigma}
determines
{\mathbf{D}}_{\sigma}
. In particular, if
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
,
\pi^{-}_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})
determines
{\mathbf{D}}
.

Proof.

Part
(1)
is direct consequence of Theorem
4.13
and Corollary
4.30
. Part
(3)
is the same as
[
18
, Theorem 3.34]
, all the references in the proof of
[
18
, Theorem 3.34]
are discussed for general case (i.e., without the generic assumption on
{\mathbf{D}}
) in Section
4.3
and Corollary
4.31
. Indeed, it is also a direct consequence of Theorem
4.28
. Part
(2)
follows from Proposition
4.25
and the fact that
{\rm Hom}^{\flat}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})={\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D_{\sigma},D_{\sigma})
.
∎

4.6
Universal extensions

Let
R_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the universal deformation ring of deformations of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. For
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, let
R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
be the universal deformation ring that pro-represents the functor
\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbf{D}},\mathcal{F}_{u}}^{?}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
, and let
R_{{\mathbf{D}},g}
be the universal deformation ring of de Rham deformations. Let
I^{?}_{u}
(resp.,
I_{g}
) be the kernel of
R_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
(resp.,
R_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow R_{{\mathbf{D}},g}
). Put
R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}=R_{{\mathbf{D}}}/(I_{g}+I^{?}_{u})
. Note that
R_{{\mathbf{D}},1,g}=R_{{\mathbf{D}},g}
. We have natural surjections
R_{{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}\rightarrow R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}
. For a continuous character
\delta
of
{\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)
, denote by the
R_{S_{0}^{u},\delta}
(resp.
R_{S_{0}^{u},\delta,g}
) the universal deformations ring (resp., the universal deformations ring of locally algebraic deformations) of
\delta
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. We have a natural surjection
R_{S_{0}^{u},\delta}\twoheadrightarrow R_{S_{0}^{u},\delta,g}
.

For a complete local Noetherian
E
-algebra
R
, we use
{\mathfrak{m}}_{R}
(or use
{\mathfrak{m}}
for simplicity when it does not cause confusion) to denote its maximal ideal. Then for
\ast\in\{\emptyset,g,u\}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, we have
({\mathfrak{m}}_{R_{{\mathbf{D}},\ast}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R_{{\mathbf{D}},\ast}})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
and
({\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},\ast}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},\ast}})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Ext}^{1,?}_{\ast}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
. Moreover,
({\mathfrak{m}}_{S_{0}^{u},\delta}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{S_{0}^{u},\delta})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}({{\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)},E)
and
({\mathfrak{m}}_{S_{0}^{u},\delta,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{S_{0}^{u},\delta,g})^{\vee}\cong{\rm Hom}_{\mathrm{sm}}({{\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)},E)
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we have the following commutative Cartesian diagram over
{\rm Art}_{E}
(for
?\in\{\circ,0\}
):

\textstyle{R_{S_{0}^{u},1}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{?}_{u}}
\textstyle{R_{S_{0}^{u},1,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{?}_{u}}
\textstyle{R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}.}

(4.67)

Let
\mathcal{H}_{u}
be the Bernstein centre over
E
associated to
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{u}(\underline{x})=\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\delta_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)}^{{1}/{2}}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\Big)^{\infty}
, and let
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{u}
be the completion of
\mathcal{H}_{u}
at
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{u}(\underline{x})
. We have isomorphism

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{u}\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{S_{0}^{u},1,g}
by sending a smooth deformation
\chi
of
z^{{\mathbf{h}}}
to
\Big({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\chi\Big)^{\infty}
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, put

A^{?}_{0,u}:=R_{S_{0}^{u},1,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\rightarrow R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
and
A^{?}_{0}:=A^{\ast}_{0,1}
. For
?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}
, let

A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\times_{R_{{\mathbf{D}},1,g}}A^{?}_{0},\text{\;and\;}A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}:=R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\times_{R_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}}A^{?}_{0,u}.\;

Then the tangent space of
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
(resp.,
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
) is naturally isomorphism to
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
(resp.,
\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,?}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
), and the tangent space of
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
is naturally isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)
. Let
\mathcal{I}^{?}_{u}
be the kernel of the natural morphism
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
. Let
\mathcal{I}^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
be the kernel of the natural morphism
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\rightarrow\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
, then we get an injection
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=A_{{\mathbf{D}}}/\mathcal{I}^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\hookrightarrow\prod_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
(In particular, if
S_{0}=\emptyset
, the infinite fern deduces that
I^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}=0
).

We next introduce some universal extensions. Let
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
(resp.,
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
) be the universal extension of
\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
(resp.,
\zeta_{u}\big({\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)\big)\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
) by
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
.

Let
\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
be the universal extension of
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\otimes_{E}\delta_{u}
by
\delta_{u}
, so that

\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is isomorphic to an extension of
(\widetilde{\delta}_{u})^{\oplus d_{u}}
by
\widetilde{\delta}_{u}
, where
d_{u}:=\dim_{E}{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)
. Similar to the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 3.35]
, the induced representation
I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is the universal extension of
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\otimes\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. Composed the above universal extensions with the pull-back map for the natural map

p_{u}:\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{\sharp}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{u}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
, we get a locally analytic representation
\big(I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\big)^{\flat}
which is the universal extension of
{\rm Hom}_{u}({\mathbf{Z}}_{S_{0}}^{u}(L),E)\otimes\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{\sharp}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
by
\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
.

Let
U_{u}
be the intersection of
I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
with the kernel of the projection
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
, then we have hence isomorphisms of
G
-representations

\displaystyle\left(\big(I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\big)^{\flat}/U_{u}\right)\oplus_{\mathrm{ST}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})}\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}.\;

(4.68)

For
?\in\{\circ,0\}
, we have a natural action of
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}\hookrightarrow R_{S_{0}^{u},1}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}
on
\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
where
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}\cong{\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)^{\vee}
acts via

x:\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)\otimes_{E}\delta_{u}\xrightarrow{x}\delta_{u}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
. Similarity,
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
admits an
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
-action, which is given by

x:\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ}}\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{u}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\otimes\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\xrightarrow{x}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\twoheadrightarrow Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ}},

where
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}\cong{\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\zeta_{u}}\big(\zeta_{u}\big({\rm Hom}_{u}\big({\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),E\big)\big)^{\vee}
. Moreover, the injection
I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}/U_{u}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
-equivalent.

Theorem 4.33
.

For
?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}
, there is a unique
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action (the
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action via the natural surjections
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow A^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow A^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
) on
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
such that for all
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we have an
A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}\times G
-equivariant injection

\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}[\mathcal{I}^{?}_{u}].

Proof.

By Theorem
4.13
, we define an
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action on
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
by letting
x\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}\cong\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})^{\vee}
act via

x:\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}})^{\vee}\otimes_{E}\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}.\;

By definition, this
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action factors through
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow A^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}}}\twoheadrightarrow A^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
. This action satisfies the property in the theorem. The uniqueness follows from the fact that
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
is generated by
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
.
∎

5
Local-global compatibility

5.1
Patched Bernstein eigenvarieties and Bernstein parabolic varieties

In this section, we recall briefly the patched Bernstein eigenvariety and Bernstein paraboline variety of Breuil-Ding (see
[
6
, Section 3.3, Section 4.2]
).

We follow the notation of
[
19
, Section 4.1.1]
and
[
16
, Section 2]
. We have a patched Galois deformation ring
R_{\infty}=R_{\infty}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}\widehat{\otimes}{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
, where
{\mathfrak{p}}
is a
p
-adic place over a totally real subfield
F^{+}
,
L:=F^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
, and
\overline{r}:{\rm Gal}_{L}\rightarrow{\rm GL}_{n}(k_{E})
is a continuous representation and
{R_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}}
is the maximal reduced and
p
-torsion free quotient of the universal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lifting ring of
\overline{r}
. We have a
R_{\infty}
-admissible unitary representation
\Pi_{\infty}
(i.e., the so-called patched Banach representation) of
G
over
E
. We refer to
loc.cit
for detail. Let
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}
be the subrepresentation of
G
of locally
R_{\infty}
-analytic vectors of
\Pi_{\infty}
(see
[
13
, Section 3.1]
).

As in Section
2.2
, for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
, we fix a cuspidal Bernstein component
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
of the Levi subgroup
{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}
. Let
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,1},{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,2},\cdots,{{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,n})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
be a strictly
S_{0}
-dominant weight. We put
{{\mathbf{h}}}_{i}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i})_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
and put
{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{\tau,i}+i-1)_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L},1\leq i\leq n}={\mathbf{h}}-\theta
. See Proposition 3.3.2, Corollary  3.3.3, Proposition 3.3.4, Theorem 3.3.5 and Proposition 3.3.6 in
[
6
]
(see also
[
25
, Proposition 4.1]
for the same patching setup) for the detail of the following definitions. Let

\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}

be the patched Bernstein eigenvariety associated to
\Pi_{\infty}
and
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
, which is defined as the Zariski-closed support of certain coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}
over
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. We have

\Gamma\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}},\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}\Big)\cong B_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})^{\vee}.

In particular, for
x=({\mathfrak{m}}_{x},\pi_{x},\chi_{x})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
,
x\in\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
iff

{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\Big(\pi_{x}\otimes_{E}((\chi_{x})_{\varpi_{L}}\circ\mathrm{det}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)})\otimes_{E}L_{S^{u}_{0}}({\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}),J_{{\mathbf{P}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{y}])\Big)\neq 0.

On the other hand, let
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
be the Bernstein paraboline varieties
[
6
, Section 4.2]
of type
(\Omega_{S_{0}},{\mathbf{h}})
. By
[
6
, Theorem 4.2.5, Corollary 4.2.5]
or
[
25
, Proposition 4.2]
, we see that the rigid space
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
is equidimensional of dimension
n^{2}+\left(\frac{n(n-1)}{2}+s\right)d_{L}
. Let
x=(\rho_{x},\underline{x},{\delta^{0}})\in{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
, then
D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})
admits an
\Omega_{S_{0}}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}=\{{\rm Fil}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})\}
such that, for all
1=1,\cdots,s
,

{\rm gr}_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{x})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}}\mathcal{R}_{k(x),L}((\delta_{i}^{0})^{-1}_{\varpi_{L}})\big[1/t\big]=\Delta_{x_{i}}\big[1/t\big].

The Bernstein paraboline variety
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
can be viewed as a local analogue of the patched Bernstein eigenvariety
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
. Consider the composition

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}

\displaystyle\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\iota_{\Omega_{S_{0}}}}{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\overline{r}}^{\Box}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}.

where
\iota_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}}:{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}
is an isomorphism given in the argument before
[
6
, (3.32)]
. By an easy variation of the proof of
[
6
, Theorem 3.3.9]
, we see that the composition in (
5.1
) factors through
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
, i.e.,

\Lambda:\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\iota_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}}^{-1}({X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}).

(5.2)

It induces an isomorphism between
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
with a union of some irreducible components of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}\times\iota_{\Omega_{S_{0}}}^{-1}({X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})})
equipped with the reduced closed rigid subspace structure.

5.2
Main results on local-global compatibility

This section follows the route of
[
18
, Section 4.1]
. We fix a Galois representation
\rho\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}
(and let
x
be the corresponding point in
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}=({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})
the corresponding maximal ideal of
R_{\infty}[1/p]
). We make the following Hypothesis.

Hypothesis 5.1
.

(Keep the notation in Section
2.2
and Definition
2.1
)

(a)

\rho_{L}:=\rho|_{{\rm Gal}_{F^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}}}}
is a potentially crystalline
p
-adic Galois representation with distinct Hodge-Tate weights
{\mathbf{h}}:=({{\mathbf{h}}}_{1}>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{2}>\cdots>{{\mathbf{h}}}_{n})
.

(b)

Let
{\mathbf{D}}:=D_{{\rm rig}}(\rho_{L})
be the associated
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
of rank
n
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
,
{\mathbf{D}}
admits a non-critical
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, which admits parameters
(\underline{x}^{u},\underline{\delta}^{u})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}}}
or
(\underline{x}^{u}_{+},\underline{\delta}^{0,u})\in{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
.

(c)

Put
y_{u}:=(\rho,\iota^{-1}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}}(\underline{x}^{u}_{+},\underline{\delta}^{0,u}))\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\infty}\times{\big(\mathrm{Spec}\hskip 2.0pt{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\Omega^{u}_{S_{0}}}\big)^{\mathrm{rig}}}\times{\mathcal{Z}_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}},\mathcal{O}_{L}}}
. Then
y_{u}\in\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
(and thus
(\rho_{L},\underline{x}^{u}_{+},\underline{\delta}^{0}_{w_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}})\in{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
).

By
[
25
, Proposition 4.10
\&
4.11]
, we have

Lemma 5.2
.

x_{u}
(resp.,
y_{u}
) is a smooth point of
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\mathfrak{p}},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}(\overline{\rho})
). Moreover,
y_{u}
does not admit companion points of non-dominant weight.

Remark 5.3
.

By the argument in the proof of
[
16
, Theorem 4.35]
, the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}
is locally free of rank
1
at all
y_{u}
.

Recall that the completion of
R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
at
\rho_{L}
is natural to
R^{\Box}_{\rho_{L}}\cong R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
, where
R^{\Box}_{\rho_{L}}
is the framed universal deformation ring of
\rho_{L}
over
{\rm Art}_{E}
. Let
R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:=R_{{\mathbf{D}}}\otimes_{R_{\rho_{L}}}R^{\Box}_{\rho_{L}}
. Put
R_{{\mathbf{D}},u}^{\Box,?}:=R_{{\mathbf{D}}}^{\Box}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}R^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
for
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
?\in\{\circ,0\}
. As
x_{w}
in non-critical, the completion of
{X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}
at
x_{w}
is naturally isomorphic to
R_{{\mathbf{D}},u}^{\Box,0}
(the same as
[
25
, Proposition 4.9]
). Keep the notation in Section
4.6
.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
be an ideal of
R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}
satisfies that
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
. Then the composition
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{a}}
is an isomorphism (i.e., the ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
deletes the information comes from framing). For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
?\in\{\emptyset,\circ,0\}
, we use
{\mathfrak{a}}
to denote its image in
R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
. Thus we get
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}}\oplus{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{0,u}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{A_{0,u}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},g}}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},g}}
, so that the natural maps

A^{?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{a}}
and
A_{0,u}\hookrightarrow R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}}\twoheadrightarrow R^{\Box,?}_{{\mathbf{D}},u,g}/{\mathfrak{a}}
are isomorphisms. We use
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
denote the preimage of
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}:=({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\subseteq{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}:=({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\subseteq R_{\infty}[1/p]
.

Our goal is to investigate
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
, which is equipped with a natural
R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{a}}\cong A_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action. Similar to
[
18
, Lemma 4.2]
, since
{\mathfrak{a}}+{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}}
and hence
{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}+{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}={\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
, we get that

\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]=\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}]
. Similar to
[
18
, Lemma 4.2 (2)]
, we can prove that

{\rm Hom}_{G}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]^{{\rm lalg}},\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}])\cong{\rm Hom}_{G}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]^{{\rm lalg}},\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}])\cong E.

Remark 5.4
.

It is conjectured that
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]^{{\rm lalg}}
is given by the modified classical local Langlands, i.e.
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]^{{\rm lalg}}\cong Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{0},\lambda)=\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. We refer to
[
16
, Proposition 4.33]
. If
\rho_{L}
is generic, this is true by
[
16
, Proposition 4.35]
and thus
\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
is irreducible.

Let
U_{u}=U_{{\mathfrak{p}},u}\times U^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{u}\subseteq\iota^{-1}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}}\big({X^{\Box}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},\mathbf{{h}}}(\overline{r})}\big)\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\overline{\rho}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}}^{\Box}
be a smooth affinoid neighourhood of
y_{u}
such
y_{u^{\prime}}\not\in U_{{\mathfrak{p}},u}
for
u^{\prime}\neq u
. Let
{\mathfrak{m}}_{y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}}
be the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}(U_{{\mathfrak{p}},u})
at
y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}\supset{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}}
be the closed ideal generated by the above ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}\subseteq R_{\overline{r}}^{\square}[1/p]
. In the sequel, we put

\mathcal{M}_{y_{u}}:=\big(\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}\big)_{y_{u}}=\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}/({\mathfrak{m}}_{y_{u,{\mathfrak{p}}}}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}}),\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}:=\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}\big)_{y_{u}}:=\mathcal{M}_{\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u},{\bm{\lambda}}_{\mathbf{h}}}^{\infty}/({\mathfrak{a}}+{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})

for simplicity. There are natural
{\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)\times R_{\infty}
-equivariant injections:

\mathcal{M}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow J_{{\mathbf{P}}^{u}_{S_{0}}}(\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}})[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}].

Since
\mathcal{M}_{\infty}
is locally free of rank
1
at
y_{u}
, we obtain that
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\cong R^{\Box,0}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}
, so that
\dim_{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}=1+(s+sd_{L})-|I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})|
. In this case, similar to the argument around
[
18
, (4.5)]
, we have a
{\mathbf{Z}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)\times A^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}_{y_{u}}\cong\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
(note that the
A^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
-action factors through
A^{0}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}\twoheadrightarrow A^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}
). Similar to the proof of
[
25
, Lemma 4.19]
, by using the non-critical assumption of
y_{u}
, the maps
\mathcal{M}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{u}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
are balanced, hence induces a
G\times R_{\infty}
-equivariant injection:

\displaystyle\iota_{u}:I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}].\;

(5.3)

where the
R_{\infty}
-action on
I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
is induced by
R_{\infty}\rightarrow(R^{\Box,0}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes_{E}(R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty}[1/p]/{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{{\mathbf{D}},u}^{0}\twoheadrightarrow(R^{\Box,\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}},u}/{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes_{E}(R^{{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\infty}[1/p]/{\mathfrak{m}}^{{\mathfrak{p}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{{\mathbf{D}},u}^{\circ}
. Moreover, we have an injection of locally analytic representations
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
.

Let
\widetilde{\pi}
be the subrepresentation of
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
generated by
\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u})
for all
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Note that
\widetilde{\pi}
has an
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}
-action via
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R^{\Box}_{{\mathbf{D}}}/{\mathfrak{a}}\xrightarrow{\sim}R_{\infty}[1/p]/{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}
. Similar to
[
18
, Lemma 4.4]
, we have

Proposition 5.5
.

\iota_{u}(\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))=(\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u}))[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
.

Proof.

The composition
\mathrm{PS}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}]
sends
\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
to
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
. Since
{\rm Hom}_{G}(\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}])\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Hom}_{G}(\mathrm{PS}^{{\rm lalg}}_{u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}])
, then the same strategy as in the first paragraph of the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 4.6]
shows that the previous composition has image in
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
. Using the same strategy as in
[
18
, Lemma 4.6]
, the injection
\iota_{u}(\mathrm{PS}_{u,1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}))\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{u}))[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]
is surjective too.
∎

Theorem 5.6
.

Assume that
\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}]^{{\rm lalg}}\cong Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,I_{0},\lambda)=\pi_{1}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
. Then we have an
A_{{\mathbf{D}}}\times G
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{\pi}\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
. In particular, we have a
{\rm GL}_{n}(L)
-equivariant injection

\pi_{\min}({\mathbf{D}})\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}]\cong\widetilde{\pi}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}]\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{a}}_{x}][{\mathfrak{m}}_{A_{{\mathbf{D}}}}]\cong\Pi_{\infty}^{R_{\infty}-{\rm an}}[{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}].

Proof.

Similar to the argument in the proof
[
18
, Theorem 4.5,  Corollary 4.6]
, we can also see that
\widetilde{\pi}
is a subrepresentation of
\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
and contains all
I^{G}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S^{u}_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm sm}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S^{u}_{0}}\otimes_{E}\widetilde{\delta}_{u}^{{\rm univ},-}
so that
\widetilde{\pi}\cong\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})^{{\rm univ},-}
.
∎

6
Appendix

6.1
I: (Parabolic) higher
\mathscr{L}
-invariants and Colmez-Greenberg-Stevens formula

Keep the notation in Section
3.4
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\delta:={\rm unr}(q_{L}^{-1})z^{{\mathbf{k}}}
for some
{\mathbf{k}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{\Sigma_{L}}=\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}
. The cup product induces a perfect pairing:

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta))\times{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\xrightarrow{\cup}{\mathrm{H}}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta)).

The local class field theory gives a natural isomorphism
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L})\cong{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
. Let
b_{\sigma}
(resp.,
b_{g}
) be the dual basis of
\log_{p,\sigma}
(resp.,
\val_{L}
) via such pairing. We have the following facts:

(1)

\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{e}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta))=d_{L}
,
\dim_{E}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{g}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta))=1+d_{L}
and
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{e}(\mathcal{R}_{E,L}(\delta))^{\perp}={\rm Hom}_{{\rm sm}}(L^{\times},E)=E\val_{L}
.

(2)

For any vector
x=\sum_{J\subseteq\Sigma_{L}}a_{\sigma}b_{\sigma}
with
a_{\sigma}\neq 0
, we have
\dim_{E}E[x]^{\perp}=d_{L}
and

E[x]^{\perp}=E\langle\val_{L},\{\log_{p,\tau}\}_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash J},\{a_{\sigma^{\prime}}\log_{p,\sigma}-a_{\sigma}\log_{p,\sigma^{\prime}}\}_{\sigma^{\prime},\sigma\in J}\rangle.

Moreover,
E[x]^{\perp}\cap{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
equal to
E\val_{L}
(resp.,
{\rm Hom}_{\sigma}(L^{\times},E)
) if
\sigma\in J
(resp.,
\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}\backslash J
).

Proposition 6.2
.

For
(\widetilde{M},\psi)\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(M,M)\times{\rm Hom}(L^{\times},E)
, there exists a deformation
\widetilde{D}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
such that
\widetilde{D}
sits in an exact sequence of
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules over
\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}
:

0\rightarrow\widetilde{M}\rightarrow\widetilde{D}\rightarrow E_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon)\rightarrow 0

iff

\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}(\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1-\psi\epsilon))\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
(recall the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
after (
3.48
)).

Proof.

Replacing
\widetilde{D}
and
\widetilde{M}
by
\widetilde{D}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}(\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon))
and
\widetilde{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}}(\mathcal{R}_{E[\epsilon]/\epsilon^{2}}(1+\psi\epsilon))
respectively, we can assume
\psi=0
. Now consider the exact sequence
0\rightarrow M\rightarrow\widetilde{M}\rightarrow M\rightarrow 0
. We get a long exact sequence:

0\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{s},M)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{s},\widetilde{M})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{s},M)\xrightarrow{c}{\rm Ext}^{2}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{s},M),

where the map
c
equals to (up to nonzero scalars) to
\langle\cdot,[\widetilde{M}]\rangle
. So we have
\langle[{\mathbf{D}}],[\widetilde{M}]\rangle=0
(i.e.,
\widetilde{M}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{FM}}({\mathbf{D}}:M)
) iff
[{\mathbf{D}}]\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{(\varphi,\Gamma)}(E_{s},M)
lies in the image of
\mathrm{pr}
iff a deformation
\widetilde{D}
of
{\mathbf{D}}
as in the statement exists. The result follows.
∎

.
