Remark 6.4
.

Observe the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},N^{\prime}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(N^{\prime}_{2},E_{u(s)}(\epsilon))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},E_{u(s)}(\epsilon))}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},N^{\prime}_{2})}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{1}(N^{\prime}_{2},E_{u^{-1}(q)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cup}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{1}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},E_{u^{-1}(q)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

(6.6)

where
\eta
and
\eta^{\prime}
are also induced by the map
E_{u^{-1}(q)}\rightarrow E_{u^{-1}(s)}(\epsilon)
. The top cup-product is perfect by the Tate-duality. On the other hand, by
[
11
, Lemma 5.1.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\rm{\displaystyle Hom}((N^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{u^{-1}(q)},(N^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{u^{-1}(s)}(\epsilon))

(6.7)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}((N^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{u(s)}(\epsilon))/W_{{\rm dR}}^{+}((N^{\prime}_{2})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{R}_{E,L}}E_{u^{-1}(q)}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{H}}^{0}({\rm Gal}_{L},\oplus_{\tau\in\Sigma_{L}}\oplus_{1\leq i\leq r_{s}}\oplus_{t_{q-1}+1\leq j\leq t_{s-1}}t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{s-1}+i+1,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR},\tau}^{+}/t^{{\mathbf{h}}_{t_{q-1}+i,\tau}-{\mathbf{h}}_{j,\tau}}B_{{\rm dR},\tau}^{+}).\;

This implies
\dim_{E}\ker\eta_{1}=|\{(\tau,i,j):t_{q-1}+i<j\}|=\sum_{j=0}^{r_{q}-1}(\rk N_{3}^{\prime}+j)=r_{q}\rk N_{3}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}r_{q}(r_{q}-1)
and
\ker(\eta_{1})\subseteq{\rm Ext}^{1}(E_{u^{-1}(s)},N^{\prime}_{2})^{\perp}
. In particular,
\ker(\eta_{1})\subseteq l_{\mathrm{FM}}(D^{\prime\prime}:N_{2}^{\prime})
.

6.2
II: Infinite-fern for non-critical potentially crystalline case

We prove the inifinite fern for non-critical (not necessary generic) potentially crystalline
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules.

Theorem 6.5
.

The natural map
g_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is surjective.

Proof.

By commutative diagram (
3.30
), it suffices to show the following assertion for Lie-algebras (where
g_{L}=(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{L}}
in Remark
3.18
):

\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})=\mathrm{Im}(\nu).

We first assume that
{\mathbf{D}}
is generic, we just need to show that

\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u,\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma}),\;\text{i.e,\;}\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})

where we note that
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma,u})=\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
and
\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
. We follows the same route as in
[
28
, Section, Lemma 2.1]
. This identity to discuss reads
\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
. Note that
\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}}(\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}^{-1}u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}))
. Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}^{-1}u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma}).

Let
e^{i,j}
be the elementary
(n\times n)
-matrix whose
(l,m)
-entry is given by
\delta_{i,l}\delta_{j,m}
. For any
i>j
, we claim that there is some permutation

u_{i,j}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
and
i-j
scalars
\{x_{i,l}\}_{j+1\leq l\leq i}
in
E
such that the matrix
a^{i,j}:=e^{i,j}+\sum_{l=j+1}^{i}x_{i,l}e^{i,l}
lies in
\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}^{-1}u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})
. Rewrite the fixed basis
\{e_{1,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{1}},\cdots,\{e_{s,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{s}}
of
D_{\sigma}
. Rewrite this basis by
e_{1},\cdots,e_{n}
. Recall that this basis gives a
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
-parabolic filtration

V_{\bullet}=(0\subseteq V_{0}\subset V_{1}\subset\cdots\subset V_{s}=E^{n})

of
D_{\sigma}\cong E^{n}
, where
V_{i}=\langle\{e_{1,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{1}},\cdots,\{e_{s,j,\sigma}\}_{1\leq j\leq r_{i}}\rangle
. Let
s_{i,j}=(i,j)\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. Suppose that
t_{j^{\prime}}+1\leq j\leq t_{j^{\prime}+1}
and
t_{i^{\prime}}+1\leq j\leq t_{i^{\prime}+1}
for
\leq j^{\prime}\leq i^{\prime}\leq s
. Consider the following basis

\mathcal{B}=\{b^{-1}_{\sigma}(e_{1}),\cdots,b^{-1}_{\sigma}(e_{j-1}),e_{j},b^{-1}_{\sigma}(e_{j+1}),\cdots,b^{-1}_{\sigma}(e_{i}),e_{i+1},\cdots,e_{n}\},

of
E^{n}
(this is also a basis since
b^{-1}_{\sigma}
is upper triangular). Define
\pi(x)=0
for
x\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\{e_{j}\}
and
\pi(e_{j})=e_{i}
. We also put
V^{\prime}_{l}=\langle e_{1},\cdots,e_{l}\rangle
for
1\leq l\leq n
. Note that

b_{\sigma}\pi b^{-1}_{\sigma}(s_{i,j}V_{l})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&1\leq l\leq i-1,\\
E\langle b_{\sigma}e_{i}\rangle,&i\leq l\leq n.\end{array}\right.

Since
\langle b_{\sigma}e_{i}\rangle\subseteq V^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq s_{i,j}V_{i^{\prime}}
and
V_{i}\subseteq V_{i^{\prime}}
, we find a morphism
\pi:E^{n}\rightarrow E^{n}
such that the endomorphism
b_{\sigma}\pi b^{-1}_{\sigma}
stabilizes the
{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{0}}
-parabolic filtration
s_{i,j}V_{\bullet}
. Then the desired
a^{i,j}
is the matrix in the standard basis. Similar to the last paragraph in
[
28
, Lemma 2.1]
, such
a^{i,j}
also form a basis of the
\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
which lie in the envelope
\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{b_{\sigma}^{-1}u}({\mathfrak{p}}_{S_{0},\sigma})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{w_{0,\sigma}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{\sigma})
.

It remains to treat the general case. By Lemma
3.19
, we have

\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})=(\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},L})+\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{p}}^{0}_{S_{0},L}))\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})\supseteq\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},L})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L}).

On the other hand, we have

\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},L})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})=\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Ad}_{u}({\mathfrak{z}}_{S_{0},L})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})

by choosing
u
with the longest element in
\mathscr{W}_{I_{0}^{\prime}({\mathbf{D}})}\subseteq\mathscr{W}_{s}
. This show that
\sum_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{Im}(\nu_{u})\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})
contains
\mathrm{Im}(\nu)\cap\mathrm{Ad}_{g_{L}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{L})=\mathrm{Im}(\nu)
. We complete the proof.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

The same strategy shows that the natural map
g_{{\mathbf{D}},\sigma}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{\sigma,u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{{\rm Ext}}_{\sigma}^{1}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})
is surjective.

6.3
III: Further preliminaries on smooth (parabolic) Steinberg representations

This appendix aims to generalizes the results in
[
12
, Section 2]
to the smooth (parabolic) generalizes Steinberg representations.

Fix an irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
over
E
. For
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we put
\pi(l):=\pi\otimes_{E}|\det_{{\rm GL}_{r}(L)}|^{l}
. Recall in
[
35
]
, a
Bernstein-Zelevinsky segment
is a non-empty set of irreducible cuspidal representations of
{\rm GL}_{r}(L)
of the form
\Delta_{k}(\pi)=[\pi(k-1),\pi(k-2),\cdots,\pi]
for some integers
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
m=kr
and
S_{r}=\Delta_{m}\backslash\{r,2r,\cdots,(k-1)r\}
(in the sequel, we identity
\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}=\{r,2r,\cdots,(k-1)r\}
with
\{1,2,\cdots,(k-1)\}=\Delta_{k}
). Then we get the standard Levi and parabolic subgroups
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{r}}(L)\cong\prod_{i=1}^{k}{\rm GL}_{r}(L)\subseteq{\mathbf{P}}_{S_{r}}(L)\subseteq{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
. For the Bernstein-Zelevinsky segment
\Delta_{k}(\pi)
, we associate an irreducible cuspidal smooth representation of
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{r}}(L)
over
E
:

\pi_{S_{r}}:=(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi(k-i))\eta_{S_{r}}.\;

(6.8)

As in
[
25
, Definition 3.7]
, for
S_{r}\subseteq I\subseteq\Delta_{m}
, we consider the principal series:

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},I}(\pi):=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{r}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{S_{r}}\right)^{\infty},\;\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I,\Delta_{m}}(\pi):=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{r}}(L)}{\rm soc}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\mathrm{PS}_{S_{r},I}(\pi)\right)^{\infty}.\;

(6.9)

For
J\subseteq\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}
, put

\displaystyle v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)=\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{J\cup S_{r},\Delta_{m}}(\pi)\big/\sum_{I\supsetneq J}\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{I\cup S_{r},\Delta_{m}}(\pi).

We call
\{v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)\}_{J\subseteq\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}
the smooth generalized parabolic Steinberg representations of
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
associated to the Zelevinsky-segment
\Delta_{k}(\pi)
.

For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{k}
, we put

\displaystyle u(\pi_{S_{r}}):=(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{k}\pi(u(k-i)))\eta_{S_{r}}.

(6.10)

It is easy to see that
u(\pi_{S_{r}})\neq\pi_{S_{r}}
for any
1\neq u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
. For
S_{r}\subseteq I\subseteq\Delta_{m}
, put

\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},I,u}(\pi):=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}_{{\overline{{\mathbf{P}}}}_{S_{r}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}u(\pi_{S_{r}})\right)^{\infty}.

By
[
25
, Proposition 3.8]
,
\{v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)\}_{J\subseteq\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}
are precisely the Jordan-Hölder factors of the smooth representation
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u}(\pi)
for any
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
.

Similar to
[
12
, Definition 2.1.4]
and the above discussion, we say a cuspidal representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{r}}(L)
is
G
-regular if
u(\pi^{\prime}):=(\pi^{\prime}\eta_{S_{r}})^{u}\eta_{S_{r}}\neq\pi^{\prime}
for any
1\neq u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
(where
(-)^{u}
means the usual
u
-conjugate of the representation). Therefore, for any
S_{r}\subseteq I\subseteq\Delta_{m}
, and an irreducible smooth representation
\pi^{\infty}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)
,
\pi^{\infty}
is called
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-regular if
J_{I,S_{r}}(\pi^{\infty})
is semi-simple and contains a
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-regular elements. In general,
\pi^{\infty}
is called
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-basic if there exists
I_{1}\subseteq I
and an irreducible admissible
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-regular representation
\pi_{1}^{\infty}
of
{\mathbf{L}}_{I_{1}}(L)
such that
\pi^{\infty}=\left({\rm Ind}^{{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}_{{\mathbf{P}}_{I_{1}}(L)\cap{\mathbf{L}}_{I}(L)}\pi_{1}^{\infty}\right)^{\infty}
.

By
[
25
, Lemma 3.4]
, we have isomorphism of smooth
{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{r}}(L)
-representations (as
[
12
, Lemma 2.1.14]
):

J_{\Delta_{m},S_{r}}(\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m}}(\pi))\cong\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}u(\pi_{S_{r}}).

By
[
25
, Lemma 6.4]
, we obtain that (as in
[
12
, Corollary 2.1.24,(i),(ii)]
)

J_{\Delta_{m},S_{r}}(v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi))=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}\\
\Delta_{k}\backslash D_{R}(u)=J\end{subarray}}u(\pi_{S_{r}}),

The set
\{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}:\Delta_{k}\backslash D_{R}(u)=J\}
equals the set of
u
such that
{\rm Hom}_{{\rm GL}_{m}(L)}(v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi),\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta,u}(\pi))\neq 0
. Moreover, the representation
\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u}(\pi)
is multiplicity free with socle
v_{\Delta_{k}\backslash D_{R}(u),\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
cosocle
v_{D_{R}(u),\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
.

Such results are proved by using the orientations on the following graph
\Gamma_{\pi}
(
[
35
, Section 2, Theorem 2.2]
). The graph
\Gamma_{\pi}
consisting of the vertices
\{\pi(k-i)\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
, the edges
\Big\{\overset{\pi(k-i)}{\bullet}-\overset{\pi(k-i-1)}{\bullet}\Big\}_{1\leq i\leq k}
, i.e.,

\Gamma_{\pi}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.28435pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.28435pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overset{\pi(k)}{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.28435pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overset{\pi(k-1)}{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 101.97795pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 139.47795pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overset{\pi(0)}{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

(6.11)

An orientation of
\Gamma_{\pi}
is given by choosing a direction on each edge. Thus all the argument in
[
12
, Theorem 2.1.20, Theorem 2.1.23]
can be applied to above new graph
\Gamma_{\pi}
. In particular, the same results as in
[
12
, Corollary 2.1.24 (iii)]
also holds (for
I=\Delta_{m}
,
w\in\mathscr{W}_{k}
and
S_{r}\subseteq I_{1},I_{2}\subseteq\Delta_{m}
).

For any
u,u^{\prime}\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, we have canonical isomorphism

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm GL}_{m}(L)}(\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u}(\pi),\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u^{\prime}}(\pi))\cong{\rm Hom}_{{\mathbf{L}}_{S_{r}}(L)}(\oplus_{v\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}vu(\pi_{S_{r}}),u^{\prime}(\pi_{S_{r}}))

of
1
-dimensional
E
-vector spaces. Therefore, there exists a unique (up to scalars) non-zero intertwining map
i_{u,u^{\prime}}:\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u}(\pi)\rightarrow\mathrm{PS}^{\infty}_{S_{r},\Delta_{m},u^{\prime}}(\pi)
. Similar to the argument in
[
12
, Remark 2.1.16 (iv)]
, any
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-basic has the simple socle and cosocle.

The following lemma is an analogue of
[
12
, Lemma 2.2.1]
.

Lemma 6.7
.

For
J_{0},J_{1}\subseteq\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}
, there exists a unique
{\rm GL}_{m}(L)
-basic representation
Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},J_{1})
with socle
v_{J_{0},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and cosocle
v_{J_{1},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and it has set of Jordan-Holder factors
v_{J_{0},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and cosocle
v_{J_{1},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
and it has set of Jordan-Holder factors
\{v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi):J\in[J_{0},J_{1}]\}
, where
[J_{0},J_{1}]=\{I\subseteq\Delta_{k}|J_{0}\cap J_{1}\subseteq J\subseteq J_{0}\cup J_{1}\}
.

Proof.

Choose
u_{0}
(resp.,
u_{1}
) such that
\Delta_{k}\backslash D_{R}(u_{0})=J_{0}
(resp.,
D_{R}(u_{1})=J_{1}
), the desired representation
Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},J_{1})
is the image of the unique (up to scalar) non-zero map
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{r},\Delta,u_{1}}(\pi)\rightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{S_{r},\Delta,u_{0}}(\pi)
. Note that
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{r},\Delta,u_{0}}(\pi)=Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},\Delta\backslash J_{0})
and
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{r},\Delta,u_{1}}(\pi)=Q_{\Delta_{m}}(\Delta\backslash J_{1},J_{1})
. Then
Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},J_{1})
is the unique subrepresentation of
Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},\Delta\backslash J_{0})
with cosocle
v_{J_{1},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
. Using the partial order
\leq_{u_{0}}
in
[
12
, Corollary 2.1.24 (iii)]
,
v_{J,\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)
is an irreducible consistent in
Q_{\Delta_{m}}(J_{0},J_{1})
iff
v_{J_{0},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)\leq_{u_{0}}v_{J_{1},\Delta_{m}\backslash S_{r}}^{\infty}(\pi)\leq_{u_{0}}
iff
J\in[J_{0},J_{1}]
.
∎

6.4
IV: Supplements for locally analytic representations

In this section, we assume that
L={\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
and
\max_{1\leq i\leq s}r_{i}\leq 2
. For
u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}
, recall the
\Omega_{S_{0}}^{u}
-filtration
\mathcal{F}_{u}
:
{\mathbf{D}}:=E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(1)}-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(2)}-\cdots-E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(s)}
. For each
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}
, we associate a locally analytic representation
\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})
that determines
E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(s)}
uniquely. More precisely, if
r_{i}=1
, we put
\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})=\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})\otimes_{E}L_{S_{0}^{u}}(\lambda)|_{{\mathbf{L}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L),i}=\pi_{\mathrm{sm}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})\otimes_{E}z^{t^{u}_{i}}
. If
r_{i}=2
, then
E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}
is uniquely determined by its underlying Deligne-Fontaine module
{\mathrm{DF}}_{x_{u^{-1}(i)}}
and an
E
-line
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{u}
in
D_{{\rm dR}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})
, then
\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}):=\pi_{{\rm an}}(\mathcal{L}_{i}^{u})
by
[
9
, The main theorem]
. Consider the locally
{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
-analytic parabolic induction

\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}):=\left({\rm Ind}^{G}_{{\mathbf{P}}^{u}_{S_{0}}(L)}\pi_{{\rm an}}(\underline{x}^{u})\eta_{S_{0}^{u}}\right)^{{\rm an}},\;\pi_{{\rm an}}(\underline{x}^{u}):=\boxtimes_{j=1}^{s}\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(j)}).\;

Let
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
be the unique maximal quotient of
\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
with socle
Q_{\Delta}^{\diamond}(\emptyset,\lambda)
. By definition, we have
\mathrm{PS}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{PS}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
and
\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
.

We can also take certain amalgamated sum of
\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
when
u
are taken over
\mathscr{W}_{s}
. There exists a locally analytic representation
\pi_{1}^{+}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
which lies in the following exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\pi_{1}^{+}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\rightarrow\Big(\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\pi^{{\rm lalg}}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\oplus\Big(\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\mathrm{ST}_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})/\mathrm{ST}_{S_{0},u}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\Big)\rightarrow 0.\;

(6.12)

We can extend the previous map
\gamma^{\circ}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1,\circ}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)
in (
4.36
) to

\displaystyle\gamma^{+}_{{\mathbf{D}}}:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\overline{{\rm Ext}}^{1}_{u}({\mathbf{D}},{\mathbf{D}})\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\kappa_{u}}\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\prod_{i=1}^{s}{\rm Ext}^{1}\big(E_{u^{-1}(i)}^{\prime},E_{u^{-1}(i)}^{\prime}\big)

(6.13)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\bigoplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{{\rm GL}_{r_{u^{-1}(i)}}({\mathbf{Q}}_{p})}\big(\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)}),\pi_{{\rm an}}(E^{\prime}_{u^{-1}(i)})\big)\xrightarrow{\oplus_{u\in\mathscr{W}_{s}}\zeta_{u}}{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{+}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right).\;

It is worth to construct a map (such map is suggested by
[
8
, (6)]
for general de Rham
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-module
{\mathbf{D}}
over
\mathcal{R}_{E,L}
)

t^{+}_{D}:{\rm Ext}^{1}_{G}\left(\pi_{\natural}^{{\rm lalg}}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}}),\pi^{+}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})\right)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{\varphi^{f}}(\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}},\mathcal{M}_{{\mathbf{D}}})\oplus{\rm Hom}_{{\rm Fil}}(D,D).

and explore its kernel and image (suggested by the following example,
t^{+}_{D}
is not a surjection in general), and then describe the information of Hodge parameters its determines.

A typical example is
n=4
,
S_{0}=\{1,3\}
and
{\mathbf{D}}=[E_{1}-E_{2}]=[E_{2}^{\prime}-E_{1}^{\prime}]
. Let
e_{1},e_{2}
(resp.,
e_{3},e_{4}
) be a basis of
D_{{\rm dR}}(E_{1})
(resp.,
D_{{\rm dR}}(E_{2})
). We list the Hodge filtration
{\rm Fil}_{\bullet}^{H}D
of
D
under the basis
e_{1},e_{2},e_{3},e_{4}
(which is determined by the
p
-adic Hodge parameters
\mathcal{L}_{12},\;\mathcal{L}_{23},\;\mathcal{L}_{13},\;\mathcal{L}_{34}
and
\mathcal{L}_{14}
):

{\rm Fil}_{i}^{H}(D)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D,&i\leq-h_{1}\\
E(e_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{12}e_{1})\oplus{\rm Fil}^{H}_{-h_{3}}(D),&-h_{1}<i\leq-h_{2}\\
E(e_{3}+\mathcal{L}_{23}e_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{13}e_{1})\oplus{\rm Fil}^{H}_{-h_{4}}(D),&-h_{2}<i\leq-h_{3}\\
E(e_{4}+\mathcal{L}_{34}e_{3}+e_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{14}e_{1}),&-h_{3}<i\leq-h_{4},\\
0,&i>-h_{4}.\end{array}\right.

Thus the Hodge parameter
\mathcal{L}_{12}
(resp.,
\mathcal{L}_{34}
) is contained in the block
E_{1}
(resp.,
E_{2}
) and
\mathcal{L}_{14},\mathcal{L}_{23},\mathcal{L}_{13}
are the so-called higher
\mathcal{L}
-invariants. Secondly, under the basis
e_{3},e_{4},e_{1},e_{2}
, the Hodge filtration can be rewritten by

{\rm Fil}_{i}^{H}(D)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}D,&i\leq-h_{1}\\
E(e_{4}-(\frac{(1-\mathcal{L}_{23}\mathcal{L}_{34})(\mathcal{L}_{12}-\mathcal{L}_{14})+\mathcal{L}_{13}-\mathcal{L}_{14}\mathcal{L}_{23}}{\mathcal{L}_{23}\mathcal{L}_{12}-\mathcal{L}_{13}})e_{3})\oplus{\rm Fil}^{H}_{-h_{3}}(D),&-h_{1}<i\leq-h_{2}\\
E(e_{1}-\frac{\mathcal{L}_{23}}{\mathcal{L}_{13}-\mathcal{L}_{14}\mathcal{L}_{23}}e_{4}+\frac{1-\mathcal{L}_{23}\mathcal{L}_{34}}{\mathcal{L}_{13}-\mathcal{L}_{14}\mathcal{L}_{23}}e_{3})\oplus{\rm Fil}^{H}_{-h_{4}}(D),&-h_{2}<i\leq-h_{3}\\
E(e_{2}+\mathcal{L}_{14}e_{1}+e_{4}+\mathcal{L}_{34}e_{3}),&-h_{3}<i\leq-h_{4},\\
0,&i>-h_{4}.\end{array}\right.

Therefore, in
E_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.,
E_{2}^{\prime}
), we see the higher parameter
\mathcal{L}_{14}
(resp., a relationship between higher parameters
\mathcal{L}_{14}
,
\mathcal{L}_{23}
and
\mathcal{L}_{13}
). In this case,
\pi^{+}_{1}(\underline{x},{\mathbf{h}})
already determines
{\mathbf{D}}
except another relationship between higher parameters
\mathcal{L}_{14}
,
\mathcal{L}_{23}
and
\mathcal{L}_{13}
, the author suspects that the remainder one relationship is related to the unknown so-called supersingular consistents.
